- [x] +tests +docs Z-0001 The comment gate accepts source comments that name `doc/tasks`, `doc/design` and `.tm.md` files
    > **Created:** 2026-11-03
    > **Completed:** 2026-11-03 git:a5a8f1a

    - **Why:** rule 2b of `scripts/check_comment_noise.sh` fails a source comment naming `doc/tasks`, `doc/design` or any `.tm.md` file as a private document. Those paths are now part of the tree, so the first comment that cites a task turns `make test` red.
    - **Do:** remove `doc/tasks`, `doc/design` and `.tm.md` from rule 2b; the rule that a named path must exist in the tree still applies to them. Keep the directories the tree still does not carry (`doc/performance`, `doc/release`, `doc/archive`, `doc/stage0-C`). Update the self-tests in `scripts/test_check_comment_noise.sh` that assert the old behaviour (items 15-21); this changes what those tests expect, so say so in the commit message. Also update the comments elsewhere in `scripts/` that call these paths private (`check_md_tables.sh`, `check_fuzz_fresh.sh`, `test.sh`) where they now say something untrue.
    - **Verification:** a scratch comment naming `doc/tasks/active.tm.md` passes the gate, one naming a missing file still fails; `make test`.
    - **Result:** rule 2b now lists only `doc/performance`, `doc/release`, `doc/archive` and `doc/stage0-C`; the `.tm.md` loop is gone, so `doc/tasks`, `doc/design` and `.tm.md` paths are held to the existence rule (2a). A bare directory (`doc/tasks`) or bare file name (`future.tm.md`) is not path-shaped and passes, like `doc/spec` or `README.md`. Self-test items 15-21 changed their expectations and the suite went from 21 to 25 cases; against the old gate four of the updated cases failed. A scratch tree confirmed `doc/tasks/active.tm.md` passes and `doc/tasks/missing.tm.md` fails. The comments in `check_md_tables.sh`, `check_fuzz_fresh.sh` and `test.sh` were read: none calls these paths private, so none changed.
    - **Found on the way:** `make test` was red on the unchanged tree because `doc/tasks/future.tm.md:13` said "a leak, which is memory-safe", a banned safety phrase (`bash scripts/check_safety_model.sh`). Reworded to "does not break memory safety" in commit `22280d7`, meaning unchanged.
    - **Checks:** `make test`: Total: 3163 run, 3163 passed, 0 failed, 37 skipped. Scripts only, so `make preflight-changed` was not required.

- [x] +fuzz +decision Z-0003 Decide when to renew the fuzz record, now that `rt/` changed
    > **Created:** 2026-11-03 **Owner:** the maintainer (a fuzz session takes the whole machine for hours).
    > **Completed:** 2026-11-03 git:986092a

    - **State:** `scripts/check_fuzz_fresh.sh` reports the record stale for `rt/` since `81e0340`. The pages that quote the record (`README.md`, `doc/SAFETY.md` §5, `doc/STABILITY.md`) describe the launched code and stay true while they say so. Z-0007 to Z-0012 will change `src/` several times.
    - **Options:** a short session now; or one after the hardening series (Z-0012), with the pages saying which code the current record describes until then. Record the decision here.
    - **Decision (2026-11-03, a delegated judgment for the maintainer to review):** renew the record once, after Z-0012, not now.
    - **Reasons:** (1) Z-0007 to Z-0012 each change `src/`, so a session run now would be stale again before anything cites it. (2) The pages are accurate as they stand: they quote the figure for the launched code (`bench/fuzz/README.md`, "The short session on the launched code"), and `scripts/check_fuzz_fresh.sh` reports the drift (`STALE ... rt`) without failing outside `--strict`. (3) Nothing is lost by waiting: the release recording step runs the same check with `--strict` and refuses while the hashes differ, so a release cannot cite the stale record. (4) A session takes the whole machine for hours; one run after the series costs less than one after each task.
    - **Conditions:** no page edit is needed now. Z-0012's completion note starts the renewal. If a release is wanted before Z-0012, a short session runs first, because the strict check requires it. Run Z-0013's new generator features in the same session.
    - **Result:** decided to renew after Z-0012. Nothing in the tree changes; the staleness stays visible through `scripts/check_fuzz_fresh.sh` and is enforced at release by its `--strict` mode. No check ran beyond `tm validate`; the maintainer should confirm or overrule the timing.
    - **Confirmed (2026-11-03):** the maintainer confirms renewing the record after Z-0012.

- [x] +safety +tests Z-0007 B0 A write into a field, element or tuple slot of a local keeps the borrow it stores
    > **Created:** 2026-11-03 **Check-in:** stop for review after it. **Closes:** `gap_34_field_assigned_slice_reset`, `gap_35_inner_block_arena_field_assigned_ref`.
    > **Completed:** 2026-11-03 git:1a02a61

    - **Rule:** `check_assign` (`src/check_zir.zrl`) records the right-hand side's borrows on the base local for any write not through a dereference: `x = ..`, `x.f = ..`, `x.0 = ..` and `x[i] = ..` alike. A projected write adds to what `x` already holds; it does not end `x`'s other borrows. Today `inherit_rv_borrows` and `propagate_borrow` run only for whole-local assignments, while `end_rv_temp_borrows` always runs.
    - **Probes:** the two gap probes flip to `compile_reject`; add probes for an array slot, a nested field and an `Option` field, and an accepted twin (the same field reassigned and read before any invalidation). Record whether the change also rejects a store or return that escapes through a field write (that is Z-0009's).
    - **Verification:** `make test`, `make preflight-changed`, the rejection differential; the fuzz record goes stale for `src/`.
    - **Fixed (2026-11-03, `1a02a61`, `5d63295`, `0e43f10`):** `check_assign` now runs `inherit_rv_borrows` and `propagate_borrow` on the base local for a write that crosses no pointer, box or slice view; the new borrows are not `direct`, and nothing `x` already holds ends. The Ref and RefMut arms already recorded a borrow for a projected write; the call, struct-literal, tuple, enum-variant and plain-copy forms did not.
    - **Lead ruled out:** testing `place_has_deref` alone. A field write through a reference (`self.f = ..`) has no explicit `PROJ_DEREF` (the pointer layer is implicit, as `place_last_deref_is_readonly` reads it), so that test rejected 159 sites in `src/` and failed the build. `place_writes_through_pointer` takes the same type steps and skips a path through a pointer, box or slice.
    - **Fallout in the tree:** two sites, annotated `#[borrows()]` like the nine functions already marked so, because both return arena copies: `parse_name_list` (`src/parser/decl.zrl`, stored with `attr.borrows = ..`) and `default_output_path` (`src/main.zrl`, stored with `o.output_path = ..`). `tools/`, `stdlib/` and the tests needed nothing. `src/` builds under the previous compiler and under the new one.
    - **Probes:** `gap_34` and `gap_35` are `compile_reject` now (their Known gaps entries are gone; 14 `known_gap` probes remain, and class (A) in `doc/SAFETY.md` §6 is deleted with the others re-lettered, its example replaced by `gap_07`). New: `410` array slot, `411` tuple slot, `412` nested field, `413` `Option` field (all `compile_reject`) and `414` the accepted twin (the field is read and reassigned before the reset). The base compiler accepted all seven.
    - **Escape through a field write (Z-0009's):** no new route. Returning a local struct whose field holds a frame view is rejected by the base compiler too (a different message now), and `out.s = v.as_slice()` through a `&mut` parameter is rejected identically by both and is untouched, because it writes through a pointer.
    - **Rejection differential** (`check_reject_diff.sh --change HEAD HEAD~1`, both sides built from source): 0 newly accepted; 1164 newly refused of 29522 programs: the 6 probes above (front end and zbuild) and 1152 store-grid cells, all unsafe cells (384 each of `struct_field`, `tuple_slot`, `array_slot` destinations) and no safe twin. `bench/fuzz/store_grid_expected_failures.txt` lost exactly those 1152 lines (8300 to 7148); the twin-rejection count is unchanged (398).
    - **Tool gap found:** `check_reject_diff.sh` and `make preflight-changed` seed the base build with the working `build/zoril`, which cannot compile a base source that breaks a rule the change added, so a stricter rule fails the base build ("building HEAD failed"). I ran both with the previous compiler as the seed (a copy of `build/zoril` from before the change). The scripts should seed the base build with a compiler built from the base.
    - **Checks:** `make test`: Total: 3177 run, 3177 passed, 0 failed, 33 skipped. `make preflight-changed` (with `@{upstream}` set to the parent branch so it targets these commits): exit 0, Total: 1151 run, 1151 passed, 0 failed, fixpoint PASS, reject-diff PASS as above, mutations "All mutations caught" (25 of 25). `scripts/check_store_grid.sh`: all 7148 expected failing cells match. `scripts/run_safety_matrix.sh`: 436 PASS, 0 FAIL. The fuzz record is stale for `src/` (reported by `scripts/check_fuzz_fresh.sh`; Z-0003 renews it after Z-0012).
    - **Awaiting review:** another model or session re-runs the probes and tries sibling routes. Re-run: `for p in 410 411 412 413 gap_34 gap_35; do timeout 30 ./build/zoril -I stdlib=stdlib --emit-zil -o /dev/null bench/safety_matrix/probes/${p}_*.zrl; done` (each must fail with a borrow error; `414` must compile); `sh scripts/run_safety_matrix.sh`; take the two new `else if` branches out of `check_assign` (the ones that call `place_writes_through_pointer`) and watch the probes pass again. Siblings to probe: a field write of a borrow made in an inner block, a borrow stored through a `match` arm's binding, an `x.f.g[i] = ..` chain, a write into a field of a struct held in a `Vec`, and a write after a move of `x`.
    - **Reviewed (2026-11-03, a different model): the fix holds.** Two findings, both re-run in this session against `main`'s compiler and this branch's:
        1. **Three holes the fix does not reach.** A slice written into a local struct's field through a pointer to it is not recorded as a borrow the struct holds, because `place_writes_through_pointer` excludes the write: through a local `&mut` (`{ let r = &mut w; r.s = v.as_slice(); }`), through a callee's `&mut` parameter (`set_fn(&mut w, v.as_slice())` with `w.s = s` inside), and through `self` in a method (`w.set(v.as_slice())`). Each then passes `a.reset()` and reads `w.s[0]`: the reviewer's programs exit 165 (the reset's poison) under both compilers. They are `known_gap` probes `gap_37_local_mutref_field_reset`, `gap_38_param_field_reset` and `gap_39_method_self_field_reset` (commit `95dd900`, which reallocates 77 into the arena after the reset so the matrix sees the stale read: each exits 77 under both compilers), listed under Known gaps with their route class and as class (G) in `doc/SAFETY.md` §6. Z-0044 closes them.
        2. **One new over-rejection.** `struct W { s: [i32], t: i32 }`; `w.s = v.as_slice(); let x = w.s[0]; a.reset(); return x - 42 + w.t - 3;` is safe: `main`'s compiler accepts it and it exits 0. With this fix it is refused: "cannot mutably borrow 'a' while it is already borrowed" at the reset, because the borrow is tracked per local, and `w` stays live for its other field `w.t`. Recorded on Z-0017.
    - **Status:** reviewed; the checkbox stays open because the maintainer merges it.
    - **Merged (2026-11-03):** the maintainer merged it into `main` with #10. Checks as recorded above: `make test` 3177/3177, `make preflight-changed` exit 0 with all 25 mutations caught and the rejection differential passing; the fuzz record is stale for `src/` until Z-0003's renewal after Z-0012.

- [x] +safety +fuzz +tests Z-0008 S1.2 The store grid gains the routes it does not cover yet
    > **Created:** 2026-11-03 **Before:** Z-0010, whose summary work these cells measure.
    > **Completed:** 2026-11-03 git:f533dda

    - **Add** to `scripts/check_store_grid.sh`'s generator, each with a safe twin: path values whose callee stores rather than forwards (inside mutual recursion; into a 65th parameter); a carrier that bundles the arena itself in a struct literal or tuple beside an external field; a destination returned by a closure call; carrier values for an aggregate a call produced (through a local, a method, an identity function, a closure) and a reference to a carrier reaching the callee directly, through a `let`, an identity call or a phi; a `static mut` destination; and a two-module mode for the summary cells (the callee compiled with `-c --write-interface`, the caller importing its `.zri`).
    - The new failing cells go into the expected-failures file. **Verification:** `make store-grid`, `make preflight`.
    - **Done (2026-11-03):** three of the routes, in `scripts/fuzz/store_grid.lua` (and the axis lists of `store_grid_classes.lua`): paths `store_mutual_rec` and `store_param_65` (the callee stores into `*out` rather than forwarding; they combine only with `deref_out`) and destination `closure_call_mut` (a closure returning `&mut out.s`, the shape of `gap_29`). 1896 new valid cells, each with its twin: 1660 unsafe cells are accepted and went into `bench/fuzz/store_grid_expected_failures.txt` (1375 `closure_call_mut`, 130 `store_mutual_rec`, 155 `store_param_65`; they exercise `gap_29`, `gap_28` and `gap_33`); the rest are rejected. Three new 1-axis classes (`path=store_mutual_rec`, `path=store_param_65`, `destination=closure_call_mut`). The regenerated `store_grid_classes.txt` also drops the 12 classes Z-0007 closed (the gate does not compare that file, so they had gone stale).
    - **Over-rejections exposed:** 96 new safe twins are rejected and recorded in `bench/fuzz/store_grid_twin_rejections.txt` (398 to 494): 90 through `closure_call_mut` and 6 through the storing paths, all with a `ref_local` or `elem_ref` source and a struct-field, tuple-slot or array-slot carrier, e.g. `elem_ref__array_slot__closure__closure_call_mut__frame_ends` ("region escape: the callee stores a pointer to local stack storage where it outlives this function"). **Correction:** these are not caused by Z-0007's rule, as this note first said. `main`'s compiler refuses all 96 too, each with the same region-escape error (re-run 2026-11-03), so they are existing over-rejections that the new axes expose. That is Z-0017's class; it is not fixed here.
    - **`static mut` destination, ruled out:** the language refuses the declaration. `static mut SLOT: [i32] = ...` and `static mut SLOT: &i32 = &0;` both fail with "a static mut cannot hold a reference or slice (use a raw pointer)" and "a static or constant initializer must be a constant", and a `fn` value is not a constant initializer either, so safe code has no way to write a frame view into a static mut and the grid cannot hold such a cell. If a regression guard is wanted it is a `compile_reject` probe on the declaration.
    - **Split (2026-11-03):** the routes not done here are tasks of their own: Z-0040 (carriers that bundle the arena), Z-0041 (carriers a call produced, and references to a carrier) and Z-0042 (the two-module mode). This task is complete with the three routes above.
    - **Checks:** `sh scripts/check_store_grid.sh`: all 8808 expected failing cells match (16432 valid cells, 494 over-rejected twins, 43 hole classes, 0 generator errors, 228 s). `make test`: Total: 3177 run, 3177 passed, 0 failed, 33 skipped. `make preflight` was not run: its full mutation set takes hours on this 4-vCPU VM, and this change touches no `src/`, `stdlib/` or `tools/` file.

- [x] +safety +fuzz +tests Z-0040 S1.3 The store grid gains carriers that bundle the arena itself
    > **Created:** 2026-11-03 **Split from:** Z-0008. **Before:** Z-0010.
    > **Completed:** 2026-11-03 git:55388e6

    - **Add** to `scripts/fuzz/store_grid.lua`, each with a safe twin: a carrier that bundles the arena itself in a struct literal, and one that bundles it in a tuple, beside an external field (the destination's reference), so a view of the arena and the arena travel together. Add the values to `store_grid_classes.lua`'s axis lists too, or they never appear as classes.
    - The new accepted unsafe cells go into `bench/fuzz/store_grid_expected_failures.txt` and any new twin rejections into `store_grid_twin_rejections.txt`, through `sh scripts/check_store_grid.sh --record`; check that the diff adds only cells of the new values. **Verification:** `make store-grid`, `make test`.
    - **Done (2026-11-03):** carriers `arena_struct_bundle` and `arena_tuple_bundle`, for the `arena_vec` source only. The arena moves into `ArenaBundle { ext_tag: ext_tag, a: a }` or `(ext_tag, a)`, `ext_tag` being a new `i32` parameter of `escape_storer`; the vector is made in `&b.a` (`&b.1`), and the reset, drop and move invalidations act on the bundle (`b.a.reset()`, `@drop(b)`, `sink(b)`). The twin bundles the caller's `&Arena` instead.
    - **Departure from the text above:** the field beside the arena comes from a parameter, as in `gap_31_struct_literal_ext_field` (the probe this carrier is taken from), not from the destination's reference. Moving `out` into the bundle would make every destination write go through `b.ext`, and the text's parenthetical was a guess made when the task was split.
    - **Result:** 1248 new valid cells. 486 unsafe cells are accepted and joined the expected failures (8808 to 9294), all of the new carriers; no other cell moved. No new twin rejection (494 unchanged), 0 generator errors, and four new 2-axis classes: each bundle with `path=fn_val` and with `path=param_65` (43 to 47). The four sampled shapes are refused with a safety reason (region escape, dropping or moving the borrowed bundle) and their twins compile.
    - **Checks:** `sh scripts/check_store_grid.sh --record`: 9294 failing cells, 494 over-rejected twins, 47 classes, 0 generator errors; the diff adds only `arena_*_bundle` lines.

- [x] +safety +fuzz +tests Z-0041 S1.4 The store grid gains carriers a call produced, and references to a carrier
    > **Created:** 2026-11-03 **Split from:** Z-0008. **Before:** Z-0010.
    > **Completed:** 2026-11-03 git:6343b7d

    - **Add**, each with a safe twin: carrier values for an aggregate a call produced (through a local, a method, an identity function, a closure), and a reference to a carrier reaching the callee directly, through a `let`, an identity call or a phi. Same recording and checks as Z-0040.
    - **Done (2026-11-03):** eight carriers, all wrapping the view in `Carrier { v }`. Four take the aggregate from a call: `call_local` (`make_carrier(src_val)` into a local), `call_method` (`maker.make(src_val)`), `call_identity` (`id_carrier(Carrier { v: src_val })`) and `call_closure` (a closure returning the carrier). Four pass a reference to a local carrier to `take_ref(c: &Carrier) -> PT`: `ref_direct` (`take_ref(&carrier_obj)`), `ref_let` (through `let carrier_ref = &carrier_obj`), `ref_identity` (through `id_ref(&carrier_obj)`) and `ref_phi` (an `if` on `clobber_stack() > 0` choosing between two carriers' references, so the join is not folded).
    - **Result:** 16640 new valid cells (17680 to 34320). 7821 unsafe cells are accepted and joined the expected failures (9294 to 17115), all of the new carriers, none removed; 0 generator errors; four new 2-axis classes, all `call_method` (with `local_vec`, `string`, `path=fn_val`, `path=param_65`; 47 to 51).
    - **Over-rejection found:** 3438 new safe twins are refused (494 to 3932). 3402 of them are every twin of `ref_let`, `ref_identity` and `ref_phi` (1134 each), while `ref_direct`'s twins are accepted. So a reference to a local carrier that reaches the callee through a `let`, an identity call or a phi makes the callee's result read as a pointer into the carrier's stack storage, though `take_ref` returns the view the carrier holds. Example: `arena_vec__ref_let__direct__deref_out__frame_ends`'s twin, "region escape: returning a pointer to local stack storage". The other 36 are `call_local` and `call_method` twins through `closure_call_mut` with a `ref_local` or `elem_ref` source, Z-0017's existing class. Not fixed here; the reference-through-`let` form is the one to start from.
    - **Checks:** `sh scripts/check_store_grid.sh --record` (about 9 minutes now): 17115 failing cells, 3932 over-rejected twins, 51 classes, 0 generator errors; the diff adds only lines of the eight new carriers.

- [x] +safety +fuzz +tests Z-0042 S1.5 A two-module mode for the store grid's summary cells
    > **Created:** 2026-11-03 **Split from:** Z-0008. **Before:** Z-0010, which is expected to close these cells.
    > **Completed:** 2026-11-03 git:ca53660

    - **Do:** for the cells whose route crosses a call summary, compile the callee as its own module with `-c --write-interface` and the caller against its `.zri`, as `zbuild` does. `scripts/check_store_grid.sh` then builds such a cell in two steps; generate the callee and the caller as two files.
    - The new accepted unsafe cells go into the expected-failures file. **Verification:** `make store-grid`, `make test`.
    - **Done (2026-11-03):** `scripts/fuzz/store_grid.lua --generate-modular DIR` writes, per cell, `pathmod.zrl` (the path's functions, made `pub`) and `unsafe.zrl`/`twin.zrl` importing them. The new `scripts/check_store_grid_modular.sh` compiles `pathmod` with `-c --interproc-summary --write-interface <iface>/<key>.zri` and each side with `-c --interproc-summary --iface-dir <iface>`, judges them as the grid does (same safety-diagnostic list, read from `check_store_grid.sh`), and compares with its own `bench/fuzz/store_grid_modular_expected_failures.txt` and `store_grid_modular_twin_rejections.txt`. `check_store_grid.sh` runs it as step 3b, `--record` included. A guard compiles the first cell against an empty interface, which must fail ("the module defines no `forward_helper`"): a key that misses would make the compiler read the source silently.
    - **Scope:** the paths whose callee is a free function (`helper`, `two_level_helper`, `fn_val`, `mutual_rec`, `param_65`, `same_param` with `deref_out` only, `store_mutual_rec`, `store_param_65`), with the `direct` carrier and the `frame_ends` invalidation over every source and destination: 318 cells, about 3 s. Methods and traits (`method`, `self_method`, `trait_method`) need their structs and impls moved into the module too, and are not covered. Widening the carriers and invalidations is a size choice, not a code change.
    - **Result:** 125 unsafe cells are accepted and 8 twins are refused, 0 generator errors. Compared with the whole-program build of the same 318 cells (160 accepted, 8 twins refused): no cell is accepted only by the two-module build, and the refused twins are the same 8. In the other direction, 35 cells the whole-program build accepts are refused when the callee is read from its interface. 33 of them have the `string` source, spread over seven destinations; the other two are `store_mutual_rec` with `arena_vec` and `local_vec` into `deref_out`, the shape of `gap_28` (the mutual-recursion summary). So `gap_28` shows only in the whole-program build here; what makes the interface path stricter is not established.
    - **Checks:** `sh scripts/check_store_grid_modular.sh --record`, then `sh scripts/check_store_grid.sh` with the gate wired in (results in the PR).

    > **Created:** 2026-11-03

    - **Reviewed (2026-11-03, a different session, Ubuntu 24.04.5, GNU ld 2.42, `/bin/sh` is dash):** the commits were rebased before they were merged, so the SHAs above are not in `main`'s history. They are `3def67d` and `69d5fae` (runtime note, two_stage.md backport record), `fe4ba5e` (`external_stub.s`), `dde4bc6` (`marker_density.sh`, check 7b), `2ba00f5` and `31d5393` (scdoc 1.11.5), and `4eb892c` (result-file race). Every re-run reproduced. Where the re-run needed a fix taken out, it was taken out in a `git archive HEAD` copy in the session scratchpad, so `build/` was not touched.
        1. **Executable stack:** `build/zbuild` gives `GNU_STACK ... RW` for a whole-program build and for a `--hosted` build. `build/zoril`, `build/zilc`, `build/zbuild`, `build/zoril-stage1`, `build/zilc-stage1` and `bootstrap/stage0-c/build/zoril-c` are all `RW`. With the note stripped from `rt/runtime.s` and both runtime objects reassembled, `scripts/check_runtimes.sh` fails on `build/runtime.o` and on `build/runtime_hosted.o`, separately from its twin byte-identity check, and exits 1. `rt/runtime.s` and `bootstrap/stage1/rt/runtime.s` are byte-identical.
        2. **`marker_density.sh` and check 7b:** under dash the old `set -euo pipefail` dies with "Illegal option -o pipefail" (exit 2), and the fixed script runs. With 7b removed, `check_portability.sh --selftest` fails ("bashism set -o pipefail in bad.sh was not detected"), and the real run passes the old script, so checkbashisms does not catch it on this host. 7b's regex flags `-o pipefail`, `-euo`, `-Eeuo`, `-e -o`, `-o errexit -o pipefail`, `x; set -o pipefail` and `set -o pipefail 2>/dev/null || true`. It does not flag a comment, an `echo` of the text, or `set -opipefail`, which bash and dash both refuse anyway.
        3. **scdoc 1.11.5:** `scripts/test_scdoc_min.sh` and `scripts/test_check_deps.sh` pass (114 passed, 1 skipped). With `scdoc_old` forced to "not old", six cases of the first fail and the new preflight case of the second fails. `scdoc_old` compares each field as a number: 1.11.4, 1.11, 1.10.99, `unknown` and an empty version count as old; 1.11.5, 1.11.10, 1.12, 2.0 and 1.11.5.1 do not.
        4. **Result-file race:** `scripts/test_result_file.sh` passes. Writing `> N.st` instead of renaming it fails check 1 (3 run, 2 passed, 1 failed). Separately, overwriting rather than appending the "left no result" line fails check 2.
    - **Siblings probed:**
        - **Objects linked that QBE did not write:** of the five tracked `.s` files, only Z-0039's two lack the note. One untracked one was found: step 7 of `scripts/test_incremental.sh` generated `c_ffi_lib.s` without the note, so `c_ffi_ld` linked `RWE`, with ld's warning, and the test still passed. Fixed in `0925c4a`: the fixture now has the note, and the step fails on a `GNU_STACK RWE` program. With the note taken out, it fails ("c_ffi binary has an executable stack"). No other script or test writes assembly. The C fixtures go through `$CC`, which emits the note.
        - **Files `scripts/test.sh` writes in one process and reads in another:** `N.out` is safe, because the worker closes it before it renames `N.st` into place. The plan file is safe, because the dispatcher writes it before it starts the pool. `stop` is read only for its existence. `pool.pid` is still written with a plain `>`, but `pool_alive` checks `kill -0 $pool` first, and util-linux `setsid` does not fork when it is started as a background job of a shell without job control. So `$pool` is the pool itself, and the pid file is only a fallback (checked: `$!` stays alive); not changed. `.lock` is also written with a plain `>` after `flock`. A run that loses the lock reads it only to word its "another test run is using" message, and it exits 2 either way; in that window the message can name an empty or stale pid. That is a wording issue only, and it was not changed.
        - **Bash-only `set` options in `#!/bin/sh`:** none in the 130 tracked `#!/bin/sh` scripts. All of them end in `.sh`, so check 7b's `*.sh` filter misses none. The five `set -uo pipefail` users under `bootstrap/stage0-c/scripts/` are `#!/usr/bin/env bash`.
    - **Checks:** `make test`: 3178 run, 3178 passed, 0 failed, 38 skipped (on the content of `0925c4a`). Only `scripts/` and this file changed, so `make preflight-changed` was not run. `0925c4a` is a fixture fix written by this reviewing session; it is not a safety rule, but a later session may re-run its take-out check above.

- [x] +tools +tests Z-0043 `make preflight-changed` checks a committed change, and builds the base of a stricter rule
    > **Created:** 2026-11-03
    > **Completed:** 2026-11-03 git:b1c9851

    - **Now, two ways the gate runs less than it says:**
        1. Once a change is committed and the branch has no upstream, or its upstream already holds the commits, the gate compares `HEAD` with itself: `mutation-check.sh --changed` prints "No mutation targets a changed file (no changes vs upstream / HEAD)" and the rejection differential prints `[SKIP] reject-diff: none of ... differs from HEAD`, and the gate still passes. Seen on Z-0007 (`1a02a61`).
        2. `scripts/check_reject_diff.sh` seeds the base worktree's `build/` with the working tree's `build/zoril` and rebuilds the base source with it. When the change adds a rule the base source breaks, that build fails ("check_reject_diff: building HEAD failed"): Z-0007's compiler rejects `main`'s `src/main.zrl` and `src/parser/decl.zrl`.
    - **Workaround used on Z-0007:** `git branch --set-upstream-to=origin/<parent branch>`, so `@{upstream}..HEAD` named the change's commits; a copy of the compiler from before the change kept as `build/zoril.base`; and `PREFLIGHT_REJECT_DIFF='sh wrapper.sh' make preflight-changed`, where `wrapper.sh` ran `cp build/zoril build/zoril.keep; cp build/zoril.base build/zoril; sh scripts/check_reject_diff.sh --change HEAD "$1"; rc=$?; cp build/zoril.keep build/zoril; exit $rc`. With the base compiler as the seed, both scratch trees build from their own source: the base compiler compiles both the base source and the change's.
    - **Do:** (1) with no base to compare against, say so as a failure or name the base to use (`PREFLIGHT_BASE=<commit>`), instead of passing; (2) seed the base build with a compiler built from the base: build it through stage1 as a fresh checkout does, or keep the previous `build/zoril`. Add a self-test for each in `scripts/test_preflight.sh`: a committed change with no upstream must not pass silently, and a change that rejects code in the base source must still produce a differential.
    - **Verification:** `scripts/test_preflight.sh`, `make test`, and a `make preflight-changed` on a stricter-rule change that runs its mutations and its differential without a wrapper.
    - **Fixed (2026-11-03, `b1c9851`):** (1) `scripts/lib/preflight_base.sh` picks the base of a `--changed` run: `PREFLIGHT_BASE`; else the upstream when `HEAD` is ahead of it; else the merge base with origin's default branch when `HEAD` has commits beyond it; else `HEAD` when the tree has uncommitted changes. With none, `preflight-changed` refuses before anything starts, naming `PREFLIGHT_BASE`. The base goes to `mutation-check.sh --changed` (`MUTATION_BASE`, `git diff BASE`) and to the differential (`PREFLIGHT_REJECT_BASE` still overrides it). (2) When `check_reject_diff.sh`'s seeded build of a scratch tree fails, it builds that tree's compiler through stage1 from its own source; `REJECT_DIFF_MAKE` is the test hook.
    - **Self-tests** (`scripts/test_preflight.sh`): case 13 checks the base in scratch repositories (no upstream and no default branch: no base; a pushed branch: its merge base with `origin/main`; an upstream behind `HEAD`; an uncommitted change: `HEAD`). Case 14 checks the refusal before anything starts and the hand-off to `mutation-check`. Case 15 checks the stage1 fallback with a stand-in make. Cases 14a and 15 fail against `main`'s `preflight.sh` and `check_reject_diff.sh` and pass with the change. The existing cases now run with `PREFLIGHT_BASE=HEAD`; case 12 changes what it expects (the chosen base is named, or the run refuses).
    - **Found on the way:** run from a shell rather than from `make test`, `scripts/test_preflight.sh` takes about 10 minutes and fails its later cases. Case 7 runs the real checked suite (it stubs neither `PREFLIGHT_CHECKED` nor `PREFLIGHT_CALIBRATE`); a "Terminated" then reaches the test, whose trap deletes its stub directory, and the later cases fail with "not found". Under `make test` the test passes, as the unchanged one did. My explanation, not verified: the nested suite cannot start there because the run holds the test lock. Case 7 is unchanged from `main`, but I did not run `main`'s version from a shell, so whether it fails the same way there is unchecked. Not fixed here.
    - **Checks:** `make test` (before the last two commits; the only failure was `check-deps-selftest`, whose scratch copy is a `git archive HEAD` and lacked the then-uncommitted library; it passes once committed). `PREFLIGHT_BASE=1a02a61~1 make preflight-changed` on this branch, against the commit before Z-0007, whose source the current compiler refuses, with no wrapper: exit 0. Its results: base taken from `PREFLIGHT_BASE`; 25 mutations targeted, "All mutations caught"; fixpoint PASS; the seeded base build failed and was rebuilt through stage1; reject-diff PASS with 0 newly accepted and 1164 newly refused of 33317 programs (Z-0007's own result); checked suite 1151/1151.

- [x] +safety +tests Z-0054 Merge the `gap_40`–`gap_44` branch and add `known_gap` probes for the two newly confirmed escape holes
    > **Created:** 2026-11-03
    > **Completed:** 2026-11-04 git:8cc54e7

    - **Branch disposition (`claude/gallant-feynman-kiu6ru-gaps-40-44`, commit `f23bf57`):** **merge into `main` (do not discard).** Verified in a scratch worktree: `f23bf57` sits cleanly on `main` (`977f1f4`), adds `gap_40_local_mutref_field_escape`, `gap_41_callee_field_escape`, `gap_42_box_field_escape`, `gap_43_box_field_reset` and `gap_44_borrows_attr_reset`, updates `doc/spec/safety_model.md`, `doc/SAFETY.md` and `README.md`, and passes `check_safety_model.sh`, `check_safety_figures.sh`, `check_comment_noise.sh` and `run_safety_matrix.sh` (all five report `[SKIP]`). Optional cleanup while touching them: drop the unused `fn set_s` helper copied into `gap_40` and `gap_42`.
    - **New hole 1 (`gap_45_closure_mutref_field_escape`):** confirmed during Z-0047's second-model review; add with a Known gaps entry in `doc/spec/safety_model.md` (widening `gap_40`–`gap_43`'s route class to include a closure capturing `&mut` to a local) and updated counts in `doc/SAFETY.md` and `README.md`:

    ```zoril
    import "mem.zrl";
    import "vec.zrl";
    use mem::Arena;
    use vec::Vec;
    struct W { s: [i32], t: i32 }
    fn mk_w() -> W { return W { s: @slice(0 as *i32, 0), t: 0 }; }
    fn f(out: &mut [i32]) {
        let a = match Arena.new(65536) { Result.Ok(a) => a, Result.Err(_) => { return; } };
        let mut v = Vec::<i32>.new_in(&a);
        v.push(42);
        let mut w = mk_w();
        {
            let s = v.as_slice();
            let r = &mut w;
            let mut cl = || { r.s = s; };
            cl();
        }
        *out = w.s;
    }
    fn main() -> i32 {
        let mut dest: [i32] = @slice(0 as *i32, 0);
        f(&mut dest);
        let a2 = match Arena.new(65536) { Result.Ok(a) => a, Result.Err(_) => { return 1; } };
        let mut v2 = Vec::<i32>.new_in(&a2);
        v2.push(77);
        return dest[0];
    }
    ```

    - **New hole 2 (`gap_46_call_ret_mutref_off_by_one`):** `storage_walk_zir` and `note_call_stores_zir` index 0-based `locals` with 1-based `Local.id`, so when `dst` is the last local, `pl_base_ty` reads out-of-bounds as `0`, `base_is_ref` is false, and `stash`'s store summary stays empty (exits 77 on `main`, whereas adding a dead `let _keep: &i32 = &n;` immediately after `dst` is refused):

    ```zoril
    import "mem.zrl";
    import "vec.zrl";
    use mem::Arena;
    use vec::Vec;
    struct Slot { out: &mut [i32] }
    fn unwrap_slot(h: Slot) -> &mut [i32] { return h.out; }
    fn stash(h: Slot, s: [i32]) {
        let dst = unwrap_slot(h);
        *dst = s;
    }
    fn f(out: &mut [i32]) {
        let a = match Arena.new(65536) { Result.Ok(a) => a, Result.Err(_) => { return; } };
        let mut v = Vec::<i32>.new_in(&a);
        v.push(42);
        stash(Slot { out: out }, v.as_slice());
    }
    fn main() -> i32 {
        let mut dest: [i32] = @slice(0 as *i32, 0);
        f(&mut dest);
        let a2 = match Arena.new(65536) { Result.Ok(a) => a, Result.Err(_) => { return 1; } };
        let mut v2 = Vec::<i32>.new_in(&a2);
        v2.push(77);
        return dest[0];
    }
    ```

    - **Verification:** `sh scripts/run_safety_matrix.sh` (all seven report `[SKIP]`), `sh scripts/check_safety_model.sh`, `sh scripts/check_safety_figures.sh`, `make test`.

    - **Done (2026-11-04):** `gap_40`–`gap_44` arrived through PR #20 (`f23bf57`) while this was open, so the duplicate cherry-pick was dropped in the rebase, `gap_45` and `gap_46` added in `e4d7dbe` (both `known_gap`, exit 77), the unused `set_s` dropped from `gap_40`/`gap_42`; the figures are 460 probes, 24 `known_gap`. Checks: `sh scripts/run_safety_matrix.sh` 436/460 passed, 0 mismatches (24 `[SKIP]`), `check_safety_model.sh`, `check_safety_figures.sh` and `check_comment_noise.sh` pass; `make test` on the combined branch: 3215 run, 3215 passed, 0 failed, 52 skipped.

    > **Created:** 2026-11-03 **Split from:** Z-0009. **Depends:** Z-0006, Z-0054. **Check-in:** second-model review complete; ready to rebase and merge.

    - **Build:** in `src/check_zir.zrl`, beside the existing analyses and changing no accept/reject decision, a forward, flow-insensitive fixpoint giving every local `L` two origin sets (`doc/design/provenance.md` §3): `O(L)`, the origins of the addresses it holds, and `S(L)`, the origins of the storage it owns, over `Frame(L)`, `Param(i)` (bit 63 meaning "63 or later"), `Static` and `Unknown`; field-insensitive.

That covers `Use`, `Ref`, `RefMut`, `Call`, `CallPtr`, `StructLiteral`, `EnumVariant`, `EnumPayload`, `Tuple`, `Array`, `ArrayRepeat`, `Boxed`, `Cast`, `PtrFromInt`, `GlobalAddr`, `StringConst`, `FuncAddr` with its environment, `GetEnv`, `Syscall` and `Intrinsic`; the scalar forms yield nothing. Calls read the existing summaries (`ret_ties`, `ret_storage`, `storage_known`), and a `CallPtr` or summary-less call yields `Unknown`. A projected write `x.f = rv` (not through a dereference) adds to `x`.
    - **Ownership:** whether a type owns storage comes from its structure (it contains a `Box`, an owning raw pointer, or a type that does), never `is_collection_type_name`. By-value parameters are locals (`Frame`); the referent of a reference parameter is `Param(i)`.

The note suggests that a read through `r` yields `O(L)` for each `Frame(L)` in `O(r)`, and stays `Param` or `Unknown` otherwise. Decide it with probes, and say in `doc/design/provenance.md` which rule holds and what Z-0045's reproducer gives under it.

- [x] +docs +safety +AI-G Z-0055 B1.1b Amend `doc/design/provenance.md` with the reviewed transfer rules and pre-1.0 language decisions
    > **Created:** 2026-11-03 **Depends:** Z-0047. **Check-in:** second-model review before Z-0048 starts.
    > **Completed:** 2026-11-04 git:6fedda8

    - **Do:** update `doc/design/provenance.md` after Z-0047 merges so the note matches what Z-0048, Z-0010, Z-0057 and Z-0011 will implement:
        1. **Checked contracts (Decision 1):** `#[borrows(..)]`, `#[stores(..)]` and `&self` elision are checked against the body; listing wider ties/stores is legal, narrowing in safe code is a compile error.
        2. **Structural ownership (Decision 2):** a type owns storage iff it is `Box<T>`, implements `Drop` (`Arena`, `BufWriter`, `File`, `FileLock`), is one of the four stdlib collections designated until collection `Drop` lands (`Vec`, `String`, `ByteVec`, `HashMap`), or contains an owning type by value. Raw pointers (`*T`/`*mut T`) do not make `Lexer`, `Parser`, `ArenaMark` or `Args` owners.
        3. **Closures vs `fn` pointers (Decision 3):** defer splitting `fn` pointers from closures until Z-0049's post-fixpoint measurement; record that monomorphized `Fn` traits are rejected.
        4. **Closure environments at `FuncAddr` and `CallPtr` (`gap_11` and `gap_45`):** correct §4's `gap_11` row (`{Env}` inside `_closure_0` is identical on `gap_11` and its safe twin). `FuncAddr(_, env)` copies `O(env)` (`{Frame(env_struct)}`), and `CallPtr(callee, args)` treats `os_through(O(callee))` as an implicit read-write argument alongside `args` in both unknown-call store effects and the unknown-call store check.
        5. **Parameter split, compact summaries, and pairwise `.zri` (`Z-0010` / `Z-0057`):** split parameter seeding into `ParamAddr(i)` (the parameter's own slot, feeding `ret_storage`) and `ParamContents(i)` (what parameter `i` reaches, feeding `ret_ties`); iterate Tarjan SCCs over compact per-function transfer summaries rather than retaining every function's ZIR in memory; serialize pairwise `i -> j` store edges (with bit 63 overflow in both directions) and separate `ret_storage`/`ret_ties` masks in `.zri`.
        6. **Scalar, cast and `extern` boundary:** casts and pointer-width arithmetic (`usize`/`isize`/`u64`/`i64`, `Vec.get`) preserve origins; narrow scalars (`<= 32` bits, `bool`, `char`, `f32`) clear them; `PtrFromInt` adds `Unknown`; unannotated `extern "C"` calls yield `{Static}` while safe wrappers carry `#[borrows(..)]`.

Probing the `Box` structural twin with `./build/zoril -I stdlib=stdlib --emit-origins` found two `Box` defects in the new text and `os_walk`: **(D1, over-rejection in §3 _Destination reach_ and `os_walk`):** `let mut b = box S { s: "lit" }` has type `BoxMut<S>` (and both `Box<T>` and `BoxMut<T>` permit stores, `src/check_zir.zrl:380`), with `O(b)={Static}` and `S(b)={Frame(_25)}`. Listing "a mutable `Box`" as a leaf mutable handle in §3 _Destination reach_ (and Z-0048 fix 4) makes `set_box(&mut b, arena_slice)` (`fn set_box(b: &mut Box<S>, s: [u8]) { (**b).s = s; }`) follow `O(b)={Static}` and refuse the safe `Box` twin of `v.push(arena_str)`; likewise `os_walk` (`src/check_zir.zrl:5730,5747`) treats `Boxed`/`BoxedMut` as a pointer crossing (`dm = O(b) | S(b)`), putting `Static` in `dm` on `(*b).s = arena_slice` (and `Param(0)` on a by-value `mut b: Box<S>` parameter). Because `Box`/`BoxMut` owns its allocation in `S(b)` (already followed by `Frame(b) -> S(b)`), `Box`/`BoxMut` — like `*T`/`*mut T`, `&T`, `[T]` and aggregates — must recurse into `T` rather than count as a leaf mutable handle (`&mut`, `[mut T]`, `fn`). **(D2, soundness hole in `OriginSets` masked today only by `check_borrow_escapes_frame`):** `ZirRvalue.Boxed`, `os_call` and `compute_origins_zir` put `T`'s field origins in `O(b)`, while `os_walk` crosses `Boxed`/`BoxedMut` and makes `os_read` run `os_through_split` a second time; on `struct W { s: [i32] } fn wrap(s: [i32]) -> Box<W> { return box W { s: s }; }` with `let b = wrap(v.as_slice()); let x = (*b).s; *out = x;`, `os_call` sets `O(b)={Frame(_3), Frame(a)}` and `S(b)={Frame(_3), Frame(a)}` (`tied` skips `owner`), and `--emit-origins` prints `x O={} S={}`, which would let `*out = x` escape once Z-0012 deletes `check_borrow_escapes_frame`. Treating `Boxed`/`BoxedMut` in `os_walk` as an owning value wrapper (unwrapping `Deref`/auto-deref to `os_pointee` without setting `crossed = true` or calling `os_through`, while `os_call` keeps `owner` in `S` for `Boxed`/`BoxedMut` results) fixes both D1 and D2 and aligns §4's `gap_42` description (`(*b).s = v.as_slice()` writes `O(b)` as a projected write, and through `&mut b`/`&mut *b` via `os_write_memory`).

D1, the `os_walk` box projection rule, §4's `gap_40`–`gap_42` row, "the buffer-escape rule of §1", and Z-0048 fix 4 are verified. Probing the sibling routes of fix 6's new clause ("keep `owner` in `S` for a call returning a box even when the result is tied") with `--emit-origins` found **(E, `S` of an owning call result when tied to a non-arena parameter or wrapped in an aggregate):** (1) returning an aggregate containing a `Box` or `Drop` type alongside a tied slice (`fn try_wrap(s: [i32]) -> Option<Box<W>>` or `fn make_pair(s: [i32]) -> Result<(Arena, [i32]), i32>`) is not a top-level box, so `--emit-origins` prints `b O={Param(1)} S={Param(1)}` and `pair O={Param(1)} S={Param(1)}` (`Frame` lost from `S`; once Z-0010 gives `Vec::new_in` `ret_storage=0, ret_ties={0}` and Z-0012 deletes §1's buffer-escape rule, `*out = v.as_slice()` on `Vec::new_in(&pair.0)` gets `O={Param(1)}` and `SIGSEGV`s when `pair.0` drops); (2) returning a heap collection with a tied non-arena parameter (`fn make_vec(s: [u8]) -> Vec<[u8]> { let mut v = Vec::new(); v.push(s); return v; }`, or `Vec::<i32>.with_capacity(n: usize)` once Z-0010 computes `ret_ties` from the body and sees `cap: usize` in `Vec`) also sets `tied = true` and drops `owner` from `S`, while `wrap_in_arena(&local_a, "lit") -> Vec<[u8]>` copies `"lit"`'s `Static` from `vo` into `S(v)`. Fix: for an owning return (`os_owns(ret_ty)`), only tied arguments whose type can reach `Arena` contribute to `vs`, and `vs` keeps `owner` whenever `ret_ty` is or contains by value a `Box`/`BoxMut` or `Drop` type, or no tied argument can reach `Arena` (and in Z-0010/Z-0057's summary, also when `S(ret)` in the callee contains a `Frame`/`Unknown` origin, e.g. `fn f(a: &Arena) -> Vec<i32> { return Vec::new(); }`).

    ```zoril
    fn pass_slot(h: Slot) -> Slot { return h; }
    fn write(d: &mut [i32], s: [i32]) { *d = s; }
    fn write_slot(d: Slot, s: [i32]) { *d.out = s; }
    fn stash(h: Slot, s: [i32]) { let d = pass_slot(h); *d.out = s; }       // s_struct
    fn stash(h: Slot, s: [i32]) { let d = unwrap_slot(h); write(d, s); }    // c_ref
    fn stash(h: Slot, s: [i32]) { let d = pass_slot(h); write_slot(d, s); } // c_struct
    fn stash(h: Slot, s: [i32]) { let d = pass_slot(h); write(d.out, s); }  // c_field
    ```

- [x] +safety +tests +AI-D Z-0058 Fix 1-based `Local.id` lookup in `note_call_stores_zir` without false-positive escape on local collections
    > **Created:** 2026-11-04 **Split from:** Z-0056. **Check-in:** stop for review after it. **Closes:** `gap_49`, `gap_50`, `gap_51`.
    > **Completed:** 2026-11-04 git:04aa68d **Closed as superseded:** no code change; `gap_49`–`gap_51` move to Z-0010's expected closures and Z-0012's flip list, and stay `known_gap` until then.

    - **Why its own task:** In `note_call_stores_zir` (`src/check_zir.zrl:5009`), `locals[jb].ty` indexes 0-based `locals` with 1-based `args[j].base` (`Local.id`). Fixing it naively to `os_local_ty` causes false-positive escape errors in `zbuild` (`tools/zbuild/src/main.zrl:112:14: error: cannot store 'manifest'`).
    - **Reproducer:** In `tools/zbuild/src/build.zrl` `prune_manifest(&ctx, &mut manifest, &mut removed)`, `let mut kept = Vec::<Entry>::new_in_raw(ctx.arena)` allocates a local vector in `ctx.arena` (`reach[kept] = {ctx}`). Calling `kept.push(e)` has `args[0] = &mut kept`. Because `&mut kept` holds a reference, `jb_is_ref` is true, so `t_reach` takes `reach[kept]` (`{ctx}`). Because the summary format uses flat bitmasks rather than pairwise edges `i -> j`, `prune_manifest` is summarized as storing `manifest` into `ctx`, which the caller `run_gc` rejects as storing `manifest` (borrowed from caller arena) into `ctx` (an external target).
    - **Do:** Distinguish local variables that merely use a caller-supplied allocator from actual references into caller memory in `note_call_stores_zir`, or refine the target reach calculation so `Vec.push(&mut kept, e)` writes into `kept`'s elements rather than treating `kept`'s allocator as a store target. Close `gap_49_call_ret_mutref_callee_write`, `gap_50_call_ret_struct_callee_write_slot`, and `gap_51_call_ret_struct_field_callee_write`.
    - **Verification:** `make test`, `make preflight-changed`, rejection differential against HEAD. Stop for review.
    - **Finding (2026-11-04): the naive fix also refuses `src/`, not only `zbuild`.** A compiler with only `jb_ty = os_local_ty(locals, args[j].base)` (scratch worktree under `build/z58/`) refuses 8 calls in the compiler's own source, so the self-hosting fixpoint fails: `src/lower_zir/exhaust.zrl:660:24`, `:678:20`, `:705:24`, `:717:20`, `:415:20`, `:428:12`, `:781:20` ("cannot store 'rows'/'spec'/'def'") and `src/fmt/fmt.zrl:131:15` ("cannot store 'p.doc'"), plus `tools/zbuild/src/main.zrl:112:14` and `:114:5`. Instrumenting the site (print the callee when the corrected lookup is what makes a call-result local's `reach` a target) gives 110 such targets in `src/main.zrl` and 95 in `tools/zbuild/src/main.zrl`. Most are `Vec::push`/`set` on a collection the caller just made (`Vec<FnDecl>` 20, `Vec<[u8]>` 11, `Vec<[mut u32]>` 8, `Vec<TypeParam>` 7, `Vec<usize>.set` 6); the rest are constructor-shaped calls (`Parser::new_modular` 20, `Resolver::new` 9, `zbuild`'s `source_state` 24 and `reuse_frozen` 12, `Command::arg` 23) and `ZirVerifier::check_stmt` 8.
    - **Lead ruled out: a type predicate at this site.** Replacing `type_holds_reference` with a walk that answers true only for a type reaching `&mut`, `[mut T]` or a `fn` value (looking through raw pointers, boxes, `&T`, aggregates; cycles cut by a set of entered types) leaves 98 and 93 targets and the same 10 refusals, and still refuses `gap_49`–`gap_51`. The reason: `v.push(x)` passes `&mut v` through a temporary that inherits `from_call` from `v` (`storage_walk_zir`'s `Ref`/`RefMut` arm), so the target's type is `&mut Vec<..>`, a mutable handle whatever the `Vec` holds. `let t = &mut kept` (a reference to a local of this frame) and `gap_49`'s `d = unwrap_slot(h)` (an alias into the caller's memory) are the same at the type level, and so are `exhaust.zrl`'s fresh `self.alloc_u32s(n)` (`[mut u32]` in `self`'s arena) and `unwrap_slot`'s result: both "point into memory reached through argument 0" under the flat `ret_ties`/`reach` masks.
    - **What would separate them:** whether a call result is a fresh allocation or an alias of a parameter's contents, and whether a reference temporary points at a local of this frame. That is Z-0010's `ParamAddr`/`ParamContents` and `ret_fresh` and Z-0048's destination reach (design note §3); under them `gap_49`'s destination is `Param` (refused) and `kept`'s is `Frame(kept)` plus `S(kept)`, with a value carrying no `Frame` (accepted). Adding a freshness bit to the legacy `storage_walk_zir`/`note_call_stores_zir` summary first would build the same thing in code Z-0012 deletes.
    - **Decision needed (stopped here):** fold `gap_49`–`gap_51` into Z-0048/Z-0010 (they stay `known_gap` until Z-0012 flips them; Z-0048's expected-closure list gains them) and close Z-0058 as superseded, or add a freshness bit to the legacy summary in this task. The test written for the false positive (`prune_manifest`'s shape with a caller-supplied arena, a `derive(Clone)` `Entry` holding a `Vec`, and `kept.push(e)`) is accepted by both the current and the naive compiler, so it does not reproduce the refusal; the `zbuild` and `src/` sites above are the reproducers.
    - **Decided (2026-11-04, maintainer): fold into the series.** Checked with `--emit-origins` on `build/zoril` that the origin sets already separate the two cases: in `gap_49`'s `stash`, `d O={Param(0)}` (an external destination); in the `prune_manifest` shape, the pushed `e O={Param(1)}` carries no `Frame` (accepted under the store rule, though `S(kept)` gaining `Param(1)` is the leak Z-0048 fix 5 covers). The store inside `stash` is legal, so the refusal is at its caller and needs `stash`'s summary computed from the origin sets: `gap_49`–`gap_51` are added to Z-0010's expected closures and Z-0012's flip list. `note_call_stores_zir`'s off-by-one stays until Z-0012 deletes the function. No code changed; `make test` not run (task text only).

- [x] +safety +tests +AI-D Z-0048 B1.2 The store and return rules on the origin sets, under `--provenance`
    > **Created:** 2026-11-03 **Split from:** Z-0009. **Depends:** Z-0047, Z-0055, Z-0056. **Check-in:** stop for review after it.
    > **Completed:** 2026-11-04 git:896eeaf

    - **First, transfer fixes from Z-0047's and Z-0055's reviews (in `src/check_zir.zrl`):**
        1. **Structural `os_owns` (Decision 2):** count `ILType.Boxed`/`BoxedMut`, types implementing `Drop`, the four designated stdlib collections (`Vec`, `String`, `ByteVec`, `HashMap`), and aggregates containing an owning type by value; remove `ILType.RawPtr`/`RawPtrMut` from `os_owns`. Update `tests/language/memory/origins/box_cast.zrl` (`S={}`) and add a golden test showing a view read through `&Lexer` carries no `Frame(lex)`.
        2. **Closure environment at `FuncAddr` and `CallPtr`:** `FuncAddr(_, env)` copies `O(env)` (`{Frame(env_struct)}`), and `CallPtr(callee, args)` includes `os_through(O(callee))` alongside `args` in `os_unknown_call_effects` and in the unknown-call store check. Add golden/twin tests for `gap_11`, `gap_45` (`w` gains `Frame(a)` + `Unknown`), and the safe twin `fn safe(out: &mut [i32], view: [i32]) { let mut cl = || { *out = view; }; cl(); }` (accepted under `--provenance`).
        3. **Unannotated `extern` is a known summary (design note §3, _Scalars, casts and `extern`_):** in `os_call`, `known = f.storage_known && !(f.is_extern && !f.has_summary)` sends every unannotated `extern` to the unknown branch, so `zoril_allocator_alloc` yields `O={Unknown}` and `memcpy`/`memset` run `os_unknown_call_effects`; without this fix every function calling them (`compute_origins_zir` itself) fails the new rules. Treat it as known: the result is `{Static}` when it carries an address and no `#[borrows(..)]` ties it, and store effects come only from a declared `#[stores(..)]`. Golden test: `--emit-origins` on a call to an unannotated `extern fn c_get_ptr() -> *u8` gives `O={Static}` and no `unknown-call` line.
        4. **Destination reach (design note §3, _Destination reach_):** the destinations the store, call and unknown-call checks use add, for each `Frame(L)`, `S(L)` and, when `L`'s type can hold a mutable handle, `O(L)`, to a fixpoint. The leaf handles are `&mut`, `[mut T]` and `fn` values; `Box`/`BoxMut`, raw pointers, `&T`, `[T]` and aggregates recurse into their element or field types (so `Vec<str>` holds none, `Vec<Slot>` with `Slot { out: &mut [i32] }` does). Not an unconditional `os_reach_all`: `let mut v = Vec::<str>.new(); v.push("lit"); v.push(arena_str);`, `let mut w = S { s: "" }; set_s(&mut w, arena_str);` and the `Box` twin `let mut b = box S { s: "lit" }; set_box(&mut b, arena_str);` (`fn set_box(b: &mut Box<S>, s: [u8]) { (**b).s = s; }`) must stay accepted. Closes `gap_47` and `gap_48` under the flag and keeps the direct-call twin `write_slot(r, v.as_slice())` (`fn write_slot(r: &Slot, s: [i32]) { *r.out = s; }`) refused by the new rules alone, so Z-0012 can delete the buffer-escape rule (design note §1); add it as a golden or `compile_reject` test that names the new rule.
        5. **Store effects grow `S` only by what is owned:** `os_summary_store_effects` and `os_unknown_call_effects` pass one reach set as both the held and the owned value (`os_write_memory(c, module, locals, tw, tsv, tsv)`), so `v.push("lit")` puts `Static` in `S(v)`. Pass the stored value's owned origins separately; golden test: after `v.push("lit")`, `S(v)` has no `Static`.
        6. **A box is a value wrapper, not a pointer (design note §3, _Details the transfer needs_; Z-0055 review D1/D2):** `os_walk` counts `ILTYPE_BOXED`/`ILTYPE_BOXEDMUT` as a pointer crossing, while `ZirRvalue.Boxed` and `os_call` already put `T`'s origins in `O(b)`, so `(*b).s` reads through the box twice. Step into a box as a projection (advance `ty` to `os_pointee`, consume `Projection.Deref`, do not set `crossed` or call `os_through`).
        7. **The `S` of an owning call result (design note §3, _Details the transfer needs_; Z-0055 review E):** `os_call`'s `if tied { vs |= vo } else { vs |= owner }` loses the fresh allocation whenever the result is tied. For a result type that owns: only tied arguments whose type can reach an `Arena` add to `S`; `owner` is added when the result type is or contains by value a `Box`/`BoxMut` or a `Drop` type, or no tied argument can reach an `Arena` (Z-0010 adds: or the callee's returned `S` holds `Frame`/`Unknown`). Golden tests: `make_pair(s) -> Result<(Arena, [i32]), i32>` and `try_wrap(s) -> Option<Box<W>>` give `S` with the call's `Frame` (today `S={Param(1)}`); `make_vec(s: [i32]) -> Vec<[i32]>` gives `S={Frame(..)}` without `Param(0)`; `Vec.new_in(&a)` keeps `S=O(a)`; a tied `"lit"` puts no `Static` in `S`. Golden tests: with `fn wrap(s: [i32]) -> Box<W> { return box W { s: s }; }` and `let b = wrap(v.as_slice()); let x = (*b).s;`, `x` carries the view's `Frame` origins (today `x O={} S={}`, and `*out = x` is refused only by the buffer-escape rule); `(*b).s = arena_slice` on `let mut b = box S { s: "lit" }` grows `O(b)` as a projected write and puts no `Static` in the destination memory; a by-value `mut b: Box<S>` parameter written as `(*b).s = ...` puts no `Param(0)` in it.
    - **Rules** (design note §3): a store through a reference, into a closure capture or into a global, whose destination origins contain anything but this frame's `Frame` origins, accepts no value with a `Frame` or `Unknown` origin. A return carries no `Frame` or `Unknown` origin. A call applies the store rule to each `i -> j` of the callee's existing store summary, `j` equal to `i` included; an unknown call (`CallPtr` or `storage_known` false) checks every argument (plus `os_through(O(callee))` for `CallPtr`) against every other, itself included.
    - **Flag:** `--provenance` adds the rules to the old ones (it never removes a rejection), and `--provenance=warn` prints what the new rules refuse and the old ones accept, as warnings that do not fail the compile. It is off by default; plumb it like `--checked` (`src/main.zrl` `Options`) and through `zbuild`, and document it in `zoril --help` and the man page (`make man`).
    - **Probes:** run every safety probe with the flag. Record which open gaps close under `--provenance` (expected: `gap_11`, `gap_13`, `gap_16`, `gap_18`, `gap_21`, `gap_23`, `gap_26`, `gap_29`, `gap_32`, `gap_36`, `gap_40`, `gap_41`, `gap_42`, `gap_45`, `gap_47`, `gap_48`; their `known_gap` headers stay until Z-0012 makes the flag the default) and list what remains for Z-0010 (`gap_07`, `gap_27`, `gap_28`, `gap_33`). Run the store grid with the flag (`ZORIL_CC` wrapper) and record newly rejected unsafe cells and newly refused twins.
    - **Verification:** `make test` with the default unchanged (0 newly accepted and 0 newly refused without `--provenance`), `make preflight-changed`, the probes and the grid with the flag. Stop for review.
    - **Implemented (2026-11-04, git:896eeaf):** all seven transfer fixes and the rules, in `src/check_zir.zrl`. Fix 1: `os_owns` counts `Box`/`BoxMut`, a type with a `T::drop` function, `Vec`/`String`/`ByteVec`/`HashMap` and aggregates of them; raw pointers own nothing (`box_cast.zrl` now `S={}`; `origins/lexer_view_no_frame.zrl`). Fix 2: `FuncAddr` copies `O(env)`, `CallPtr` passes the environment as argument `args.len` (`origins/closure_env_gap11.zrl`, `origins/closure_env_gap45.zrl`, `provenance/closure_env_safe_twin.zrl`). Fix 3: a bare `extern` is known: `{Static}` unless `#[borrows]` ties it, stores only from `#[stores]` (`origins/extern_unannotated_static.zrl`). Fix 4: `os_dest_reach` (`OS_Q_HANDLE`: `&mut`, `[mut T]`, `fn` are leaves) for every check (`provenance/dest_reach_{vec_push,set_field,set_box}_twin.zrl` accepted, `provenance/dest_reach_write_slot.zrl` refused, `EXPECT: call store rule`). Fix 5: `os_arg` reads `O` and `S` apart; store effects grow `S` by the stored arguments' `S` only (`origins/vec_push_lit_no_static_s.zrl`). Fix 6: `os_walk` steps into a box as a projection (`origins/box_wrap_read.zrl`, `origins/box_projected_write.zrl`, `origins/box_byval_param_write.zrl`). Fix 7: `OS_Q_ARENA` ties add to `S`; `owner` when `OS_Q_FRESH` or no arena tie (`origins/call_result_{make_pair,try_wrap,make_vec,tied_lit}.zrl`; `Vec.new_in(&a)` keeps `vec_new_in_local.zrl`). The lexer and push goldens fail on the base compiler (checked by stashing the change: `t O={Param(1), Frame(lex)}`, `S={Frame(_1), Static}`).
    - **Rules:** after the fixpoint, `os_pass` runs once more with `c.checking` (no set changes): the store rule in `os_check_store` (a crossed write with an external `os_dest_reach(OS_T_DM)`, or a write into an owned capture), the return rule at `Ret`, the call store rule in `os_summary_store_effects` (every `from` bit against every `to` bit, `i -> i` included, bit 63 a global), and the unknown-call rule in `os_unknown_call_effects`. Diagnostics are `provenance: ... (<rule> rule)`; `--provenance` sets `had_error`, `=warn` prints `warning:` and does not. `--provenance=warn` prints every refusal of the new rules; when the old rules accept the program the compile succeeds, so its warnings are exactly what the new rules refuse and the old accept. `scripts/check_store_grid.sh` counts `provenance: ` as a safety diagnostic.
    - **Decisions for the reviewer (not in the task text):** (1) an `unsafe fn` body is not checked (`src/driver/module.zrl`): `mmap_or_trap` and `arena_init_result` return `@ptr_from_int` (`Unknown`) and otherwise fail every program; recorded in design note §3 _Where they apply_. (2) `OS_Q_ARENA` counts `ArenaHeader` as an arena (`Vec.new_in_raw(arena)`; without it `281_raw_arena_buffer_returned` is refused). (3) A `fn` value of `Static` origin adds no environment memory (without it `232_reference_route_census` is refused at `f(&x)`). (4) The unknown-call effects add `Unknown` to the targets' `O`, not their `S`, so a call does not make its own arguments' storage external.
    - **Probes under `--provenance`:** closed: `gap_11`, `gap_13`, `gap_16`, `gap_18`, `gap_21`, `gap_23`, `gap_26`, `gap_29`, `gap_32`, `gap_36`, `gap_40`, `gap_41`, `gap_42`, `gap_45`, `gap_47`, `gap_48` (all expected), and also `gap_27` and `gap_28`, refused because the closure call and the recursive cycle are `Unknown` (Z-0010 should keep them closed by the summary instead). Still open for Z-0010: `gap_07`, `gap_33`, `gap_49`, `gap_50`, `gap_51`; liveness (Z-0044/Z-0011): `gap_37`, `gap_38`, `gap_39`, `gap_43`, `gap_44`. Newly refused non-gap probes (six: five `accept` and `25_stack_overflow`, a `runtime_fault`): `213_closure_capture_multiple_drop` (a `u64` closure-call result is `Unknown`, return rule), `25_stack_overflow` (recursion `Unknown`, return rule), `356_store_callee_deref_param_arena_accepted` and `357_store_callee_deref_static_slice_accepted` (call store rule: the unpaired mask does not say whether `copy_into` stores `src` or `*src`, so the value includes `Frame(local)` of `&local`; Z-0010's `ParamAddr`/`ParamContents`), `375_closure_view_used_dropped_in_call_accepted` and `376_closure_copy_element_stored_out_accepted` (unknown-call rule: the call's own effects put `Unknown` into the captured values, which `os_through(O(callee))` then reads as an external destination; for Z-0049's Decision 3 count). - [x] +audit +safety +AI-D Z-0046 Find why the interface build refuses 35 store-grid cells the whole-program build accepts
    > **Created:** 2026-11-03 **Diagnosis, not a fix.** Found by Z-0042's comparison. **Before:** Z-0010 and Z-0057, so the `.zri` summary design acts on the answer.
    > **Completed:** 2026-11-04 git:bee3600

    - **Question:** when a callee is compiled to an interface (`-c --write-interface`) and its caller reads that interface (`--iface-dir`), 35 of the 318 two-module cells are refused that the whole-program build accepts. Is the interface path's behaviour the right one, which the whole-program build should share, or is it an over-rejection, or does it cover only a part of what the whole-program path covers? Answer it before anyone changes either path.
    - **The cells,** all with the `direct` carrier and the `frame_ends` invalidation (`<source>__direct__<path>__<destination>__frame_ends`): 30 with the `string` source, for each of the paths `helper`, `two_level_helper`, `fn_val`, `mutual_rec` and `param_65` and each of the destinations `call_struct_mut`, `closure_call_mut`, `closure_capture_mut`, `closure_owned_capture`, `local_field_assigned_out` and `ten_copies`; the `string` source with `deref_out` and each of `same_param`, `store_mutual_rec` and `store_param_65`; and `store_mutual_rec` into `deref_out` with the `arena_vec` and `local_vec` sources. The first to look at: `string__direct__fn_val__call_struct_mut__frame_ends`.

Two-module (path functions in `pathmod.zrl` compiled with `-c --interproc-summary --write-interface`, the cell compiled with `-c --interproc-summary --iface-dir`) is refused: "region escape: callee stashes local stack pointer into global memory". The cell stores into `out` through `get_struct_slot(out)`, a local struct, not into global memory, so the message does not describe the cell.
    - **What running them shows:** 34 of the 35 cells, built whole-program, run and exit 42: the stored string or vector is still intact, because collections are not freed at scope end. Only `arena_vec__direct__store_mutual_rec__deref_out__frame_ends` reads clobbered memory (exit 77), the shape of `gap_28`. So for 34 cells the interface path refuses a program that works today, and would be the one that stays correct once the `Box` and collection drop series makes frees real.
    - **To establish:** (1) which summary field differs between the two paths for this cell (compare `pathmod`'s interface with what the whole-program pass computes for `forward_fn_target`); (2) whether an interface that omits a field reads as "unknown, so strict", which would explain a stricter result for any callee compiled separately; (3) why the `string` source is so prominent, when `arena_vec` and `local_vec` cells with the same paths are refused by both builds; (4) whether the one `arena_vec` cell is `gap_28` showing in one path only. Record each finding on this task as it is made.
    - **Done when:** the task states whether the interface path is right, over-strict, or incomplete, with the evidence, and names the task that acts on it (`Z-0010` or `Z-0057`). No compiler change in this task.
    - **Answer (2026-11-04):** the 35 refusals are not the interface path's. 32 are an artefact of `scripts/check_store_grid_modular.sh`, which is over-strict for the wrong reason; 3 (`store_mutual_rec`) are the interface path being right where the whole-program build misses `gap_28`'s shape. Acting tasks: Z-0057 (the harness, and the 32 cells it hides), Z-0010 (the 3).
    - **(1) and (2), the summary that differs is not `pathmod`'s.** `forward_fn_target`'s `.zri` (`escape_via = 2, storage = 1`) plays no part: the first cell is refused by `-c --interproc-summary` with no interface at all (`./build/zoril -c -I stdlib=stdlib -I build/z46/string__direct__fn_val__call_struct_mut__frame_ends --interproc-summary -o /dev/null .../unsafe.zrl`), and accepted by `-c` without `--interproc-summary`, with or without `--iface-dir`. The two errors are at `s.push_u8(42 as u8)` and `s.as_slice()`, not at the store: the harness gives an interface only for `pathmod`, so `string.zrl` is read from source and its non-generic methods have no body in the TU and no `#[summary]`, and `InterprocSummaryPass.apply_conservative_defaults` (`src/summary_pass.zrl`, `is_zoril_extern`) gives each "stashes every pointer parameter", so `&mut s` reads as a stack pointer stashed into global memory. So yes, an import without a summary reads as unknown and strict (the right default), and the harness feeds it one. Minimal reproducer, refused by `-c --interproc-summary`, accepted whole-program and by `zbuild`: `import "string.zrl"; use string::String; pub fn t() -> i32 { let mut s = String.new(); s.push_u8(42 as u8); return s.len() as i32; } fn main() -> i32 { return t() - 1; }`. (`stdlib/string.zrl` itself is refused the same way when compiled with `-c --interproc-summary` and no `vec.zri`, at line 214.)
    - **(3) why `string`:** `String`'s methods are non-generic functions of another module, so in `-c` they get the defaults; `Vec`'s are generic, so `arena_vec` and `local_vec` cells instantiate them with bodies in the TU and see the real summary. The `string` twins store a literal and call no `String` method, so the artefact never reaches `bench/fuzz/store_grid_modular_twin_rejections.txt` (0 modular-only twin refusals). Every other `string` cell is refused by both builds for a real reason, so only these 32 show.
    - **What the user's path does:** `zbuild -I stdlib=stdlib -I <cell> --cache-dir build/z46zb -o ... unsafe.zrl` on the 35: 32 build and exit 42, like the whole-program build; the 3 `store_mutual_rec` cells are refused (`cannot store 'v'` / `'s'`). The harness compile with `zbuild`'s stdlib `.zri` files added to `--iface-dir` gives exactly the same split (32 accepted, the same 3 refused), so the harness, not the interface path, is what differs. The 32 are unsafe cells the separately compiled build misses as the whole-program build does (holes in both, `gap_07`/`gap_33`/closure and `fn`-value shapes), so `bench/fuzz/store_grid_modular_expected_failures.txt` understates the interface path's misses by 32.
    - **(4) the `store_mutual_rec` cells:** `pathmod.zrl` (`stash_a` stores `v` into `out` and calls `stash_b`, which calls `stash_a`) compiled alone writes `stores = 4, into = 1` for both functions (the ZIL verifier's summary, written after the module's bodies). Whole-program, the ZIR store rule reads each callee's per-function storage walk at the call: a caller of `stash_a` is refused and a caller of `stash_b` is accepted (checked by editing the cell's call), so the whole-program build misses it through `stash_b`, `gap_28`'s shape (exit 77 for `arena_vec`, 42 for `local_vec`/`string`). Here the interface path is right and the whole-program build is incomplete.
    - **Next:** Z-0057 gives the modular harness interfaces for the standard-library modules (as `zbuild` does, or compiles each cell through `zbuild`), re-records `store_grid_modular_expected_failures.txt` (32 more failing cells expected) and only then compares the modular grid under `--provenance`. Z-0010's SCC fixpoint closes the 3 `store_mutual_rec` cells whole-program, after which both builds refuse them. No compiler change in this task; scratch files were under `build/z46*`.

    > **Created:** 2026-11-03 **Depends:** Z-0008, Z-0048, Z-0046. **Check-in:** stop for review after it. **Split:** the per-body summary here (B2.1a); the SCC fixpoint and deferred call checks in Z-0059 (B2.1b); pairwise `.zri` serialization in Z-0057 (B2.2). The title predates both splits.

    - **Build:** see _B2.1a, this task now_ below. `ParamAddr(i)` is the parameter's own slot (feeding `ret_storage`: e.g. `&c.n`) and `ParamContents(i)` the memory reached through parameter `i` (feeding `ret_ties`: e.g. `c.v` in `take_ref`).
    - **`ret_fresh` (design note §3, _The summary_; Z-0055 review E):** the summary records whether the callee's `S(ret)` holds a `Frame` or `Unknown` origin, and `os_call` (Z-0048 fix 7) adds the call's own `Frame` to the result's `S` when it does. Once ties come from bodies, `Vec.with_capacity(cap: usize)` is tied to `cap` (it stores it), so without this bit `Vec::<i32>.with_capacity(n)` would give `S={Param(0)}` instead of `Frame`; golden tests: that call, and `fn f(a: &Arena) -> Vec<i32> { return Vec.new(); }` giving its caller a fresh `Frame` in `S`.
    - **Expected to close (under `--provenance`, whole-program, when the callee is declared before the caller):** `gap_07_path_same_param` (`0 -> 0`), `gap_33_param_65_arena_slice_store` (the overflow bit), `gap_49_call_ret_mutref_callee_write`, `gap_50_call_ret_struct_callee_write_slot` and `gap_51_call_ret_struct_field_callee_write` (folded in from Z-0058: the store inside `stash` is legal, so the refusal is at the caller and needs `stash`'s summary from the origin sets, where `d = unwrap_slot(h)` already has `O={Param(0)}`), and `gap_52_closure_passed_to_caller_of_fn_value` (found in Z-0048's review: `call_it`'s call through its `fn` parameter records `ParamContents(0) -> ParamContents(0)`; its siblings through two callers, a method taking a `fn` and a `let`-bound closure are on Z-0048).     - **Split (2026-11-04), before any code:** the order-independent fixpoint needs more than one session. Lowering follows declaration order (`Driver.lower_module`), so a caller's body is checked before a callee declared later has a summary: 14 of the 14 `unknown-call` sites in `--emit-origins src/main.zrl` (`compile`, `write_zil`, `parse_program`, `verify_module`, `check_module`, `fmt_main`, `ZilPrinter::emit_module`, `write_interface_file` twice, ...) are callees not yet lowered, most of them not recursive. Removing them means running the call checks after every body is lowered, from a compact record per function, because each body's ZIR is gone by then. So this task (B2.1a) keeps the summary itself and Z-0059 (B2.1b) takes the deferred SCC fixpoint and the deferred checks. **Premise corrected in review (2026-11-04):** lowering is not in declaration order for these calls (a body is lowered on demand, callee first: `fn a() { b() } fn b()` dumps `b` before `a`), and the 14 sites were callees whose old masks had `storage_known = false`, spread from recursive cycles; with body summaries `src/main.zrl` has 0.     - **Implemented (2026-11-04, git:bfb5cef):** as planned, with these differences: the edges are one row per stored value (code, targets' `ParamAddr` bits, `ParamContents` bits, global bit) rather than one entry per edge; the summary is written by `store_origin_summary` from the driver (so `compute_origins_zir` now declares `#[borrows(table)]`, which its body proves: the sets hold nothing of `module`); an `unsafe fn` writes none (its callers keep the old masks, as Z-0048 exempts its body); `--emit-origins` prints the result's `S` and a `store` line per edge row. Six transfer rules were needed, each found by a probe or golden and recorded in design note §3 _The summary as built_: a `ParamContents` target is the argument's destination reach (else `v.push(arena_str)` after `v.push("lit")` is refused, `provenance/dest_reach_vec_push_twin.zrl`); an edge never puts a local's own storage into what it holds (else every `return v` of a pushed-to `Vec` is refused); unknown-call edges carry what the arguments reach, not their synthetic `Unknown` (else every closure handed to a function is refused); integer results ignore `ParamContents` ties (else `return v.len()` is refused, `origins/ret_int_contents_untied.zrl`); under a body summary an arena tie adds the arena argument's own origins to `S` (else the `Static` of an arena's blocks makes every `Vec` in a local arena external); a slice's `len` holds no origins (else `307_clone_is_independent` is refused through `String.push_u64`'s `extend_from_slice`).
