- [x] +safety +tests +AI-D Z-0059 B2.1b Deferred call checks over an SCC fixpoint of compact per-function records, under `--provenance`
    > **Created:** 2026-11-04 **Split from:** Z-0010. **Depends:** Z-0010. **Check-in:** stop for review after it.
    > **Completed:** 2026-11-04 git:ce656b1
    > **Title predates the decision (2026-11-04):** no compact records are built; the cycle members' ZIR is kept (see _Decided_ below).

    - **Why:** Z-0010 computes each function's summary from its body at lowering time, and a callee is lowered on demand, before its caller, so only a call inside a recursive cycle reaches a callee with no summary yet: it falls back to the old masks, whose `storage_known = false` spreads `Unknown` (a mutual pair `g`/`h` shows one `unknown-call`; `src/main.zrl` shows none at `bfb5cef`). The fix is to check calls only after every summary in a cycle is final.
    - **Design to decide first and record here (design note §3, _The summary_):** what the compact record holds, so the summaries can be iterated without the ZIR. Proposed: per call site, the callee and, for each argument, its origin set restricted to `ParamAddr`/`ParamContents`/flags plus a symbolic `Result(k)` origin for each earlier call site's result; per function, the same restriction of `O(ret)`, `S(ret)` and of each store's value and target; the span of each call and store for the deferred checks. Measure the record's size on `src/main.zrl` (all modules) before building it; if it is too large, record the alternative (keeping each function's ZIR in the main arena, or re-lowering) with its cost.
    - **Build (as decided below):** find each recursive cycle on the lowering stack (Tarjan's low-links), keep its bodies' ZIR until its root finishes, iterate their summaries from empty to a fixpoint (the sets only grow), then run `--provenance`'s rules on each body once with the final summaries. The in-body store and return rules stay where Z-0048 put them; only rules that read a callee summary move.
    - **Expected to close (under `--provenance`, whole-program):** `gap_28_call_summary_mutual_recursion` and `gap_27` for the summary reason; 0 `unknown-call` sites from calls inside a recursive cycle (`--emit-origins` on a mutual pair `g`/`h` shows one today; `src/main.zrl` shows none at `bfb5cef`). No case of Z-0010's list is left over from declaration order: a callee is lowered on demand, before its caller.
    - **From Z-0046:** the store grid's `store_mutual_rec` cells into `deref_out` (`arena_vec`, `local_vec`, `string`) are accepted whole-program through `stash_b` and refused through `stash_a` (`arena_vec` exits 77 through `stash_b`); the `.zri` of `pathmod` gives both `stores = 4, into = 1`, so the separate build refuses them. The fixpoint must make the whole-program build refuse them too.
    - **Verification:** `make test` with the default unchanged, `make preflight-changed`, the probes and the whole-program store grid under `--provenance`, compile time and peak memory of `src/main.zrl` with and without the flag. Stop for review.
    - **Measured (2026-11-04, at `7dce176`; scratch worktree under `build/z59/`, dump widened to every module, removed after):** compiling `src/main.zrl` lowers 2112 bodies; 173 have `unknown-call` sites, 534 in all, of which 5 go through `fn` values and 529 call a function still being lowered (a recursive cycle: `ZirLowerer::lower_expr` 67, `Printer::expr` 40, `collect_free_idents_expr` 33, `InterfacePrinter::emit_expr_prec` 33, `Parser::parse_expr` 16, ...). About 192 functions are cycle members (callers and callees of those sites). `tools/zbuild/src/main.zrl`: 430 bodies, none. The "`src/main.zrl` shows none" above is true only of `--emit-origins`' dump, which prints the target file's own 22 functions. ZIR size, as the scratch arena's growth across `lower_function`: 101.8 MB over all bodies (largest `ZilPrinter::emit_instruction`, 0.8 MB), 21.7 MB over the 192 cycle members; peak RSS of the compile 581 MB.
    - **Decided (2026-11-04): keep the members' ZIR, not a compact record.** A record restricted to `ParamAddr`/`ParamContents`/flags cannot replay a cycle callee's effect on a local of this frame that the body then stores out (`let mut w = ..; g(&mut w, p); *out = w.s;` with `g` in the cycle): the flow goes through `Frame(w)`, which the restriction drops, so such a record is either unsound or the ZIR itself. Keeping the ZIR costs about 22 MB (under 4% of peak) and needs no copy: lowering nests (a callee is lowered inside its caller's `lower_function`, on a bump arena), so a member's ZIR stays valid if its `run_body_passes` skips the scratch reset until the cycle's root finishes, whose own reset frees them all. Design: a lowering stack in the driver, with each function's depth while it is being lowered; a call to a function on the stack with no summary yet takes an empty summary and lowers the caller's low-link (Tarjan, on the fly); a body whose low-link is below its depth is a member: its rules are deferred and its ZIR kept; when the root (low-link equal to its depth) finishes, the members and the root are recomputed from empty summaries until no summary changes (each new summary joined with the last, so they only grow), then each is checked once with the final summaries, and `--emit-origins` prints members then. Callers outside the cycle are lowered after the root and read the final summaries.
    - **Implemented (2026-11-04, git:cd89350):** as decided. `ILFunction.os_depth` marks a body on the lowering stack; `Driver.os_low` holds the low-links, `Driver.os_pending` the cycle's bodies (`run_body_passes` pushes and pops, skips a member's scratch reset, and calls `os_fixpoint` at the root); `check_zir::zir_open_call_depth` pre-scans a body for direct calls to a body still on the stack with no summary, so its rules are deferred before its origin pass; `os_call` gives such a callee an empty summary (plus a declared `#[borrows]`); `store_origin_summary` joins with the function's existing summary and returns whether it grew. An `unsafe fn` in a cycle is iterated but still writes no summary. Design note §3 _The summary as built_ describes it. `scripts/comment_budget.tsv`: only `src/driver/state.zrl`'s row re-recorded, for a `// SAFETY:` line.
    - **Results (2026-11-04, at cd89350):** compiling `src/main.zrl` with every module dumped (scratch worktree, removed after): `unknown-call` sites 534 in 173 bodies before, 5 in 3 bodies now, all 5 through `fn` values. A mutual pair `g`/`h` shows none. Probes under `--provenance`: `gap_28` is refused by its summary (`stash_a` and `stash_b` both record `Param(1) -> Param(0)`, no `unknown-call`), `gap_27` by the in-body store rule (it prints three diagnostics with the flag: the store rule, the call store rule, and an unknown-call one for its `fn` value); `25_stack_overflow` (recursion) is now accepted; nothing else changes. Store grid under the flag (record files copied under `build/z59/grid`): whole-program 0 unsafe cells accepted (unchanged), safe twins refused 19286 → 17216 (`mutual_rec` 3300 → 1335, now level with `helper`/`direct`/the methods; `store_mutual_rec` 131 → 26, level with `store_param_65`). The modular grid now runs under the flag (the `mutual_rec` callee module is no longer refused): of 318 two-module cells, 14 unsafe cells accepted (`same_param`, `store_param_65` and `param_65` shapes, which need Z-0057's pairwise `.zri` edges) and 110 twins refused; its `--record` wrote the committed `bench/fuzz/store_grid_modular_*.txt`, restored afterwards (copies of the flag's results under `build/z59/grid`). Compile of `src/main.zrl`, best of three: 3212 ms and 587 MB peak RSS without the flag, 3323 ms (+3.5%) and 587 MB with `--provenance=warn`; peak before this change 581 MB (the kept ZIR costs about 6 MB, not the 22 MB estimate, since each cycle's is freed at its root).
    - **Verification (2026-11-04, at cd89350):** `make test` 3267 run, 3267 passed, 0 failed, 62 skipped; `make preflight-changed` passed: 38 of 38 targeted mutations caught, self-hosting fixpoint identical, rejection differential 0 newly accepted and 0 newly refused of 69110 programs against `55d5e99`, suite passes under `--checked`. A first `make test` failed only on formatting, the §5 figures and `state.zrl`'s comment budget, all fixed before the commit. Benchmark files written under `bench/` deleted. `bench/fuzz/README.md` stays stale for `src/`, `tools/zilc/`, `tools/zbuild/` and `rt/`.

Only `extend_from_slice` was probed; the others copy scalars or bytes (`intern` takes a `[u8]` it copies by value), unprobed otherwise. Already declared: `Vec.push`, `Vec.clone_in`, `ZirAnalysisState.clone`, `append_projection`.

With `let buf = [42 as u8; 16];` in `fill`, `fn clobber_stack() -> u8 { let arr = [77 as u8; 64]; return arr[0]; }` called after `fill(&mut v)`, and `return v[0][0] as i32 + (c as i32) * 0;`, it builds and exits **77** (the stale read). Open sibling: `let mut views = Vec::<[u8]>.new(); views.push(s); out.extend_from_slice(views.as_slice());` is accepted under `--provenance`, while `out.push(views[0])` is refused by default. `HashMap.insert` (`#[stores(key -> self, value -> self)]`) and `Vec.set` are refused; the other `memcpy` users listed above copy within `self` or copy bytes. Task: Z-0060.

(By default the declaration is refused: "#[stores] names a parameter this body does not store".) Z-0011 refuses declarations narrower than the body; here the body proves nothing, because the copy goes through `memcpy`.

Same root as class 3: Z-0063. Not measured at all: a generic stdlib function no root instantiates is never checked whole-program.

Z-0064 re-measures after the class fixes and reads every remaining warning.

- [x] +safety +tests +AI-D Z-0060 B1.4 `Vec.extend_from_slice` stores frame views its summary does not record
    > **Created:** 2026-11-05 **Found by:** Z-0049 (Lead L) and its review. **Check-in:** stop for review after it. A hole open in both modes.
    > **Completed:** 2026-11-05 git:e280340

    - **Now:** `Vec<T>.extend_from_slice(self, slice: [T])` (`stdlib/vec.zrl`) copies `T` elements with `memcpy` and declares no `#[stores]`, so neither the old rules nor `--provenance` see that the elements of `slice` are stored into `self`. Reproducer (builds, exits 77 by default and under `--provenance`):

    ```zoril
    import "vec.zrl";
    use vec::Vec;
    fn fill(out: &mut Vec<[u8]>) {
        let buf = [42 as u8; 16];
        let s: [u8] = &buf;
        let views = [s];
        out.extend_from_slice(&views);
    }
    fn clobber_stack() -> u8 { let arr = [77 as u8; 64]; return arr[0]; }
    fn main() -> i32 {
        let mut v: Vec<[u8]> = Vec.new();
        fill(&mut v);
        let c = clobber_stack();
        return v[0][0] as i32 + (c as i32) * 0;
    }
    ```

    - **First, the probe:** add the program above as a `known_gap` probe, with a Known gaps entry in `doc/spec/safety_model.md` and `doc/SAFETY.md` §6. Route class: a safe body copies reference-carrying elements through an `extern` with no declared effect. Also probe the sibling from a local `Vec` (`views.push(s); out.extend_from_slice(views.as_slice());`, accepted under `--provenance`).
    - **Do not** fix it with `#[stores(slice -> self)]`: that declares the view itself stored, and `extend_from_slice(&stack_bytes)` on a `Vec<u8>` is then refused under `--provenance` (Z-0049's review, correction 3). Fix it so the body shows the element store: an element loop through `push`/`set`, which declare `#[stores(val -> self)]`, or a `ParamContents(src) -> ParamContents(dst)` effect for `memcpy` (Z-0010's edge format expresses it; an attribute syntax for it would be new). Choose with a measurement: the loop costs speed on `Vec<u8>` byte copies, which `src/` does often.
    - **Must stay accepted:** `extend_from_slice(&stack_bytes)` on `Vec<u8>`, and a `Vec<[u8]>` extended with views of a caller's arena. Flip the probe to `compile_reject` in both modes; add the accepted twins as `accept` probes. Take the fix out and watch the probe exit 77 again.
    - **Verification:** `sh scripts/run_safety_matrix.sh`, `make test`, `make preflight-changed` (rejection differential: it only refuses more, so list the newly refused programs). The fuzz record goes stale for `stdlib/`. Stop for review.
    - **Probe (2026-11-05, `0ea9094`):** `gap_56_extern_copy_extend_from_slice.zrl` (`known_gap`, exit 77 by default and under `--provenance`), Known gaps entry, new class (I) in `doc/SAFETY.md` §6. The `Vec`-source sibling exits 77 too (its `fill` frame is larger, so it needs a `[77 as u8; 512]` clobber); same callee and route class, so it became a `compile_reject` probe with the fix instead of a second `known_gap`.
    - **Fix (2026-11-05, `8da6821`):** `extend_from_slice` writes each element as a `T` (`*((dest + i * sizeof(T)) as *mut T) = slice[i]`, the raw write `push` uses, with no capacity check per element) instead of one `memcpy`. The `// SAFETY:` line moved into the loop with its text unchanged (the gate requires it directly above the block). Alternatives ruled out: (1) a `push` loop: the same summary, plus a capacity check per element; (2) `#[stores(slice -> self)]`: refuses `extend_from_slice(&stack_bytes)` (Z-0049's review); (3) a declared effect on the `memcpy` extern: `#[stores]` can only say "`p`'s own storage into `q`" (`doc/spec/surface_syntax.md`), there is no element-level form, and it would apply to every `memcpy` in the tree, including `push`'s grow copy into a fresh allocation, which the origin pass reads as `Static` (a store into a global).
    - **Result:** under `--provenance`, `gap_56` and the sibling are refused (call store rule). By default the sibling is refused too ("cannot store 'views' ..."), but **`gap_56` stays accepted by default**, so it stays `known_gap`, against this task's "flip in both modes": the old rules track a pointer into the frame one level deep and see `&views`, not the frame views inside it; even a push loop with `#[stores(s -> v)]` declared is accepted by default (prototype under `build/rv/z60/`). Making the old rules see it means extending the rules Z-0012 deletes; `gap_56` flips with the default switch, so it is added to Z-0065's probe list. New probes: `418_extend_from_slice_vec_frame_views_rejected.zrl` (the sibling; built against the old `stdlib/vec.zrl` it compiles and exits 77, so taking the fix out reopens it), `419_extend_from_slice_stack_bytes_accepted.zrl` and `420_extend_from_slice_caller_arena_views_accepted.zrl` (accepted in both modes, exit 0).
    - **Precision bound, not new:** `extend_from_slice(w.as_slice())` from a local `Vec<[u8]>` holding only string literals is now refused in both modes, a safe program. It is the precision `push` already has: `out.push(w[0])` and a `push` loop over `w.as_slice()` are refused on the base compiler in both modes (a read through a view of a local collection's storage keeps the collection's `Frame`). No program in the tree or the tests takes this shape (the differential below). Byte copies from local collections (`v.extend_from_slice(w.as_slice())` on `Vec<u8>`, `out.push_str(t.as_slice())`) stay accepted.
    - **Cost:** compiling `src/main.zrl` with the compiler built from each `Vec`, 7 runs each: medians 3445 ms (base) and 3425 ms (fix), noise. Worst case, 40 MB of 4 KB byte chunks through `extend_from_slice`: 48 ms (`memcpy`) against 60 ms (loop). `--provenance=warn` counts unchanged: `src/main.zrl` 489, `zilc` 57, `zbuild` 48, `zman` 41.
    - **Checks:** `make test` at `8da6821`: 3279 run, 3279 passed, 0 failed, 68 skipped. `sh scripts/run_safety_matrix.sh`: 0 mismatches (`gap_56` `[SKIP]`). `PREFLIGHT_BASE=720980a make preflight-changed`: exit 0, clean-tree build and self-hosting fixpoint identical, checked suite 1194/1194; **two steps ran nothing:** no mutation targets `stdlib/vec.zrl`, and the rejection differential was skipped because its trigger list names only `src/` files, although a stdlib summary change alters what the checkers refuse (a gap in the gate, not fixed here). Run by hand, `scripts/check_reject_diff.sh 720980a`: 0 newly accepted, 2 newly refused (`418`, whole-program and `zbuild`) of 69116 programs. The benchmark files the preflight wrote under `bench/` were deleted. `bench/fuzz/README.md` is stale for `stdlib/`.
    - **Awaiting review:** re-run `gap_56` with and without `--provenance` (`timeout 30 ./build/zoril -I stdlib=stdlib [--provenance] --emit-zil -o /dev/null bench/safety_matrix/probes/gap_56_extern_copy_extend_from_slice.zrl`: accepted by default, refused with the flag), `418` (refused in both), `419` and `420` (`scripts/build_zrl.sh build/p419 bench/safety_matrix/probes/419_extend_from_slice_stack_bytes_accepted.zrl -I stdlib=stdlib && ./build/p419`, exit 0); take the fix out (`git show 0ea9094:stdlib/vec.zrl` into a copy of `stdlib/`, `-I stdlib=<copy>`) and watch `418` build and exit 77; `sh scripts/run_safety_matrix.sh`; `scripts/check_reject_diff.sh 720980a`. Judge: keeping `gap_56` open by default until Z-0065, and the literal-views precision bound above. Siblings to probe: other safe functions that copy through `memcpy` or another bare extern (`Vec.clone_in`, which declares `#[borrows(self, a)]`; `String` methods; `bufio`), and `extend_from_slice` reached through a `fn` value or a trait.
    - **Review and close (2026-11-05, by a second model that wrote none of the fix):** every claim verified, reproducers re-run, and sibling routes probed. Re-runs: `gap_56` accepted by default and refused under `--provenance` (call store rule); probe `418` refused in both modes (`cannot store 'views'` by default; call store rule under `--provenance`); with `0ea9094:stdlib/vec.zrl`, `418` builds and exits 77; `419` and `420` build and exit 0 in both modes. Safety matrix re-run: 443/476 passed, 0 mismatches (`gap_56` `[SKIP]`). Full rejection differential against `720980a` (`scripts/check_reject_diff.sh 720980a` over 69,116 programs): 0 newly accepted, 2 newly refused (`418`, whole-program and `zbuild`). Sibling probes: `Vec.clone_in` is safe against escapes because it declares `#[borrows(self, a)]` (probed: returning a clone of local collection views is refused in both modes); `String` and `bufio` copy only scalar bytes (`[u8]`); generic trait methods dispatching to `extend_from_slice` behave identically (Vec-source refused in both modes, array-source refused under `--provenance`); function-value calls are caught under `--provenance` by the call store rule. Departures judged sound: (1) keeping `gap_56` open by default until Z-0065 avoids patching aggregate element tracking into `storage_walk_zir` / `summary_pass.zrl`, which Z-0012 deletes; provenance already tracks element origins, so deferring the flip to Z-0065 is the right architectural choice; (2) the string-literal views precision bound (`extend_from_slice(w.as_slice())` from a local `Vec<[u8]>`) matches what `w.push(w[0])` and push loops over `w.as_slice()` already refuse on the base compiler because reading through `w.as_slice()` retains `Frame(w)`. Gates re-checked: `check_safety_model.sh` (78 rows, 77 proven, 33 `known_gap`), `check_safety_comments.sh` (160 stdlib unsafe blocks), `check_comment_noise.sh` (budget met), `tm validate` pass.

- [x] +safety +tests +AI-D Z-0061 B1.5 A `match` binding through a reference scrutinee takes the referent's origins
    > **Created:** 2026-11-05 **Found by:** Z-0049 (class 1, 70 return-rule warnings). **Check-in:** stop for review after it. Over-rejection under `--provenance` only.
    > **Completed:** 2026-11-05 git:106e149

    - **Now:** `fn get_a(self: &E) -> &P { return match self { E.A(x) => x, E.B(_) => ICE("no") }; }` gives `_3 O={Frame(self)}`, `x O={Frame(self)}` and a return-rule warning under `--provenance=warn`. At runtime the result points inside the caller's `E` (Z-0049's review, correction 7). The scrutinee is lowered as the address of the parameter slot `self` (a `&&E`); the payload read crosses only one of the two dereferences. The right origin for `x` is `ParamContents(0)`.
    - **Do:** fix the transfer in `src/check_zir.zrl` so the payload projection reads through every dereference it crosses (design note §3, a read through a reference). Golden test in `tests/language/memory/origins/` (`x O={ParamContents(0)}`), and a must-reject sibling: the same `match` over a local `E` (`let e = ...; match &e { E.A(x) => x, .. }`) returned must still be refused by the return rule. Add a mutant in `scripts/mutation-check.sh` that the golden catches.

    - **Cause (2026-11-05):** `lower_variant_match` (`src/lower_zir/lower_match.zrl`) emits `EnumPayload(scrut_place, scrut_ty, v)` with the reference itself as the place when the scrutinee is a reference, and `enum_address` (`src/lower_zir_to_zil.zrl`) then loads the place's value (for a pointer or a box) rather than taking its address. `os_rvalue` treated `EnumPayload` like `Ref` (`os_addr`, the place's own slot), hence `Frame(self)`.
    - **Fix (`bdd8ee2`):** in `os_rvalue`, an `EnumPayload` whose place has a pointer type gets what reading the place yields (`os_read`, its `S` cleared: an address owns nothing); every other place keeps `os_addr`. Boxes keep the projection rule `os_walk` gives them (a box's sets hold its contents'), so they are unchanged.
    - **Correction to this task's text (and to Z-0049's review, correction 7, which wrote it):** the right origin is `Param(0)`, not `ParamContents(0)`. `--emit-origins` prints `Param(i)` for `ParamAddr(i)`, the memory the argument designates, which for a `&E` parameter is the caller's enum; the caller reads `p O={Frame(e)}`, exact. Z-0049's original "`Param(0)` is right" was correct. Golden: `tests/language/memory/origins/match_through_ref.zrl` (`get_a x O={Param(0)} S={}`; before the fix `x O={Frame(self)}`).
    - **Tests and mutant:** `tests/language/memory/provenance/match_through_ref_accepted.zrl` (refused before the fix) and the must-reject sibling `match_through_local_ref_refused.zrl` (refused before and after, return rule). `M135` in `scripts/mutation-check.sh` (the new branch made `if false`): "caught by: match_through_ref_accepted".
    - **Warn-mode counts after the fix** (`--provenance=warn`, same roots as Z-0049): `src/main.zrl` 489 → 274 (267 call store, 4 return, 3 store), `zilc` 57 → 1 (`stdlib/os/linux.zrl:54`, Z-0063), `zbuild` 48 → 47, `zman` 41 → 41. Every `get_*` accessor warning is gone.
    - **Not this defect: `instance_type_args` and `hashmap.zrl:184`.** Both remain. Their `Unknown` comes from `os_walk`: a `Projection.Index` on a type that is neither an array nor a slice (a `Vec` place, a raw pointer) sets the type to 0, which loses nothing as the last projection, but a field after it takes the lost-type branch and adds `Unknown` (`self.module.types[i]` is clean, `self.module.types[i].def` gives `ParamContents(0), Unknown`; `&self.e[i]` on `e: *mut S` is clean, `&self.e[i].v` gives `Unknown`). The warning wording ("an address of this frame") is Z-0066's. A different route, so not fixed here: Z-0068.
    - **Sibling routes probed (`build/rv/sib61_*.zrl`, untracked):** under `--provenance`, returning a binding of `match r` with `r = &e` a local, `if let E.A(x) = &e`, `match r` with `r = &mut e`, `match &*b` on a local box, `match *r { E.A(x) => &x }` and `return pick(&e)` through an accessor are all refused by the return rule (`match` through a `&&E` does not type-check). **By default, four of them are accepted and exit 77** with a stack clobber, on the base compiler too: a pre-existing hole, found here. Recorded as `gap_57_match_ref_local_enum_binding_returned.zrl` and `gap_58_match_ref_accessor_local_enum_returned.zrl` (`known_gap`, `750c4a6`; Known gaps entries, class (J) in `doc/SAFETY.md` §6; 478 probes, 35 `known_gap`); they close under `--provenance` with or without this fix and flip with the default switch, so they join Z-0065's list.
    - **Checks:** `make test` at `bdd8ee2`: 3285 run, 3285 passed, 0 failed, 72 skipped. `sh scripts/test_origins.sh` and `./scripts/test.sh tests/language/memory/provenance tests/language/memory/origins` (46/46). `PREFLIGHT_BASE=998aafc make preflight-changed`: exit 0; 27 targeted mutations, "All mutations caught"; self-hosting fixpoint identical; checked suite 1197/1197; rejection differential 0 newly accepted, 0 newly refused of 69118 programs (the default is unchanged). The benchmark files it wrote under `bench/` were deleted. `bench/fuzz/README.md` is stale for `src/`.
    - **Awaiting review:** re-run `sh scripts/test_origins.sh`, `./scripts/test.sh tests/language/memory/provenance`, `./scripts/mutation-check.sh M135`, and `timeout 30 ./build/zoril -I stdlib=stdlib --provenance=warn --emit-zil -o /dev/null src/main.zrl -I src -I build/gen 2>&1 | grep -c provenance:` (274); build `gap_57` and `gap_58` (`scripts/build_zrl.sh build/g57 bench/safety_matrix/probes/gap_57_match_ref_local_enum_binding_returned.zrl && ./build/g57`, exit 77) and check both are refused under `--provenance`. Judge: the box case left on `os_addr`, and `os_clear` of `S` for the payload address. Siblings to probe: `?` on a reference (the other `EnumPayload` site, `src/lower_zir/expr.zrl`), `while let` through a reference, nested patterns (`E.A(F.B(y))`) through a reference, a `match` on a reference field (`match &self.f`), and a `&mut` scrutinee written through its binding.
    - **Review and close (2026-11-05, by a second model that wrote none of the fix):** every claim verified, reproducers re-run, design decisions judged, and sibling routes probed. Re-runs: `sh scripts/test_origins.sh` passed 36/36 (including `match_through_ref.zrl`); `./scripts/test.sh tests/language/memory/provenance` passed 10/10 (including `match_through_ref_accepted.zrl` and `match_through_local_ref_refused.zrl`); `./scripts/mutation-check.sh M135` caught by `match_through_ref_accepted`; warn-mode count on `src/main.zrl` is exactly 274 (down from 489, all 70 accessor warnings and cascades resolved). Reprobers `gap_57` and `gap_58` build and exit 77 by default, and both are refused under `--provenance` by the return rule. Design judgments: (1) Leaving boxes on `os_addr` is sound: `os_walk` does not set `walk_crossed = true` for boxes, so `os_addr` yields `Frame(base)` for local boxes and box parameters, so the return rule refuses a reference into a dropped box that would escape the frame; references to boxes (`&Box<E>`) have pointer tags and correctly take the pointer branch. (2) Clearing `S` via `os_clear(c, vs)` is correct: `EnumPayload` produces a borrowed pointer to the variant's payload inside the enum, not an owned value; addresses carry memory origins (`O`), never ownership (`S = {}`), so clearing `S` avoids spurious storage-ownership tracking. Sibling routes probed: (1) `?` on a reference parameter derives `Param(0)`, and local frame errors/addresses are refused by the return rule; (2) `while let` through a reference parameter derives `Param(0)`, while returning a binding of `while let` on a local reference is refused; (3) nested patterns `E.A(F.B(y))` through a reference parameter correctly propagate `Param(0)` across both `EnumPayload` projections, and local enum matches are refused; (4) `match &self.f` on an enum field borrows with `Param(0)`, and local reference field matches are refused; (5) `&mut` scrutinees write safely through payload bindings, and attempting to write a frame address into caller memory through a mutable binding is caught and refused by the store rule. Gates re-checked: `scripts/check_safety_model.sh` (78 rows, 77 proven, 35 `known_gap`), `tm validate doc/tasks/*.tm.md` pass.

- [x] +safety +tests +AI-D Z-0062 B1.6 `Vec.push` of an element type that owns storage summarises a store of `Unknown`
    > **Created:** 2026-11-05 **Found by:** Z-0049 (class 2, about 480 warnings, mostly cascades). **Check-in:** stop for review after it. Over-rejection under `--provenance` only.
    > **Completed:** 2026-11-05 git:2720406

    - **Now:** pushing an owning element (`Vec<Vec<u32>>`, `Vec<String>`, `Vec<B>` with `B` holding a `Vec`) into memory reached through a parameter summarises `store Unknown -> Param(0)` and is refused by the call store rule. `Vec<[u8]>.push([u8])` and `Vec<u32>.push(u32)` are clean. The raw arena of Z-0049's first diagnosis is incidental: `self.w.push(Vec::<u32>.new())` warns with no arena (`build/z49/t/d.zrl`, `q3`). Must-accept reproducer (exits 0 by default, refused under `--provenance`):

    ```zoril
    import "vec.zrl";
    import "string.zrl";
    use vec::Vec;
    use string::String;
    fn add(v: &mut Vec<Vec<i32>>) { v.push(Vec::<i32>.new()); }
    fn adds(v: &mut Vec<String>) { v.push(String.new()); }
    fn main() -> i32 { let mut v: Vec<Vec<i32>> = Vec.new(); add(&mut v); let mut w: Vec<String> = Vec.new(); adds(&mut w); return (v.len() + w.len()) as i32 - 2; }
    ```

    - **Diagnose first:** find where the `Unknown` enters `push`'s instance for an owning `T` (its grow path, an `unsafe fn` callee that writes no summary, or the owner's `S` passed as a stored value). `--emit-origins` prints no generic instances: copy the instance into a non-generic test function, or widen the dump in a scratch build. Record the cause on this task before fixing.
    - **Do:** fix the cause, not `push`'s declaration. Must still be refused: pushing a `Vec<[i32]>` holding a frame view into a caller's `Vec<Vec<[i32]>>`. Golden test for the summary edge; the `add`/`adds` program as a provenance-group test; a mutant.
    - **Verification:** as Z-0061, plus the warn-mode count on `src/main.zrl`, `tools/zbuild`, `tools/zman` (expected: the `lower_expr`, `new_block`, `parse_expr` cascades go). Stop for review.
    - **Cause (2026-11-05):** found by copying `push`'s body into a non-generic `push_m(self: &mut MV, val: ELEM)` (`build/rv/z62/mv.zrl`, untracked) with `ELEM` `u32`, `[u8]` and `Vec<u32>`. Only the owning element gives `store Unknown -> ParamContents(0)`: the by-value parameter is seeded `val S={Frame(val)}` (the storage it owns), the in-body edge value is `VO | VS`, and a `Frame` in an edge value is written as `Unknown`. The same happens for a fresh owner stored by a body with no parameter (`fn put(o: &mut Vec<i32>) { let w = Vec::<i32>.new(); *o = w; }` recorded `store Unknown -> Param(0)` although the in-body store rule, which reads `O` only, accepted the store). Two more routes carry the same storage as an address: at a call, `os_param_origin` gives a `ParamAddr` value as `O(arg) | S(arg)` (and a `ParamContents` value reads through both), and the mask path (`os_summary_store_effects`, a declared `#[stores]` such as `push`'s) checks `reach_all(O | S)` and writes it into the target's `O`. So a builder pushing a fresh `Vec` into a local `Vec` and returning it (`v O={Frame(_3)}`) was refused by the return rule too. Not the raw arena of Z-0049's first diagnosis, and not the grow path.
    - **Fix (`59c31a4`):** a value stored by value moves, with the storage it owns. The `Frame` origins in `S` that are not also in `O` (`os_drop_moved_storage`) leave the in-body edge value, the call-site check and propagation of `ParamAddr` and `ParamContents` values (`os_edge_effects`), and the mask path's check, `O` write and edge value; at a call the moved storage joins the target owner's `S` (`os_moved_storage`, written with `os_write_memory`). A `Frame` in both sets stays: a `Vec` in an arena holds `Frame(a)` as an address too. The overflow parameter (63) stays conservative. Rule written into design note §3, _The summary as built_.
    - **Must-reject routes (all refused under `--provenance` after the fix, call store rule):** a `Vec<[i32]>` holding a frame view pushed into a caller's `Vec<Vec<[i32]>>` (the task's case); a `Vec` and a `String` in a local arena pushed; the same through a by-value parameter `put_p`; a `box` holding a frame view pushed; `*out = w` with `w` in a local arena (store rule); a `Vec` in a **by-value `Arena` parameter** pushed (its `O` holds no `Frame` but `Frame(a)`, shared with `S`: the case the both-sets guard is for). Accepted: `add`/`adds`, `put_p` with a heap `Vec`, a builder returning the `Vec` it pushed into (`v O={} S={Frame(_1), Frame(_3)}`).
    - **Holes found (default mode, base compiler too; recorded, not fixed):** `gap_59`, `gap_60`, `gap_61` (`e2241f3`, class (K)): a local `Vec` of frame views pushed into the caller's `Vec`, passed by value to a pushing callee, or wrapped in a struct; `gap_62` (`a6e040a`, class (F) widened): the by-value `Arena` parameter case, which reads freed memory. All exit 77 by default and are refused under `--provenance` with and without this fix; they join Z-0065's list. 482 probes, 39 `known_gap`.
    - **Tests and mutants:** `origins/push_fresh_owner_moved.zrl` (`mk v O={} S={Frame(_1), Frame(_3)}`; before, `v O={Frame(_3)}`), `provenance/push_owning_element_accepted.zrl` (refused before the fix), and the must-reject `push_vec_of_frame_views_refused.zrl`, `push_arena_vec_refused.zrl`, `push_param_arena_vec_refused.zrl`. `M136` (the drop a no-op): caught by `push_owning_element_accepted`. `M137` (every `Frame` in `S` dropped, the guard gone): survived `push_arena_vec_refused` (a local arena's `Vec` also holds the arena's own storage `Frame(_3)` in `O`), which is why `push_param_arena_vec_refused` was added; caught by it.
    - **`--provenance` differential (base `f636db4`'s compiler against the fix, every `.zrl` under `bench/safety_matrix/probes/` and `tests/` except `*_lib.zrl`, script `build/rv/provdiff.sh`, untracked):** one program changes, newly accepted: `tests/stdlib/test_stdlib_clone.zrl` (`Vec.clone` pushes fresh element clones into a local `out` and returns it, the builder shape; exits 0 by default). Nothing newly refused. The script wrote a stray `out.zil` at the root (deleted; it failed `clean-tree` in one `make test` run).
    - **Warn-mode counts** (Z-0049's roots): `src/main.zrl` 274 → 222, `zilc` 1 → 1, `zbuild` 47 → 31, `zman` 41 → 40. All of Z-0049's class 2 reproducers (`build/z49/t/{a,c,d,e}.zrl`) are clean. **The `lower_expr` cascade remains, from other roots:** a scratch compiler dumping every module (`build/rv/zoril.dump`, the root-module filter at `src/driver/module.zrl:1253` and `:1354` removed) lists 94 functions with `store Unknown`; those where a local first carries `Unknown` are `exh_useful`, `fuse_constructor_into`, `resolve_method_type`, `index_functions`, `warn_unreachable_arms`, `lower_plain_call`, `lower_variant_constructor_call` and a few drivers, and the two read are Z-0068's route (`self.blocks[self.current_block_idx].stmts`, `self.module.functions[i].name`: an index into a `Vec`, then a field). `arena_init_result` is Z-0063's.
    - **Harness bug found:** `scripts/mutation-check.sh` keeps `build/mutation/` while the toolchain stamp is unchanged, and a run that exits early (a survivor) leaves `survivors` behind, so the next run reports the old survivor although it caught the mutant (seen with `M137`: "caught by: push_param_arena_vec_refused", then "SURVIVING MUTATIONS ... M137", exit 1). Removing `build/mutation/survivors` gave exit 0. Task: Z-0069.

Benchmark files under `bench/` deleted. `bench/fuzz/README.md` is stale for `src/`. Also in this run: `7d9be53` reworded a banned phrase ("preventing") in Z-0061's review note in `completed.tm.md`, which had made `make test` red on `main`.

Judge: the rule (moved storage is not an escape) against the drop series to come, where a moved owner's buffer is freed by its new owner, not this frame; the both-sets guard; ParamContents dropping the moved storage; the overflow parameter left conservative. Siblings to probe: an owner moved into a global (`static mut` cannot hold one; a `Box` in a global?), a moved owner whose `S` holds a `Param` (a by-value parameter re-stored), `Option<Vec<T>>` and tuple-wrapped owners, and moving out of a `match` payload.
    - **Review and close (2026-11-05, by a second model that wrote none of the fix):** every claim verified, reproducers re-run, design decisions evaluated, and sibling routes probed. Re-runs: all 5 new tests pass (`push_fresh_owner_moved.zrl`, `push_owning_element_accepted.zrl`, `push_vec_of_frame_views_refused.zrl`, `push_arena_vec_refused.zrl`, `push_param_arena_vec_refused.zrl`); `sh scripts/test_origins.sh` passed 37/37; mutants `M136` and `M137` are both caught (with `build/mutation/survivors` cleared between runs); probes `gap_59`, `gap_60`, `gap_61`, and `gap_62` all exit 77 by default and are refused under `--provenance` (call store rule); warn-mode counts match exactly (`src/main.zrl` 222, `zbuild` 31, `zman` 40); `--provenance` differential confirms only `tests/stdlib/test_stdlib_clone.zrl` is newly accepted, 0 newly refused. Design judgments: (1) The rule that moved storage is not an escape is sound under linear/affine ownership: by-value stores transfer buffer drop responsibility to the recipient, so the buffer will be freed by the target container rather than this frame. (2) The both-sets guard (`Frame` in `S` but not `O`) is load-bearing: collections in arenas hold the arena's `Frame` in both `S` and `O`, so keeping `Frame` when in both sets ensures escaping arena memory continues to be refused (proven by `M137` and `gap_62`). (3) `ParamContents` dropping moved storage correctly reflects that callee parameter slots are local to the callee while elements move into the caller's container. (4) Leaving the overflow parameter (63) conservative safely favors over-rejection for extreme argument lists. Sibling routes probed: (1) storing a `Vec` into a global raw pointer is allowed for fresh owners and refused by the store rule when holding frame views; (2) forwarding a by-value owner parameter records clean summaries, and forwarding an owner with frame views is refused by the call store rule; (3) `Option<Vec<T>>` and tuple-wrapped owners pass for fresh allocations and are refused when holding frame views; (4) moving out of a `match` payload by value into a container passes for fresh owners and is refused for frame views. Gates re-checked: `scripts/check_safety_model.sh` (78 rows, 77 proven, 39 `known_gap`), `tm validate doc/tasks/*.tm.md` pass.

Decision taken for the reviewer: what the integer carried stays checked, so `(&x) as *i32 as u64` turned back into a pointer is `{Frame(x), Raw}` and refused (`raw_roundtrip_frame_refused.zrl`).

Siblings to probe: `@ptr_from_int` of a `Param`-derived integer stored into a caller's memory; a `Raw` pointer stored into a global; an `Intrinsic` result (still reads its arguments); a raw pointer in a closure capture; `Raw` through a `fn` value result.

Re-runs: all 7 new tests pass (`ptr_from_int_raw.zrl`, `raw_ptr_return_accepted.zrl`, `syscall_result_accepted.zrl`, `raw_roundtrip_frame_refused.zrl`, `raw_return_store_through_refused.zrl`, `unsafe_fn_body_checked_refused.zrl`, and `prov_reject_raw_store.zrl` under whole-program and `zbuild`); `sh scripts/test_interface_attrs.sh` passes; all 38 origin tests in `sh scripts/test_origins.sh` pass; mutants `M138`–`M141` are each caught by their targeted tests; warn-mode counts match (`src/main.zrl` 221, `zbuild` 30, `zman` 39, `zilc` 0); `--provenance` differential confirms 1 newly refused (`unsafe_fn_body_checked_refused.zrl`, the gap_47 unsafe storer probe) and 21 newly accepted (13 stdlib/functional, 2 constant raw pointers, 3 new accepted tests, 3 interface fixtures), 0 unsafe programs accepted. Design judgments: (1) `Raw` exempt in values allows valid allocator and OS pointer handling (`mmap`, `arena_init`) while remaining `external` as a memory destination, which correctly refuses frame escapes into raw targets (`*p = &x`). (2) Pointers loaded through raw memory inheriting `Raw` via `os_through` enables chained raw memory walks without false positives while preserving destination boundaries. (3) Tracking origins through integer conversions (`os_read` on integer argument in `PtrFromInt`) ensures frame addresses cast through integers cannot escape the return or store rules. (4) `@syscall` results carrying no origins correctly reflects kernel boundary encapsulation under `unsafe` blocks and clears the false escape in `linux.zrl:54`. Sibling routes probed: (1) `@ptr_from_int` of a `Param`-derived integer stored into caller memory records `store Param(1) -> Param(0)` and caller refuses a frame address passed there; (2) storing a pure `Raw` pointer into a global is accepted, while storing a frame address through a global raw pointer is refused; (3) `Intrinsic` results correctly propagate their argument origins (`@slice(&x, 1)` is refused by the return rule); (4) a closure capturing a raw pointer refuses storing a frame address into it; (5) calling through a `fn` value returning a raw pointer produces `Unknown` as designed. - [x] +safety +tests +AI-D Z-0071 B1.7d A value a callee reads through an argument keeps owned storage apart from addresses at the call
    > **Created:** 2026-11-05 **Found by:** Z-0070. **Before:** Z-0070, which needs it. **Check-in:** stop for review after it. Accepts more under `--provenance`: the rejection differential applies.
    > **Completed:** 2026-11-05 git:6418164

    - **Now:** `os_edge_effects` (`src/check_zir.zrl`) reads an edge's `ParamContents(i)` value with `os_param_origin(.., contents = true)`, which merges what the locals argument `i` reaches hold (`O`) and own (`S`) into one value, and writes it into the targets' `O`. A body reading through a reference keeps them apart (`os_read`, `os_through_split`, design note §3 _Reads through a reference_), and Z-0062 kept moved storage apart for a `ParamAddr` value; this path does not. Seen with Z-0070's patch, where `Vec.push` gains the true edge `ParamContents(0) -> ParamContents(0)` (growing copies the elements): in `fn mk() -> Vec<Vec<i32>> { let mut v = Vec::<Vec<i32>>.new(); v.push(Vec::<i32>.new()); return v; }` the inner Vec's storage lands in `O(v)` and `return v` is refused (`push_owning_element_accepted.zrl`).
    - **Do:** read a `ParamContents` value split, as `os_read` does through a pointer, and write the owned part into the targets' `S` (where the target owns storage) rather than their `O`, keeping the Z-0062 guard (storage the value also holds an address into stays an address). Reproduce first without Z-0070's patch: a callee whose own body copies through a parameter (`*dst = *src` of a `Vec<Vec<i32>>` element type, or a typed copy loop) should show the same merge; if no shape reaches it before Z-0070, apply Z-0070's patch in the scratch tree for the reproducer and say so. Tests: an origins golden for the owned part staying in `S`, the must-reject twin (a Vec holding views of this frame copied through a parameter into a caller's Vec stays refused), and a mutant.
    - **Verification:** the reproducer, `sh scripts/test_origins.sh`, the provenance group, the `--provenance` differential (every probe and test), `make test`, `make preflight-changed` (default 0/0). Stop for review.
    - **Stopped (2026-11-05): the plan above is unsound, and the trigger is not where it says.** Nothing committed to `src/`.
      - **A split by set alone opens a hole.** A view into an owner's buffer reaches the caller only through the owner's `S`: `struct Holder { v: &Vec<i32> } fn leak(out: &mut [i32], h: &Holder) { *out = h.v.as_slice(); }` called with `Holder { v: &v }` for a local `v` has the edge `ParamContents(1) -> Param(0)`, and the value's `Frame` of `v`'s buffer comes from `S(v)` alone; splitting would take it as moved storage and accept the escape (refused today: call store rule). An edge does not record the stored value's type, so the call cannot tell a moved owner (its storage goes with it) from a view into one (an address).
      - **The trigger is an integer, not a copy of elements.** With Z-0070's patch, `Vec.push`'s new edge `ParamContents(0) -> ParamContents(0)` comes from `zoril_allocator_alloc`'s `store Param(1) -> Param(0)`: the allocator stores `size` (offset bookkeeping, `arena.offset = aligned_offset + size - ARENA_HEADER_SIZE`), and in `push` the size is `size_of_n(new_cap, ..)`, an integer computed from `self.cap`, read through `self`. At the caller, that `ParamContents(0)` value merges what `v` reaches, its own buffer `Frame(_1)` included. (`memcpy` is a bare `extern`: no edge, gap_56's class.)
      - **Ruled out:** dropping the `ParamContents` bits of an integer-typed value at an in-body store (`ZirStmt.Assign`, mirroring `os_call`'s integer-result rule): no change, since the integer arrives here as a by-value parameter (`ParamAddr`), whose bits `p as usize` needs. Scratch compiler `build/z71/zoril.x` in the session; same five golden diffs, `push_owning_element_accepted.zrl` still refused, warn counts 197/40/0.
      - **Options for the maintainer:** (a) typed edges: each summary edge records whether the stored value is an integer, a moved owner or an address, and the call maps it accordingly (an integer's value carries only what the reached locals hold, never what they own; a moved owner's storage goes into the targets' `S`; an address keeps both); this changes the summary and the `.zri` (`#[summary(..)]`, Z-0057) and is its own task. Ruled out as a smaller fix: splitting an integer read inside the callee, since the callee records it as `ParamContents(i)` either way and the caller does the merging. (b) Defer Z-0070: an `unsafe fn` keeps the old masks until Z-0012, which then needs (a) or (b) before it deletes them.

    **Feedback from Gemini Argon:**
    Proceed with Option a: rewrite Z-0071 to implement typed summary edges now, keep Z-0070 active, and move to Z-0064 only after Z-0071 passes all probes.
    To keep `.zri` files minimal and avoid bloat, use a 2-way edge distinction (`addr` vs. `own`) instead of a 3-way tag by pruning pure scalars at the source:
    1. **Drop Scalar Edges Completely:**
      - When generating summary edges (for both `.direct` parameters and `.contents` reads) or evaluating stores, check the static type of the transferred value.
      - If the type is a pure scalar (`usize`, `i32`, `bool`, enums without payloads) that cannot contain references, slices, or owned handles, emit **no summary edge at all**.
      - This removes the `alloc` size-to-arena-header false edge (`self.cap` arithmetic into `*arena`) without needing an `integer` edge kind in `.zri` files.
    2. **Classify Remaining Edges into Two Kinds (**`addr` **vs.** `own`**):**
      - `addr` **(Address/View Edge):** Emit when the stored or returned value's type contains a borrowed reference or slice (`&T`, `&mut T`, `[T]`, or a struct holding a reference/slice like `Holder`).
      - `own` **(Owner/Move Edge):** Emit when the stored or returned value is a moved owning value (`Vec<T>`, `String`, or an owning struct with no borrowed views) or an internal raw backing pointer assignment (`self.ptr = p`) inside an `unsafe fn`.
      - **Mixed Structs (Owner + Reference):** If a struct contains both owned storage and a reference, emit both an `own` edge and an `addr` edge (or only `own` if `own` already forwards existing `src.addr` to `dst.addr` while keeping `src.owned` in `dst.owned`; only promote `src.owned` into `dst.addr` when an owned field is projected into a view).
    3. **Call-Site Instantiation Rules:**
      - **For an** `own` **edge (**`src -> dst`**):** Add `src.addr` into `dst.addr`, and add `src.owned` into `dst.owned`. Do not merge `src.owned` into `dst.addr`.
      - **For an** `addr` **edge (**`src -> dst`**):** Add both `src.addr` and `src.owned` into `dst.addr`.
    4. **Z-0071 Verification Checklist:**
      - Update the summary representation and `.zri` reader/writer to distinguish `addr` and `own` edges.
      - Confirm the `Holder` / `leak` probe is rejected (`*out = h.v.as_slice()` emits an `addr` edge, merging `v.owned` into `out.addr`).
      - Confirm `Vec::push` with nested `Vec<Vec<T>>` and arena allocation succeeds without polluting outer `v.addr` with inner owned storage.
      - Keep Z-0070's inferred summaries on `unsafe fn` bodies enabled, verify the full test suite is clean, and then proceed to Z-0064.

    - **Plan (2026-11-05, from the feedback above as reviewed; replaces the Do and Verification above):**
      - **Two edge kinds.** An edge's stored value is `own` when its type owns storage (`os_owns`) and it is stored by value (a move), and `addr` otherwise; the kind is not decided by whether the type holds references (a moved owner holding views is `own`, and `own` forwards its addresses as addresses, so the views stay checked). Encoded as a second range of value codes: `own` = base code + 130 (`ParamAddr` 130..193, `ParamContents` 194..257), so a row is keyed by (code, kind) and the `.zri` keeps its four integers per edge; an interface written before this reads as all `addr`.
      - **At the call.** `own` `ParamAddr(i)`: as Z-0062 (the argument's owned storage it does not hold an address into goes to the targets' `S`). `own` `ParamContents(i)`: what argument `i` reaches, read split (`os_through_split`, iterated): the held part to the targets' `O`, the owned part it holds no address into to their `S`. `addr`: both into `O`, as today; an `addr` `ParamAddr(i)` no longer drops the argument's owned storage (a pointer into a by-value owner's buffer is an address), and an `addr` `ParamContents(i)` keeps Z-0062's behaviour. Unknown calls and the old masks record `addr`.
      - **Decided: integer stores record no edge.** An in-body store whose type is a primitive records no summary edge. Narrow scalars carry no origins already; this drops the pointer-width ones (`usize`, `isize`, `u64`, `i64`), which the Scalars decision lets carry an address. The route given up: a callee storing a caller's frame address, cast to an integer, into the caller's memory is no longer seen at the call, so a later `@ptr_from_int` of it in the caller yields `Raw` and passes the return rule. `@ptr_from_int` needs `unsafe`, so safe code cannot use the route; it is written into design note §3 as a boundary, with a test showing it. The in-body store rule still checks such a store in the body that makes it. This removes the allocator's `size` -> arena-header edge that regressed Z-0062 under Z-0070.
      - **Not done:** no rule keyed on the function being `unsafe` (a store into a local's own storage is already left out by `os_write_edge`).
      - **Tests:** the `leak` probe refused; the `push_fresh_owner_moved.zrl` shape accepted with Z-0070's patch applied in a scratch tree (Z-0070 itself stays a separate commit); an origins golden for an `own` `ParamContents` edge keeping owned storage in `S`; a must-reject twin (a Vec of frame views moved through a parameter into a caller's Vec); the `.zri` round trip of an `own` edge in `tests/modular/iface_attrs/`; the integer boundary test; mutants for the kind choice, the split and the pruning.
      - **Verification:** `sh scripts/test_origins.sh`, the provenance group, `sh scripts/test_interface_attrs.sh`, the `--provenance` differential (every probe and test) with each newly accepted program read, `make test`, `make preflight-changed` (default 0/0). Stop for review.
    - **Done (`ad9045c`), as the plan above, with two changes found on the way:**

      - **M145 (the split read) moves to Z-0070.** Without Z-0070 no compilable program reaches an `own` `ParamContents` edge except through `Vec.pop`, whose result owns `Param(src)` (the arena tie: a popped Vec may live in `src`'s arena), so every such caller also holds `Frame(a)` and is refused with or without the split. The split is pinned by the golden `origins/own_contents_edge.zrl` (`b O={Frame(a)} S={Frame(_1), Frame(_3), Frame(_6)}`; merged it reads `O={Frame(_1), Frame(a), Frame(_3)} S={Frame(_6)}`, checked against a mutant built by hand), which the mutation harness does not run (goldens run only as the `origins` gate). With Z-0070's patch, `push_owning_element_accepted.zrl` needs the split (Z-0070's regression), so M145 belongs with Z-0070.

Judge: the kind decided by `os_owns` of the stored value's type; `own` for a declared edge only when every stored argument owns; the integer boundary (design note §3) against keeping pointer-width integer edges; `addr` `ParamAddr` keeping a by-value owner's storage (sound, and it newly refuses `addr_edge_by_value_owner_refused.zrl`). Siblings to probe: an `own` edge through the overflow parameter (63); an `own` edge into a global; a moved owner holding a `Param` view; a struct holding both an owner and a reference stored by value; the `Option.take` route above.

    **Feedback from Gemini Argon**
    **TL;DR:** The implementation in `ad9045c` and `f008948` is clean, minimal, and addresses the core feedback well— preserving the 4-`u64` `.zri` layout via `OS_EDGE_OWN = 130` and pruning primitive store edges at `os_stmt`. All four design judgements are sound for single-kind types, **except** that probing your Siblings #1, #3, and #4 reveals one minor bounds bug on overflow parameter `63` and one soundness hole when a callee packs a non-owning view/pointer derived from a parameter (`v.as_slice()` or `b.data`) into an owning container/struct (`Vec<[i32]>` or `Both`) and moves that container/struct into `*out`.

    **Review of the Four Judgements**
    - `OS_EDGE_OWN` **encoding (**`code + 130`**):** Spot-on minimalist choice. It preserves the 4-integer `.zri` row layout and backward compatibility without adding schema bloat.
    - **Declared** `#[stores(..)]` **edges (**`M147`**):** Marking declared edges `own` when every stored argument satisfies `os_owns` is necessary and sound so `Vec.push`'s declaration behaves identically to its inferred body summary.
    - **Primitive store boundary (**`ILTYPE_PRIMITIVE`, `M146`**):** Pruning primitive stores from summary generation in `os_stmt` while keeping the in-body `os_check_store` check right above it (`int_store_in_body_refused.zrl` vs. `int_store_through_call_accepted.zrl`) cleanly removes the arena allocator's `size -> *arena` false edge and is properly documented in `doc/design/provenance.md`.
    - `addr ParamAddr` **keeping by-value owned storage (**`M148`**):** Gating `os_drop_moved_storage` on `code >= 64 || own` fixes a real pre-existing hole (`addr_edge_by_value_owner_refused.zrl` where `keep` stores `b.data` and drops `b: Buf`).
    **Analysis of the Five "Siblings to Probe"**
    - **Sibling 1 (**`own` **edge through overflow parameter** `63`**):**
      - `os_param_contents_split` (line 6510) properly loops `while k < args.len && (k == i || i == 63 as usize)`.
      - However, in `os_edge_effects` (line 6610), `if code < 128 as usize && pidx < 63 as usize` skips `pidx == 63`. An `own ParamAddr(63)` edge on argument 63+ will neither populate `moved` nor drop its moved storage from `tv`.
      - **Fix:** Loop `k` from `pidx` across `args.len` when `pidx == 63` in that block just like `os_param_origin` and `os_param_contents_split` do.
    - **Sibling 2 (**`own` **edge into a global):**
      - Sound as written in `os_edge_effects` (lines 6620–6631).
      - Stripping `S \ O` before checking `bound = os_frame_bound(c, tv)` allows moving a pure heap owner into a global while still rejecting an owner that holds a frame view (`Frame` in `O`) or lives in a local arena (`Frame(a)` in both `O` and `S`).
    - **Siblings 3 & 4 (Moved owner holding a** `Param` **view, and Mixed struct holding both an owner and a reference/pointer):**
      - **When built in the caller and moved by the callee:** Sound (`own_contents_edge_frame_views_refused.zrl` passes because the view is in `O(a)` and the buffer is in `S(a)`).
      - **When built in the callee from a parameter view/pointer and moved into** `*out`: **Unsound** when `own` is decided solely by `os_owns(ty)` at the final `*out = ...` store.
      - **Reproducer A (Sibling 3 —** `Vec<[i32]>` **built in callee):**
    ```text
    import "vec.zrl";
    use vec::Vec;
    fn leak_vec(out: &mut Vec<[i32]>, v: &Vec<i32>) {
        let mut tmp = Vec::<[i32]>.new();
        tmp.push(v.as_slice());
        *out = tmp;
    }
    fn f(out: &mut Vec<[i32]>) {
        let mut v = Vec::<i32>.new();
        v.push(7);
        leak_vec(out, &v);
    }
    ```
    - Here `v.as_slice()` puts `ParamContents(1)` into `O(tmp)` via an `addr` store (`[i32]`), but `*out = tmp` sees `os_owns(Vec<[i32]>) == true` and emits `own ParamContents(1) -> ParamAddr(0)`. At `leak_vec(out, &v)`, `os_param_contents_split` reads `v` split (`O(v) = {}` into `tv`, `S(v) = { Frame(v_buf) }` into `moved`), so `f` is accepted and `out` dangles when `v` drops.
      - **Reproducer B (Sibling 4 — Mixed struct built in callee):**
    ```text
    import "vec.zrl";
    use vec::Vec;
    struct Both { own_v: Vec<i32>, view: [i32] }
    fn leak_both(out: &mut Both, v: &Vec<i32>) {
        *out = Both { own_v: Vec::<i32>.new(), view: v.as_slice() };
    }
    fn f(out: &mut Both) {
        let mut v = Vec::<i32>.new();
        v.push(7);
        leak_both(out, &v);
    }
    ```
    - (And the `ParamAddr` twin `keep_both(out: &mut BothPtr, b: Buf) { *out = BothPtr { own_v: Vec.new(),` `p: b.data }; }`.) Because `os_owns(Both)` is `true`, `os_stmt` marks `ParamContents(1)` / `ParamAddr(1)` as `own` even though it entered via a non-owning `[i32]` or `*mut u8` field.
      - **Minimal Fix for Siblings 3 & 4:** A parameter origin (`ParamAddr(i)` or `ParamContents(i)`) inside `ev` should only emit an `own` edge if it remained an unprojected owner all the way to the store. Either:
        - Track moved-owner parameter bits separately from held-address parameter bits (clearing the owner bits whenever an expression, field projection like `b.data`, call result like `v.as_slice()`, or store like `tmp.push(slice)` has `!os_owns(ty)`), **or**
        - Only classify a store as `own` when `os_owns(ty)` is true **and** `ty` cannot hold non-owning views/raw pointers (or emit `addr` for any parameter origin that passed through a `!os_owns` expression/field). Note that the first option preserves `own` for `Vec<[i32]>` when the `Vec<[i32]>` itself is moved from a parameter (`own_contents_edge_frame_views_refused.zrl`) while still rejecting `leak_vec`, `leak_both`, and `keep_both`.
    - **Sibling 5 (**`Option.take` **/** `swap_bytes`**):**
      - Recording this on Z-0012 (so the legacy check is not removed until `Option.take` and other `swap_bytes` callers use a typed move or summary attribute) is the right call.
    **Recommended Next Steps for the Agent**
    1. Probe `leak_vec`, `leak_both`, and `keep_both` above and fix the `own` classification so non-owning views/projections (`!os_owns`) packed into owning structs/containers still emit `addr` edges.
    2. Fix the `pidx < 63` bound in `os_edge_effects` (line 6610) so overflow parameter `63` handles `own ParamAddr(63)` consistently with `os_param_contents_split`.
    3. Resume Z-0070 (re-apply the two `store_origin_summary` sites, add mutant `M145`, update the four `Raw` goldens), then proceed to Z-0064.
    - **Review probed (2026-11-05), `--provenance`, base `e09b409` (`build/z71/zoril.base`) against `ad9045c`; files in `build/z71r/`:**
      - **`leak_vec` (A above): refused by the base (call store rule), accepted by `ad9045c`. A regression.** The default mode accepts it on both (gap_59 to gap_61's class: the old rules do not see the views a moved owner holds), so `--provenance` was its only refusal. The `--provenance` differential missed it: no probe builds the owner inside the callee.
      - **`leak_both` (B above):** both refuse, but `ad9045c` only by the old rule (`cannot store 'v'`); the provenance refusal is gone, so it opens when Z-0012 deletes the old rules.
      - **`keep_both` as written:** refused by both (that `Buf` owns nothing). **With `Buf` freeing its block in `Drop`** (as in `addr_edge_by_value_owner_refused.zrl`), `BothPtr { own_v: Vec::<i32>.new(), p: b.data }` stored into `*out`: **accepted by both.** Not a regression, an older hole: a ParamAddr edge dropped a by-value owner's storage before Z-0071, and an `own` edge still does.
      - **Cause:** the kind is decided per store from the stored value's type (`os_owns`), but it belongs to each parameter origin in the value: one that arrived as a whole owner moved by value is `own`, one that arrived as a view or a field projection (`v.as_slice()`, `b.data`) is `addr`, whatever the outer type.
      - **The reviewer's minimal fix is not enough.** Tracking owner bits apart from address bits must start them at a by-value owning parameter (`Vec.push`'s `value`, or `push_owning_element_accepted` regresses), and `own_contents_edge.zrl` gets its owner from a call result (`src.pop()`), so a callee returning a `Vec<[i32]>` it built around a view would carry A's hole through `return`: return summaries need the kind too. Split out as Z-0072.
      - **Overflow parameter 63** (`pidx < 63` in `os_edge_effects`, since Z-0062): correct, and a precision gap only: the skipped split leaves owned storage counted as an address, which refuses more. Listed on Z-0072.
      - **Z-0071 stays open until Z-0072 lands**, so `--provenance` is not left weaker than the base. Z-0070 waits for both.
      - **Stepped back (2026-11-05):** the integer rule alone, without the edge kinds, fixes Z-0070's regression at the same warning counts (196 / 40 / 0) and refuses `leak_vec` and `leak_both` (measured in Z-0072's text). The kinds were planned from the diagnosis without measuring the integer rule alone first. Z-0072 is now the removal of the kinds; the `keep_both` case is Z-0073's decision.
    - **Review and close (2026-11-05, by the reviewer of Z-0071 and Z-0072):** the edge kinds this task added at `ad9045c` were removed by Z-0072, which left this task's surviving part in the tree: an integer stored in the body records no summary edge (`6418164`). That rule is what fixes Z-0070's regression; the kinds bought no measured precision and let a callee wrap a view of a parameter in an owner it stores out (`leak_vec` accepted, `leak_both` refused only by the old rule). The routes the kinds got wrong are pinned by `owner_built_in_callee_around_view_refused.zrl` and `struct_owner_beside_view_refused.zrl`; the first one's default-mode acceptance is `gap_63` (`81b53d7`). Under `--provenance` the base-to-HEAD differential over every probe and test changes one program, `int_store_through_call_accepted.zrl` (the decided boundary); the default mode is unchanged. The `keep_both` case is Z-0073; the `Option.take` route is on Z-0012. `make test` (with Z-0072): 3332 run, 3332 passed, 0 failed, 82 skipped.

- [x] +tests +tools +AI-G Z-0081 Isolate test suite from user Git configuration and signing prompts
    > **Created:** 2026-11-06

    - **Now:** running `make test` or `./scripts/test_preflight.sh` prompts for a Git commit signing password/passphrase when the user has `commit.gpgsign = true` in their global `~/.gitconfig`. In `scripts/test_preflight.sh` Case 13, scratch test repositories are created to test `preflight_base.sh`, and dummy commits are made via `g() { git -c user.name=t -c user.email=t@t ...; }` without `-c commit.gpgsign=false`, falling back to the user's global signing key and invoking GPG/SSH pinentry. Furthermore, scratch test repositories and test runners inherit user-level and system-level Git configurations, hooks, and environment variables.
    - **Do:** (1) isolate `scripts/test.sh` from user and system Git configurations (`GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`, `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM=/dev/null`, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`, unsetting `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`, `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS`, and environment `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_*`/`GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_*`); (2) update `scripts/test_preflight.sh`'s `g()` helper and scratch repo creation to disable commit/tag signing (`-c commit.gpgsign=false -c tag.gpgsign=false -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null`) and set Git config isolation; (3) ensure sibling test scripts creating throwaway scratch repos (`test_build_info.sh`, `test_full_test_stamp.sh`, `check_clean_tree.sh`, `check_stray_files.sh`, `check_fuzz_fresh.sh`, `test_fuzz_tools.sh`, `dist_user_paths.sh`) explicitly disable signing and isolate from user config.
    - **Tests:** run `scripts/test_preflight.sh` and `make test` under simulated `commit.gpgsign = true` with a failing signer (`gpg.ssh.program = false` / dummy gpg) to prove that neither signing nor password prompts are triggered. Run `make test`.
    - **Completed (2026-11-06, git:84a4095):** (1) `scripts/test.sh` now sets `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`, `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM=/dev/null`, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`, unsets `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`, `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS`, and unsets inherited `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_*` / `GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_*`. (2) In `scripts/test_preflight.sh`, `g()` helper now includes `-c commit.gpgsign=false -c tag.gpgsign=false -c core.hooksPath=/dev/null` and sets config isolation. (3) `test_build_info.sh`, `test_full_test_stamp.sh`, `check_clean_tree.sh`, `check_stray_files.sh`, `check_fuzz_fresh.sh`, `test_fuzz_tools.sh`, and `dist_user_paths.sh` updated with Git isolation and disabled signing/hooks. Verified on all individual scripts with simulated broken signing key (`[commit] gpgsign = true`, `[gpg "ssh"] program = false`, `[core] hooksPath = /nonexistent`), confirming 0 signing attempts or failures. Checks: `scripts/check_portability.sh` passed, `scripts/check_comment_noise.sh` passed, `make test` passed (3347 run, 3347 passed, 0 failed, 82 skipped).

- [x] +safety +tests +AI-D Z-0078 B1.7e2 `#[fresh]`: an `unsafe fn` declares that its result is fresh storage (the attribute, without its origin rule)
    > **Created:** 2026-11-05 **Decided by:** the maintainer, from Z-0076's options (option (a), as an attribute, merged with Z-0077). **Before:** Z-0076, which gives it its meaning in the origin pass. **Check-in:** stop for review after it.

    - **Why:** an allocator's anonymous `mmap` and a raw pointer to memory outside the frame are one `Raw` bit (Z-0076's cause). The `unsafe` author who maps fresh pages is the one who knows they alias nothing; `#[fresh]` is that claim, made where `Raw` is made, so the origin pass can read the result as fresh storage of the caller (as a call returning an owner already is) instead of `Raw`.
    - **Do (language and interface only):** specify `#[fresh]` in `doc/spec/surface_syntax.md` §2.5: on an `unsafe fn` returning a raw pointer, the claim that each call returns memory no other live pointer reaches, which the caller owns; a false claim is the author's fault, like any `unsafe` obligation (cross-reference `doc/spec/unsafe_policy.md` and design note §3 _Raw operations_). The compiler records it on the function (`ILFunction`), refuses it on a safe `fn`, on a non-function item, and on a function whose result is not a raw pointer (say which, with a diagnostic test each), and writes it into the `.zri` and reads it back (`tests/modular/iface_attrs/`, a `want`-style check that a `#[fresh]` declaration survives, and that a library's `#[fresh]` fn read from the interface is marked). No origin rule here: `--emit-origins` output does not change (the goldens are the check).
    - **Stage1:** `stdlib/mem_raw.zrl` is in `src/`'s import closure. Probed 2026-11-05: `build/zoril-stage1` and `build/zoril` both accept `#[fresh]` on an `unsafe fn` silently (exit 0, no diagnostic; `build/z76/attr.zrl`), so the stdlib can carry it without a re-pin. The same probe shows an unknown attribute is accepted silently today, so a misspelt `#[frsh]` would pass; this task does not change that (a finding to record, not fix, unless the maintainer asks).
    - **Verification:** `make test`, `make preflight-changed` (rejection differential 0/0), the interface gate, the stage1 build of `src/` (the fixpoint) if any `stdlib/` file in the closure is touched (none should be here). Stop for review.
    - **Done (`4a17e22`, mutants `ee9705e`):** `src/parser/decl.zrl` reads `#[fresh]` into `Attribute.fresh` (with its span) and refuses it in three places: on a safe `fn` ("freshness is a claim its author makes, like any unsafe obligation"), on a function whose result is not written `*T` or `*mut T` (a type alias of a raw pointer is refused too: the check is on the written type), and on an item that is not a function (at the declaration, and for an associated constant in an impl body). `FnDecl.fresh` and `ILFunction.fresh` carry it (`tools/zilc`'s `ILFunction` too); `src/interface_print.zrl` writes it for plain, generic, impl and trait functions. Nothing reads `ILFunction.fresh` yet (Z-0076). Spec: `surface_syntax.md` §2.5 and `unsafe_policy.md` §1, which say no check reads it yet.
    - **Tests:** `tests/error_reporting/err_fresh_{on_safe_fn,not_raw_pointer,on_struct,on_assoc_const}.zrl`; `tests/modular/iface_attrs/lib.zrl` gains `gen_fresh`, `fresh_block`, `impl_fresh` and the trait method `tr_fresh`, the gate checks `#[fresh]` above each in the `.zri`, and `accept_fresh_imported.zrl` builds a caller of three of them whole-program and through zbuild (the `.zri` parses back). With an older compiler (`build/z70/zoril.c5099ab`, no `#[fresh]`) the gate fails on `gen_fresh`. Origin goldens unchanged (`sh scripts/test_origins.sh`). "Marked on import" is not observable until Z-0076 reads the flag; Z-0076's interface test is where it is checked.
    - **Mutants:** M151 to M154 (the four refusals), each caught by its test. The interface printing has none: the harness runs no gate. Checked by hand instead: scratch compilers with the impl and trait printing disabled fail the gate on `impl_fresh` and on `tr_fresh`. **Found:** `scripts/mutation-check.sh`'s header names `scripts/mutation_uncovered.list` and `scripts/mutation_incremental.list`, and neither file exists, so no mutation is run against the incremental scenario and no list records the uncovered ones. Recorded here, not fixed.
    - **Found:** a generic body using `@ptr_from_int`, `@ptr_cast`, `@int_cast` or `@alloca` cannot be read back from its `.zri` (printed with `::<`). Z-0079. The fixture's generic `#[fresh]` bodies avoid those builtins for that reason.
    - **Stage1:** the fixpoint in `make preflight-changed` built `src/` through stage1 with these changes; no `stdlib/` file changed.
    - **Checks:** `make test` at `4a17e22`: 3346 run, 3346 passed, 0 failed, 82 skipped. `PREFLIGHT_BASE=4bbe023 make preflight-changed`: exit 0, 30 targeted mutations caught, fixpoint identical, rejection differential 0 newly accepted and 0 newly refused of 69123 programs. M151 to M154 run one by one after it. Safety figures re-recorded (compiler lines 49,218; 874 `unsafe { }` blocks: the textual count matches the new `if !is_unsafe {` guard; src density 4.62%). Benchmark files deleted. `bench/fuzz/README.md` is stale for `src/`.
    - **Awaiting review:** re-run the four `err_fresh_*` tests, `sh scripts/test_interface_attrs.sh`, `sh scripts/test_origins.sh`, `./scripts/mutation-check.sh M151` to `M154`.     - **Cause (2026-11-05, `--emit-origins` with a scratch compiler printing every module, `build/z76/zoril.dump`, on `build/z76/p2a.zrl`):** `Arena::new` returns `O={Raw}` (the `mmap`'d header, through `arena_init_result`). In `main`, `a O={Frame(_2), Raw} S={Frame(_2), Frame(_4), Raw}`: the call already gives `a` fresh storage of this frame (`Frame(_2)`, an owning result, `OS_Q_FRESH`), and the result flags add `Raw` beside it. `Vec::new_in` returns `{Param(0), ParamContents(0)}`, so `v O={Frame(_2), Frame(_4), Frame(a), Raw}`. `Vec::push`'s edge `store ParamContents(0) -> Param(0)` targets `v`; `os_dest_reach` from `Frame(v)` adds `S(v)` and, the `Vec` type holding a mutable handle, `O(v)`, so the destination holds `Raw`, `os_external` is true, and the value (what `&mut v` reaches, this frame's storage) is refused. The heap `Vec` differs only in that no `Raw` reaches the caller: `Vec::new`'s result is `S={Frame(_1)}`, `O={}` (`build/z76/p1b.zrl`). **The arena's own mapping and a raw pointer to memory outside the frame are the same single `Raw` bit**, so no rule on the caller's sets can tell them apart.
    - **The obvious rule is unsound in principle:** dropping `Raw` from an owning result that already gets fresh storage (the first candidate above) would also drop it for an owner wrapping a raw pointer to memory outside the frame; a safe method of it storing an argument through that pointer would then look like a store into this frame's storage.
