- [x] +safety +tests +AI-D Z-0082 B1.7h An `Arena` taken out of its `Result` by `unwrap` is not the `Result`'s contents
    > **Created:** 2026-11-06 **Found by:** Z-0076 (the one program of Z-0070's 42 it left refused). **After:** Z-0076. **Before:** Z-0064. **Check-in:** stop for review after it.
    > **Completed:** 2026-11-06 git:22120a8

    - **Over-refusal:** `let arena = Arena.new(1 << 20).unwrap();` then `Command.new("/bin/true", &arena)` and any later call storing through `&arena` (`list_dir("/tmp", &arena)`): refused under `--provenance` by "the callee stores an address of this frame into memory that outlives it (call store rule)" (`tests/stdlib/test_stdlib_arena_fns_safe_paths.zrl`, lines 96 to 137; reduced: `build/z76/t/c3.zrl`). The same with `match Arena.new(..) { Ok(a) => a, Err(_) => .. }` is accepted (`c2.zrl`). A local `Arena` returned after `unwrap` (`fn mk() -> Arena { let a = Arena.new(4096).unwrap(); return a; }`) is refused by the return rule, by `508e39d`'s compiler too (`build/z76/sib/s6_arena_returned.zrl`).
    - **Cause (Z-0076, `--emit-origins`, `build/z76/c1.origins`):** `Command.new`'s edge `store ParamContents(0) -> ParamContents(1)` writes the static `program` slice into the arena's memory, whose destination reach is `S(arena) = {Frame(_2), Frame(_4)}` (the `Result` temporaries), so `O(_2)` gains `Static`. `unwrap` takes the `Result<Arena, Error>` by value; in `os_call` the result is owning and the argument can reach an `Arena`, so `arena_tie` puts the argument's own origins (`O(_2)`, now with `Static`) into the result's storage: `arena S={Frame(_2), Frame(_4), Static}`. On the fixpoint's next pass every store through `&arena` has `Static` in its destination and is refused. Before Z-0070 `Command.new_raw` had no summary, so the `Static` never reached the arena.
    - **Do:** first confirm the cause with the scratch dump compiler, then the narrowest rule. Candidates, not a plan: for an argument passed by value (the `Result` moves into `unwrap`), the arena tie names the storage the argument owns (`S`), not what it holds (`O`); or the edge's write into an owner's storage leaves what the owner holds as a value apart from its storage. Series rule: it removes origins, so measure it alone and probe the sibling routes (a view of the arena's buffer returned after `unwrap` stays refused; an `Arena` in a `Result` returned whole stays accepted; `Option<Arena>` and a user enum wrapping an `Arena` behave the same).
    - **Done when:** `test_stdlib_arena_fns_safe_paths.zrl` and `s6_arena_returned.zrl` are accepted under `--provenance`, so all 42 of Z-0070's programs are; nothing the base refuses for another reason is accepted (the `--provenance` differential read line by line).
    - **Verification:** `make test`, `make preflight-changed`, the differential and the warning diff recorded; the fuzz record's staleness noted. Stop for review.
    - **Cause confirmed (2026-11-06):** as above, with one correction to the line: `os_call`'s arena branch adds `tao` to the result's storage, and `tao` is the argument's `O` with its `S` merged in (`os_or(c, tao, tas)` before the loop). For `&a` that is `{Frame(a)}`; for a `Result<Arena, _>` passed by value it is also what the arena's memory holds. Minimal reproducer: `let a = Arena.new(4096).unwrap();`, a literal pushed into one `Vec` in `a`, then a push into another (`arena_unwrap_literal_then_push_accepted.zrl`; `Command.new` is not needed).
    - **The rule (`src/check_zir.zrl`):** a new type question `OS_Q_ARENA_REF`, whether a type reaches an `Arena` through a pointer or a slice (an `Arena` or `ArenaHeader` held by value answers no, though its header field is a pointer). In `os_call`, an argument that can reach an arena and answers no names the arena's storage by `S(arg)` alone, in both the `ret_storage` and the body-summary `ret_ties` branch; every other argument (a reference, `&Arena` in a struct, a raw `*mut ArenaHeader`, an unknown type) keeps `tao`. The non-body `ret_ties` branch (`tr`) is unchanged.
    - **Results:** the `--provenance` differential (HEAD `1ca761e` against the change, the same `stdlib/`): one program newly accepted, `tests/stdlib/test_stdlib_arena_fns_safe_paths.zrl`, none newly refused. Warnings: 195 / 25 / 0 before and after, identical line by line. Sibling probes (`build/z82/sib/`): a view of an unwrapped arena's buffer returned stays refused by the new return rule (`arena_unwrap_view_returned_refused.zrl`); `Option<Arena>` unwrapped is accepted (`option_arena_unwrap_store_accepted.zrl`), as is a user enum holding an `Arena` at the call store rule; a struct holding `&Arena` passed by value stays refused when a frame view goes into a `Vec` in the caller's arena (`arena_ref_wrapper_frame_view_refused.zrl`), and so does a struct holding a raw `*mut ArenaHeader`; a `Result<Arena, _>` returned whole stays accepted; a `Result<Arena, _>` parameter unwrapped, then a frame view pushed into the caller's `Vec`, stays refused.
    - **Not done here, so `s6_arena_returned.zrl` stays refused (a change to "Done when"):** a local `Arena` returned after `unwrap` is refused by the return rule for a cause that is not the arena's: the same `os_or(c, tao, tas)` puts the argument's owned storage into the result's *addresses*, so any owner passed by value through a call and returned is refused. `fn id(v: Vec<i32>) -> Vec<i32> { return v; }` with `return id(v)` from a local heap `Vec`, and `Option.Some(Vec::<i32>.new()).unwrap()` returned, are refused the same way, by `508e39d`'s compiler too (`build/z76/t/idv.zrl`, `ido.zrl`). It is general, older than the series, and removing that merge changes every by-value call result, so it is Z-0083, not part of this task.
    - **Mutations:** M158 (always `tao`: caught by `arena_unwrap_literal_then_push_accepted`), M159 (always `S`: caught by `arena_ref_wrapper_frame_view_refused`), M160 (`Arena` by value answers yes: caught by `arena_unwrap_literal_then_push_accepted`), M161 (a pointer to an `Arena` answers no: caught by `arena_ref_wrapper_frame_view_refused`). The wrapper test's first version pushed the `Vec` into an out-parameter, which refused it by another route, and M159 and M161 survived it; it now only pushes the frame view.
    - **Verification (2026-11-06, at `b2767f3`):** `make test`: 3378 run, 3378 passed, 0 failed, 82 skipped. `PREFLIGHT_BASE=1ca761e make preflight-changed`: exit 0; 42 targeted mutations caught (M158 to M161 as above); fixpoint identical; rejection differential 0 newly accepted, 0 newly refused, of 69123 programs (the default mode is unchanged). `bench/fuzz/README.md` is stale for this `src/` change.
    - **Awaiting review:** re-run `arena_unwrap_literal_then_push_accepted.zrl` and `test_stdlib_arena_fns_safe_paths.zrl` under `--provenance` against `1ca761e`; `--emit-origins` on the first with a dump compiler (`S(a)` holds no `Static`); judge `OS_Q_ARENA_REF`'s answers (an `Arena` or `ArenaHeader` by value: no; a pointer, a reference or a slice to one: yes; an unknown type or a generic: yes) and probe more holders: a tuple `(Arena, &Arena)`, a `Box<Arena>`, `[Arena; 2]`, a struct holding both an `Arena` and an `&Arena`, a generic wrapper `Wrap<T>` instantiated with `Arena`; confirm that narrowing "Done when" to the store-rule cause and splitting out Z-0083 is acceptable.
    - **Review (2026-11-06, by a second model; nothing changed by it):** confirmed cause and fix. `test_stdlib_arena_fns_safe_paths.zrl` and `arena_unwrap_literal_then_push_accepted.zrl` re-run under `--provenance` against `1ca761e` (refused at base, pass at HEAD); `--emit-origins` confirmed `Static` absent from `S(a)`. `--provenance` differential: exactly `test_stdlib_arena_fns_safe_paths.zrl` and the two new accept tests newly accepted, 0 newly refused. Warnings unchanged (195 / 25 / 0). Mutants M158–M161 re-tested and caught. Sibling holders probed: `(Arena, &Arena)`, `struct Both { a: Arena, r: &Arena }`, `Wrap<&Arena>` and `Box<&Arena>` all stay refused by the call store rule; `[Arena; 2]`, `Box<Arena>` and `Wrap<Arena>` accepted; unresolved/generic types safely fall back to `tao`. - [x] +safety +tests +AI-D Z-0083 B1.7i An owner passed by value through a call and returned is not an address of this frame
    > **Created:** 2026-11-06 **Found by:** Z-0082. **After:** Z-0082. **Before:** Z-0064 (or counted by it, if the maintainer prefers). **Check-in:** stop for review after it.
    > **Completed:** 2026-11-06 git:ebad9c1

    - **Over-refusal:** `fn id(v: Vec<i32>) -> Vec<i32> { return v; }` and `fn mk() -> Vec<i32> { let v = Vec::<i32>.new(); return id(v); }` is refused under `--provenance` by "the returned value holds an address of this frame (return rule)"; so is `return Option.Some(Vec::<i32>.new()).unwrap();` through a local, and a local `Arena` returned after `Arena.new(..).unwrap()` (`build/z76/t/idv.zrl`, `ido.zrl`, `build/z76/sib/s6_arena_returned.zrl`). Refused by `508e39d`'s compiler too: not from this series.
    - **Cause (Z-0082, read, not yet confirmed with `--emit-origins`):** `os_call` merges each argument's owned storage into its addresses (`let _ = os_or(c, tao, tas);`, from Z-0048, no recorded reason) before the result takes `tao` for an argument in `ret_storage`. A by-value owner moved into the call then gives the result `Frame(arg)` as an address, and `return` refuses it. The result should own that storage (`S`), as a moved owner does in an edge (Z-0062's moved-storage rule).
    - **Do:** confirm the cause with the dump compiler; find why the merge was written (a result that really points into an argument's owned storage, a box passed by value, the `Cast` of an owner); then the narrowest rule, for example: for an argument passed by value whose type owns storage, the result takes its `S` as storage, not as addresses. It removes origins, so the series rule applies: measured alone, sibling routes probed (a view into a by-value `Vec` argument returned through the call stays refused; a box passed by value and an address into it returned; `Option<&T>` unwrapped; a struct holding a view beside an owner).
    - **Done when:** the three programs above are accepted under `--provenance`, nothing the base refuses for another reason is accepted (the `--provenance` differential and the warning diff read line by line), and the sibling probes are tests.
    - **Verification:** `make test`, `make preflight-changed`; the fuzz record's staleness noted. Stop for review.
    - **Cause confirmed (2026-11-06, `--emit-origins`, scratch dump compiler `build/z83/zoril.dump`, on `idv.zrl`):** `id`'s summary is `return O={Param(0)} S={Frame(v)}`: the result holds what argument 0 holds, and owns fresh storage. In `mk`, `os_call` takes `tao` for the argument in `ret_storage`, and `tao` is `O(v) ∪ S(v)` after the merge, so the result gets `O={Frame(_1)}`, the moved `Vec`'s own buffer, as an address, and `return` refuses it. Why the merge is there: `ParamContents(i)` (the `ret_ties` branch) is what is reached through the argument, and for a by-value owner that is through its storage too, so the merge is right for ties; only the `ret_storage` branch (`Param(i)`, what the argument holds) misreads it.
    - **The rule (`os_call`, `src/check_zir.zrl`):** under a body summary, an argument in `ret_storage` whose type owns storage (`os_owns`) gives the result its `O` alone, kept before the merge in a new scratch set `OS_T_AH`. Every other case is unchanged (a reference, a non-owning value, an old mask). A first version also put the argument's `S` into the result's storage; no test could see that line (the result already owns fresh storage when the callee returns its parameter), so it was taken out before the final measurement. The `body` condition is kept as the narrower reading: an old mask's `ret_storage` says the result points into the argument, and no whole-program test reaches that branch with a by-value owner, so no mutant covers it.
    - **Results:** the `--provenance` differential (HEAD `d7e8286` against the change, the same `stdlib/`): three programs newly accepted, none newly refused. `owner_through_call_returned_accepted.zrl` (new); `tests/language/memory/linear_deep_no_resource_accepted.zrl` (`return a + sum(t)`: `sum` takes a `List` by value, and the integer result was given the list's own storage as an address); `tests/stdlib/test_stdlib_bufio.zrl` (`BufWriter.into_inner` returns `inner_opt.unwrap()`, the writer moved out of `self`). Both are this class and safe: what is returned is a moved owner or an integer. Warnings: 195 / 25 / 0 before and after, identical line by line; no warning in the tree was of this class.
    - **Sibling routes (`build/z83/sib/`, both compilers):** still refused: a `Box` passed by value whose address is returned (`b as *i32`); a moved `Vec`'s `as_ptr()` returned through the call (refused at the caller too: `moved_vec_raw_buffer_returned_refused.zrl`, its `EXPECT` anchored to the caller's line); a struct holding a view beside an owner, passed through and returned; `Option<&i32>` of a local unwrapped and returned; a `Vec` of frame views moved through `unwrap` and returned (`owner_through_call_views_kept_refused.zrl`) or through `id` into the caller's slot (`owner_through_call_into_caller_slot_refused.zrl`); a moved `Vec`'s slice returned; a box moved through `id` and a view of it returned; an arena `Vec` moved through `id` and returned (its arena is this frame's). Accepted: the three programs of the over-refusal, and a `Vec` moved through `id` and returned.
    - **Mutations:** M162 (the rule off: caught by `owner_through_call_returned_accepted`), M163 (the result gets none of what the owner holds: caught by `owner_through_call_into_caller_slot_refused`).
    - **Verification (2026-11-06, at `1d81871`):** `make test`: 3386 run, 3386 passed, 0 failed, 82 skipped. `PREFLIGHT_BASE=d7e8286 make preflight-changed`: exit 0; 44 targeted mutations caught (M162, M163 as above); fixpoint identical; rejection differential 0 newly accepted, 0 newly refused, of 69123 programs (the default mode is unchanged). `bench/fuzz/README.md` is stale for this `src/` change.
    - **Awaiting review:** re-run `build/z83/idv.zrl`, `ido.zrl`, `s6_arena_returned.zrl` and the four new tests under `--provenance` against `d7e8286`; `--emit-origins` on `idv.zrl` (`mk`'s result `O={}`); read the two existing programs newly accepted; judge the claim in the rule's comment (a callee returning an address into its by-value parameter's storage says so with `Unknown`) on more routes: a field of a by-value struct parameter returned by address, a slice of a by-value array parameter, a closure capturing a by-value parameter, a by-value parameter's address stored into a caller's slot and then the parameter returned.
    - **Review (2026-11-06, by a second model; nothing changed by it):** confirmed cause and fix. `idv.zrl`, `ido.zrl`, `s6_arena_returned.zrl` and `owner_through_call_returned_accepted.zrl` re-run under `--provenance` against `d7e8286` (all refused at base, pass at HEAD); `--emit-origins` on `idv.zrl` confirmed `return O={}` for `mk`. `--provenance` differential: exactly 3 newly accepted (`owner_through_call_returned_accepted.zrl`, `linear_deep_no_resource_accepted.zrl`, `test_stdlib_bufio.zrl`), 0 newly refused. Warnings unchanged (195 / 25 / 0). Mutants M162 and M163 re-tested and caught. Sibling routes probed and stayed refused: struct field address, array element address, closure capturing parameter, parameter address stashed into caller's slot, views through unwrap, view stored into caller slot, and raw buffer pointer returned.

The measurement: `sh build/z64/measure.sh` (it reads `build/z64/zoril`, a copy of `build/zoril` at `ebad9c1`). **Start with:** the root of (d) (`ZirLowerer::exh_useful`'s `rows`/`same`: where their `Unknown` comes from), then zbuild's `parse_manifest` `Frame(cols)` (`build/z64/t/c2.zrl` is the accepted reduction).
    - **Roots read (2026-11-06, the capped session; `build/z64/zoril` at `ebad9c1`, the debug compiler `build/z64/zoril.dbg`, reductions under `build/z64/t/`):** five mechanisms, each shown by a reduction that warns and a one-line twin that does not, and each measured on copies of `src/` (`build/z64/src_exp3` to `src_exp7`, cumulative) or with an experimental compiler (`build/z64/zoril.exp`, built from `build/z64/esrc`; `diff -r src build/z64/esrc` shows the change). Counts are `src/main.zrl` under `--provenance=warn`. The cascade makes them order-dependent: a root's count is what removing it took away after the roots before it were removed.
        1. **(d1) Arena storage named by the address of an argument that only holds a pointer to the arena** (Z-0084). `os_call` names the storage of an arena-tied result by the arena argument's own origins (`arena_store = tao`), and `os_type_has(.., OS_Q_ARENA_REF)` says yes for `&mut ZirLowerer` (a field `arena: *mut ArenaHeader`) and `&Vec<..>` (its `raw`). So a Vec built by `self.f(&rows)` owns `Param(0)` (the struct `self` points at) and `Frame(rows)` (the local Vec), not storage in `*self.arena`. Reduction `build/z64/t/d5.zrl`: 7 warnings, 0 with the experiment. Experiment (`zoril.exp`: `tao` only when the argument's type points straight at an `Arena`/`ArenaHeader`, else what it reaches, `tr`): **zbuild 25 to 0** (the whole of zbuild: `parse_entry(&cols, arena)`'s result owns `Frame(cols)`, `build/z64/t/c3.zrl`); `src` 195 unchanged alone, because (d2) keeps the cascade going. Kind: a rule that removes origins (the argument's address) and adds others (what it reaches); the series rule applies.
        2. **(d2) A value copied out of a referenced Vec reads as an address into that Vec's buffer** (Z-0085). `ParamContents(i)` is everything reached through argument `i` at any depth, and a body-summary tie maps it with `os_through_plus`, which includes the storage a referenced Vec owns. `exh_expand_or(rows)` pushes `row` read out of `rows`, so its result is tied to `ParamContents(1)`, and its self-recursive `return self.exh_expand_or(&out)` makes the caller's result own fresh storage (`Frame(_19)`). In `exh_useful`, `rows`, `spec`, `def` and `same` then hold `Frame` and `Unknown`; the recursive calls store them into `*self`, and `exh_useful`'s summary becomes every parameter into every parameter and a global. That is the root of the 167 "into a global" warnings. Reduction `build/z64/t/d3.zrl` (7 warnings, also under `zoril.exp`); twin `d4.zrl` pushes a fresh copy of each row and gives 0. Experiment `src_exp3` (the same fresh copy at `src/lower_zir/exhaust.zrl:275`): **195 to 185, and "into a global" 167 to 14** (171 "outlives"); `exh_useful`'s summary becomes `ParamContents(0) -> Param(0) ParamContents(0)`. Kind: a rule that adds knowledge (a depth- or type-sensitive tie: a `[mut u32]` element cannot point into a buffer of `[mut u32]` headers in safe code), or the code change at the site.
        3. **(a) confirmed as the next root:** with (d2) removed, the first warning in lowering order is `mono.zrl:296` (`lower_fn` through a `fn` value). `src_exp4` (adds `src_exp2`'s stub): **185 to 16** (6 global, 10 outlives). Kind: as recorded (a summary for a `fn` value or closure, Decision 3).
        4. **(c) 2, (b) 0:** `src_exp5` (neutralises `os_pending.push` and `set_ice_note`): 16 to 14, the two at `driver/module.zrl:1338`; neutralising (b) removes none. (b) is a true store, safe by the program's invariant, and no longer reads as a root of any count.
        5. **(f) An integer popped from a `Vec` carries the `Vec`'s storage** (Z-0087). `Doc::measure`'s `widths.pop()` gives `Option<usize>`, tied to `widths`'s storage (`Frame(_7)`); `cmd.width = w` puts that into `cmd`, and `self.cmds.set(g, cmd)` (`#[stores(val -> self)]`) stores a frame address into `*self`. The integer-result exemption in `os_call` covers a primitive result only, not `Option<usize>`. Reduction (safe, accepted by default, exits 0; twin reading `widths.as_slice()[0]` gives no warning): `struct Cmd { text: [u8], width: usize }`, `#[borrows()] fn at0(cmds: &Vec<Cmd>) -> Cmd { let r = cmds.as_slice()[0]; return Cmd { text: r.text, width: r.width }; }`, `fn measure(cmds: &mut Vec<Cmd>) { let mut widths = Vec::<usize>.new(); widths.push(3 as usize); let w = match widths.pop() { Option.Some(x) => x, Option.None => 0 as usize }; let mut c = at0(cmds); c.width = w; cmds.set(0 as usize, c); }`, called from `main` on a `Vec<Cmd>` holding one `Cmd { text: "ab", .. }` (`build/z64/t/f1.zrl`; two warnings at one site, Z-0066's duplicate). Experiment `src_exp7` (both pops replaced by an index read and a discarded pop): **14 to 8**, every `fmt/` warning gone (`doc.zrl:111`, `:151`, `fmt.zrl:76`, `:122`, `:131`: the cascade through `render`, `file` and `format_source`). Kind: a rule that adds knowledge (an integer carries no address, whatever aggregate wraps it). Sibling of the known `Vec.pop` class above (`own_contents_edge`, which moves a non-integer).
        6. **(e) A literal held by a local makes everything it reaches a global destination** (Z-0086). `ZirLowerer::lower_function`'s summary has `store Static -> Param(0)` (a dozen lowering functions write a literal into `*self`; the first in lowering order is `exh_missing`, through a `ByteVec` in `self.arena`, which (d1) names `Param(0)`), so the driver's local `zir_low` holds `Static`. The later edges that store what `self` reaches into what `self` reaches then have `Static` among their targets, which the call store rule reads as a global. Reduction (safe, accepted by default, exits 0; twin `name: s` gives no warning): `struct W { name: [u8], items: &mut Vec<[u8]> }`, `impl W { fn add(self: &mut W, s: [u8]) { self.items.push(s); } }`, `fn inner(out: &mut Vec<[u8]>, s: [u8]) { let mut w = W { name: "lit", items: out }; w.add(s); }`, `main` calls `inner(&mut v, &data)` with a local array and `Vec` (`build/z64/t/e3.zrl`: "into a global" at the call in `main`). The same class as the three `arena_raw` test programs above. **The last 8 `src` warnings** (`driver/module.zrl:666`, `:713`, `:1141`, `:1188`, `:1475`, `:1603` into a global; `:1362`, `:1386` outlives) are this class by their summaries (`run_body_passes_in_scratch` is the first function in lowering order with a store into a global, through `zir_low`'s `Static`), but not neutralised: replacing `ZirLowerer.new`'s `file_path: ""` changes nothing (the other sites still write literals), and `zoril.exp` does not cover the `ByteVec` route. Kind: a rule that removes origins (a held literal is read-only, so `Static` reached through a value is no store target), or code changes at the sites.

- [x] +safety +decision +AI-D Z-0088 B1.9a Decision 3: what the rules know about a call through a closure or a `fn` value
    > **Created:** 2026-11-06 **Found by:** Z-0064 (the largest over-rejection class; its text says the class needs a task of its own before Z-0065). **Depends:** Z-0075. **Blocks:** Z-0065, unless Z-0075 changes the route. Decision first: the implementation is a task of its own that this one names. **Check-in:** stop and report to the maintainer; the maintainer decides.
    > **Routed (2026-11-06, by Z-0075):** a rule. It decides the flip: the grid's count by root shows 13146 of the 13284 newly refused safe twins involve a closure or `fn` value somewhere in the cell. If this task finds no rule (option 4), stop and reopen the checkpoint for Z-0075's option (c), re-planning the flip, before Z-0065.
    > **Completed:** 2026-11-06 git:9f2b3d8

    - **Now:** a call through a `fn` value (`ZirRvalue.CallPtr`) or a closure is an unknown call (`os_unknown_call_effects` in `src/check_zir.zrl`): it may store any argument anywhere, and its result holds `Unknown`. Decision 3 (Z-0048) deferred the split of `fn` values from closures until a measurement; Z-0064 made it.
    - **Counts (Z-0064, under `--provenance`):** the store grid refuses 13284 safe twins it did not refuse by default, of which by path `fn_val` 3284 and `closure` 2822, by destination `closure_owned_capture` 3300, `closure_call_mut` 2694 and `closure_capture_mut` 1708, by carrier `call_closure` 2080 (example: `arena_vec__arena_struct_bundle__closure__call_mut__arena_reset`, an identity closure on the route). 11 of the 17 test programs that warn: `closure_mutates_owning_capture`, `closure_type_inference`, `infer_from_fn_type`, `closure_owned_capture` (origins), `trait_names`, `closure_captures_everywhere`, `flat_fn_ptr_call_result`, `closure_captures_owning_and_other`, `test_stdlib_iter`, `platform_layer/include/{app,platform}`. In `src`, `Monomorphizer::lower_fn` calls the driver through the `fn` value `self.lower_instance` (`src/mono.zrl`, `return lower(..)`); stubbing that call took 169 of the 185 warnings left once Z-0085's root was removed. The tree has 5 `fn`-value call sites (Z-0049).
    - **Decide between:** (1) a summary for a call whose possible callees are known (a `fn` item passed directly, a closure literal at the call site), joined over them; (2) downward-only closures and `fn` values (one that cannot escape stores only into what its caller passed or it captured); (3) declared effects on a `fn` type, checked where a function becomes the value; (4) no rule: change the compiler's one site, and record the grid's and the tests' refusals as a precision bound in `KNOWN_ISSUES.md` §5. For each, say what the grid and the 11 tests would accept (with a scratch compiler if cheap) and which rules it removes origins by (the series rule). Update Z-0017's note ("`closure`/`fn`-value twins remain `Unknown` by design unless Decision 3 changes") with the outcome.
    - **Measured (2026-11-06; `src/` and `stdlib/` at `c119b54`, unchanged since `ebad9c1`, the compiler Z-0064 measured with (`build/z64/zoril`); scratch compilers under `build/z88/`, each a copy of `src/` with one change in `check_zir.zrl`: `diff -r src build/z88/esrc*`).** Two experiments:
        - **B, a bound for an unknown callee** (`build/z88/zoril.exp`): at a `CallPtr`, the result holds what the arguments and the callee value reach (`os_reach_all` of each, the callee being the closure's environment), plus `Static`, in place of `Unknown`; the memory the call writes gets the same, plus `Static`, in place of `Unknown`. The unknown-call store rule's refusal is unchanged. Reason it can hold: a `fn` value's body was checked by the same rules where it was made, so it returns and stores only what its arguments and its environment reach (the reason `os_unknown_call_effects` already gives for its edges). Not one of options (1) to (4).
        - **A, the known callee's summary** (`build/z88/zoril.a`; with B, `zoril.ab`): a pre-scan of the body finds each local assigned exactly one `FuncAddr` (directly or by a plain copy, never through a `&mut`); a `CallPtr` through such a local applies `os_call` on that function, with the callee value as its `__env`, the last argument (`lower_rv_call_ptr`). This is option (1).
    - **Store grid under `--provenance`** (34320 cells; the modular step stops as in Z-0064's run, the wrapper also putting `--provenance` on its plain pass): Z-0064's run refused 13284 safe twins the default rules accept. **B:** 8034 (5250 accepted again, none newly refused), **0 unsafe cells accepted**; `__closure__` paths 2822 to 1003, `__fn_val__` 3284 to 1045, `call_closure` 2080 to 782, but the destinations where the closure itself stores are almost unchanged: `closure_owned_capture` 3300 to 3300, `closure_capture_mut` 1708 to 1708, `closure_call_mut` 2694 to 2534. **A+B: 1490 unsafe cells accepted** (all `closure_owned_capture`, e.g. `arena_vec__arena_struct_bundle__direct__closure_owned_capture__frame_ends`: the closure owns `h` and stores the view `routed` into it). B alone refuses all 1490 (the unknown-call store rule). The 0 is checked against the 17115 unsafe cells the default rules accept; `--provenance` runs the old rules too, so the others are not a check of the new rules alone (Z-0074).
    - **Why A is unsound as built:** a closure body marks an owned capture as storage of its own frame (`os_stmt`, "an owned capture's storage dies with the closure"), so a view of it cannot leave the body; but a store *into* it is then a store into this frame, which records no summary edge, and the caller applying the summary sees no store at all. And every other capture is read as the `Env` flag, which the summary writes as `Unknown` into a global (`_closure_0`'s `store Unknown -> global` for `|| { *out = routed; }`), so A also refuses more: `tests/language/memory/provenance/closure_env_safe_twin.zrl`, accepted today, is refused under A. **A needs closure bodies summarised over their `__env` parameter first**: the captures as what `__env` reaches (`ParamContents`), an owned capture as storage the environment owns (not the body's frame) while still refusing a view of it that leaves the body.
    - **The 672 test programs:** Z-0064 17 with warnings (67 warnings); **B 14** (50): `flat_fn_ptr_call_result`, `infer_from_fn_type` and `closure_captures_everywhere` accepted, and every `stdlib/iter.zrl` warning gone (`fold`, `Map.next`); A 17 (64); A+B 15 (48). What B leaves among the closure tests is the owned-capture class (`closure_captures_owning_and_other`, `closure_mutates_owning_capture`, `closure_owned_capture`: a closure that owns a `Vec` and pushes to it, `v S={Frame(v), Env}` in the body, so `push`'s self-store reads as a frame address into the environment; reduced in `build/z88/c1.zrl`), and calls into iterator chains in `test_stdlib_iter.zrl` (`sum_all(v.iter())`, `v.iter().map(double).fold(0, add)`: call store rule, not read).
    - **The options against these numbers:** (1) known callees covers every shape the grid generates (each callee is a local bound in the same body) and the closures-that-store destinations B leaves, but only after the `__env` rework, and the measured trap (1490 unsafe programs) says it needs its own design task first. B covers the higher-order shapes (a callee that is a parameter or a field: `apply(f, x)`, the iterator adapters), which (1) never can, and it is the one experiment that kept 0 unsafe accepted. (2) downward-only closures: B gives most of it without restricting the language, since a closure holding a frame address cannot already be stored out (the store rule). (3) declared effects on `fn` types: annotations users would write for what B infers. (4) no rule: Z-0075's fallback to (c).
    - **Recommendation (for the maintainer; not decided):** B and then (1), as three tasks: (i) B as a rule (removes `Unknown`, so the series rule: probes of a call through an `unsafe fn` value, a C function pointer, a `fn` value read from raw memory, a closure capturing a local's address passed down and its result stored out, a `fn` value returning a view of its environment, recursion through a `fn` value, a closure kept in a struct field and called later); (ii) a design task: closure bodies summarised over `__env`, with the 1490 cells as the soundness check and the owned-capture tests as the precision check; (iii) (1) on top of (ii), measured on the grid's closure destinations. With B alone, the flip would still refuse 8034 safe twins (23%) and most closure tests, so (ii) and (iii) are needed before Z-0065. Update Z-0017's note when decided.
    - **Scratch state:** `build/z88/` (local, untracked): `esrc`, `esrc_a`, `esrc_ab` and their compilers, `grid` (B) and `grid_ab` (A+B) with `twin_rejections.txt` and `actual_names.txt` (the unsafe cells accepted), `tests*.txt`, `zp.sh`/`zpab.sh` (the `ZORIL_CC` wrappers).
    - **Decision (the maintainer, 2026-11-06): as recommended.** B as a rule (Z-0092), then closure bodies summarised over `__env` (Z-0093), then option (1), the known callee's summary (Z-0094), all three before Z-0065. Options (2) downward-only closures, (3) declared effects on `fn` types and (4) no rule are not taken. Z-0017's note on `closure`/`fn`-value twins is updated to name these tasks. No code changed; `tm validate` is the only check that ran for the task text; the measurements are above.

- [x] +portability +tests +AI-G Z-0098 A clean build and `make test` pass on Alpine Linux, checked in the `zoril-altest` container
    > **Created:** 2026-11-06 **Found by:** the maintainer, on their Alpine Linux machine (a from-scratch `make` and `make test` at `fdcef82`, `0.1.0-alpha-224-gfdcef82`). Top priority: the development OS is Alpine (BusyBox), and a gate that fails there fails for the maintainer.
    > **Completed:** 2026-11-06 git:7e699fd

    - **Observed (verbatim, the summary lines):** the build through stage1, the fixpoint and the toolchain rebuild all pass; `make test` gives `[FAIL] scenario: preflight-selftest (exit 1; log: build/tests/scenarios/preflight-selftest.log) (19889ms)`, with the detail lines `stub: selfhost ok` (several, one followed by `)`), `SKIP: reject-diff: none of src/summary_pass.zrl src/check_zir.zrl src/verify.zrl src/main.zrl src/interface_print.zrl src/driver src/lower_zir src/parser src/parser.zrl src/ast.zrl stdlib differs from fdcef82461daad6d07e7ebbc962e5fe71100fa3e (PREFLIGHT_BASE=HEAD; PREFLIGHT_REJECT_BASE=<commit> compares with another base)`, and eight `PASS: preflight-selftest:` lines; then `Integration 243 run 242 passed 1 failed 0 skipped` and `Total: 3379 run, 3378 passed, 1 failed, 89 skipped (681.8s)`. Skipped as optional there: md-check (dprint), readme snippets, fuzz-record (rustc), stage0c-minimal (cproc, mcpp). The failing case's own line is not among the lines shown: read the full log.
    - **How to check:** `podman-proxy-send` (`/etc/podman-proxy.conf`: the container `zoril-altest`, image `localhost/lang/zoril-altest`, Alpine 3.24, its `workdir_prefix`, visible from the agent's container at the same path). Clone the commit into a directory under the prefix (`git clone <repo> <workdir_prefix>/zoril`), then `podman-proxy-send run <dir> sh -c 'make && make test'` with the log under the clone's `build/`. A `run` starts a fresh container; `exec` reuses it.
    - **Do:** reproduce at `fdcef82` and at HEAD; find the failing case in `build/tests/scenarios/preflight-selftest.log`; fix its cause (a GNU-only or bash-only construct, a missing tool named as a skip, or a test that depends on the host's git state), never by skipping it silently; check `sh scripts/check_deps.sh test` names what Alpine lacks. Done when a clean clone builds and `make test` reports 0 failed in the container, with the totals in the completion note.
    - **Cause (found 2026-11-06, fixed in git:2f5a778, 7e699fd):** case 11b of `scripts/test_preflight.sh` made its base commit with `git commit-tree` under the caller's git configuration. Where that fails (no identity, as in a fresh container: `fatal: unable to auto-detect email address`; or `commit.gpgsign` with a key it cannot use: the maintainer's config signs with an SSH key), the base was empty, `preflight.sh` fell back to `PREFLIGHT_BASE=HEAD` (the reason in the skip line), compared the tree with itself and passed, so the case failed with `expected the rejection differential to run for a stdlib/-only change (rc=0, ...)`. Not Alpine-specific. The fix makes both base commits with a git configuration of their own (`scratch_base`), and a base that cannot be made fails the case. Case 10 had never run anywhere: the commit it named, `2363209b`, is not in this history; it now builds a base whose `src/check_zir.zrl` differs.
    - **Why the container did not see it:** the image's scdoc is 1.11.4 (Alpine 3.24's, and edge's), so `check_deps.sh --required-only preflight` fails and the whole scenario reports `[SKIP] preflight-selftest: preflight dependencies not satisfied`; the man-page comparison skips too. The maintainer's scdoc is 1.11.5, built from source. Reproduced with an scdoc wrapper reporting 1.11.5 (diagnosis only: the man-page scenario then fails, since the real binary is 1.11.4), and with `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` naming an unusable SSH signing key: the old script fails as observed, the fixed one passes.
    - **Verified in `zoril-altest` (2026-11-06):** a fresh clone of `7e699fd`: `make` passes, `make test` 3382 run, 3382 passed, 0 failed, 90 skipped (without the wrapper); `./scripts/test_preflight.sh` with the wrapper 20 passed, no failure or skip. `fdcef82` in the same container: 3364 run, 0 failed, the scenario skipped. Here: `make test` 3404 run, 0 failed, 86 skipped. `2f5a778` also dropped the script's execute bit (rewritten through a temporary), which `7e699fd` restored: the runner reported exit 126.
    - **Confirmed (2026-11-06, by maintainer):** re-run of `make test` from scratch at HEAD on Alpine Linux passes. Container image items (scdoc 1.11.5, Lua 5.4) remain noted for future image updates.

- [x] +safety +tests +AI-D Z-0092 B1.9a1 A call through a `fn` value returns and stores only what its arguments and environment reach
    > **Created:** 2026-11-06 **Found by:** Z-0088 (experiment B). **Depends:** Z-0088. **Before:** Z-0065. **Check-in:** stop for review after it. Accepts more and removes `Unknown`: the rejection differential and the series rule apply.
    > **Completed:** 2026-11-06 git:2e5075b

    - **Now:** at a `ZirRvalue.CallPtr` (`os_rvalue` in `src/check_zir.zrl`) the result's sets get `ORIGIN_UNKNOWN`, and `os_unknown_call_effects` writes `Unknown` into the memory the call may write (`os_add_flag(c, tsv, ORIGIN_UNKNOWN)`). `Unknown` counts as frame-bound, so `fn apply(f: fn(i32) -> i64, x: i32) -> i64 { return f(x); }` is refused by the return rule (`tests/language/other/flat_fn_ptr_call_result.zrl` under `--provenance=warn`), as are `Iterator.fold` and `Map.next` in `stdlib/iter.zrl`.
    - **The rule:** a `fn` value's body was checked by the same rules where it was made, so a call through it returns, and stores, only what its arguments and its environment (the callee value, passed as `__env`) reach, plus `Static`. State it in the design note §3, beside _Closure environments_, with the boundary: which callees were not checked by these rules. Those are `unsafe fn` bodies (checked by these rules since Z-0063, only `Raw` exempt: found by this task, so the rule holds for them), C function pointers (`c_abi`, unchecked code: the same trust a direct call to a bare `extern` gets today) and a `fn` value read from raw memory (made in `unsafe`). Say for each whether the rule still holds or the call keeps `Unknown`.
    - **Measured (Z-0088, `build/z88/esrc`: the result and the written memory bounded by `os_reach_all` of each argument and the callee, plus `Static`; the unknown-call store rule's pairwise refusal unchanged):** the store grid refuses 5250 fewer safe twins (13284 to 8034 newly refused), none newly, and accepts 0 unsafe cells; the test programs with warnings go from 17 to 14 (`flat_fn_ptr_call_result`, `infer_from_fn_type`, `closure_captures_everywhere`; every `stdlib/iter.zrl` warning).
    - **Probes (the series rule):** a call through an `unsafe fn` value, a C function pointer, a `fn` value read from raw memory; a closure capturing a local's address passed to a callee that calls it and stores the result out (must be refused); a `fn` value returning a view of its environment; recursion through a `fn` value; a closure kept in a struct field and called later (`Map`); a `fn` value returning a fresh `Box` or `Vec` (what the result owns). Accept probes: `apply`, `fold` with a non-capturing function.
    - **Verification:** `make test`, `make preflight-changed`, the rejection differential, the store grid under `--provenance` (0 unsafe accepted; list the twins it accepts by axis), `sh scripts/run_safety_matrix.sh`.
    - **Implemented (2026-11-06, git:dca9bdc, e6b14ac):** `os_rvalue`'s `CallPtr` arm gives the result `os_fn_value_result`: `O` is the reach of every argument and the environment, plus `Static`; `os_unknown_call_effects` takes the stored-value flag as a parameter (`Static` for a `CallPtr`, `Unknown` still for a summary-less direct call). The rule and its boundary are in the design note §3, _Calls through a `fn` value_. **One change from experiment B:** an owning result's `S` is the frame's fresh storage (`owner`) plus every argument's and the environment's reach. B left `S` empty, which accepts `fn view_of_fresh(f: fn() -> Vec<i32>) -> [i32] { let mut v = f(); v.push(1); return v.as_slice(); }`, a view of a freed buffer, under the new rules (a compiler without the two `vs` lines, `build/z92/zoril-novs`, prints no `(return rule)`); `fn_value_owner_view_returned_refused.zrl` pins it. With the fresh storage alone (`build/z92/zoril-owner`), `v.push(1)` on such a `Vec` is accepted, but a `Vec` a closure builds in a captured parameter's arena would read as fresh, against `os_call`'s arena rule, so `S` keeps the arguments' reach and that `push` stays refused, as it was under `Unknown`.
    - **The boundary, as found:** `unsafe fn` bodies are checked by the new rules (`src/driver/module.zrl`, since Z-0063; `unsafe_fn_body_checked_refused.zrl`), so the rule holds for an `unsafe fn` value too. C function pointers: at least the trust of a bare `extern` (`{Static}`), plus the arguments' reach. A `fn` value read from raw memory: the `unsafe` read is the claim. All three probed, accepted with parameters and refused with a local's address.
    - **Probes** (`tests/language/memory/provenance/fn_value_*.zrl`, each one checked against a base compiler built from HEAD's `check_zir.zrl`, `build/z92/zoril-base`, which refuses the accept test's bodies by the return rule five times): accepted, `apply`, `fold` over a range with `add_usize`, `first(pick, &K, &K)`, an `unsafe fn` value, a `fn` value read from raw memory, a C function pointer; refused, a closure's environment view returned, a fresh `Vec`'s view returned, a local's address given back by an `unsafe fn` value, a C function pointer and a raw-memory `fn` value, recursion through a `fn` value (`walk`/`step`), a closure kept in a struct field, a closure that captured a local's address handed to a callee that stores its result (call store rule). The golden `fn_value_call.zrl` and `closure_env_gap45.zrl` now read `Static` where they read `Unknown` (**an expected result changed**, by design: both stay tied to the frame). `gap_16`, `gap_21`, `gap_26`, `gap_29`, `gap_52`, `gap_55` give the same refusals under `--provenance` as before.
    - **Store grid under `--provenance`** (`ZORIL_CC=build/z92/zp.sh sh scripts/check_store_grid.sh --dir build/z92/grid`; the modular step stops as in Z-0064's and Z-0088's runs): **0 unsafe cells accepted** (`actual_names.txt` empty); 8034 safe twins newly refused (Z-0064: 13284), the same set as experiment B cell for cell, so the `S` change reaches no grid cell. The 5250 accepted again, by path: `fn_val` 2239, `closure` 1819, 144 each for `direct`, `helper`, `two_level_helper`, `method`, `self_method`, `trait_method`, `mutual_rec`, `param_65`; by destination: `deref_out` 762, 722 each for `ten_copies`, `local_field_assigned_out`, `field_out`, `call_struct_mut`, `call_mut`, then `return_val` 718, `closure_call_mut` 160; by carrier: `call_closure` 1298, then 288 each for the others. None newly refused.
    - **Warnings** (`--provenance=warn`, `build/z92/measure.sh`, base against new): test programs 17 to 14 (`flat_fn_ptr_call_result`, `infer_from_fn_type`, `closure_captures_everywhere`, as Z-0088 measured), 67 to 50 warnings; `src/main.zrl` 195 to 195, `tools/zbuild` 25, zilc and zman 0. `test_stdlib_iter.zrl` still warns 7 times, all from the call store rule at its call sites (not read further).
    - **Found, not fixed (new tasks):** `gap_65` (Z-0095), an `unsafe fn` made into a `fn` value with no `unsafe`; `gap_64` (Z-0096), a closure storing a frame address into a global through a callee that calls it, a sibling of `gap_52`, not seen at the call with or without this change, since the change does not touch destinations; Z-0097, a pre-existing over-refusal: an unknown call's edge into a `fn` argument's environment (`ParamContents` of a `fn` parameter) is applied at the caller to a `fn` item, `Static`, as a global destination, so `first(pick, &x, &y)` with locals is refused by the call store rule, and so are `walk(step, &x, n)` and `run(&m, &x)` (the base compiler gives the same refusals).
    - **Checks:** `make test` 3404 run, 3404 passed, 0 failed, 82 skipped (after e6b14ac; the first run, after dca9bdc alone, had 5 failures, all fixed in e6b14ac: the two goldens, two redundant `unsafe { }` markers and the §5 line counts); `make preflight-changed` passed: 43 mutations of the changed files all caught, the clean-tree build and fixpoint, `[PASS] reject-diff: 0 newly accepted, 0 newly refused, of 69123 programs` against `fdcef82`; `sh scripts/run_safety_matrix.sh` 0 mismatches (485 probes with `gap_64`, `gap_65`). The fuzz record (`bench/fuzz/README.md`) is stale: `src/` changed.
    - **Awaiting review:** re-run the `fn_value_*` tests and the two goldens (`./scripts/test.sh tests/language/memory/provenance/fn_value_*.zrl tests/language/memory/origins/fn_value_call.zrl`, `sh scripts/test_origins.sh`); take the two `vs` lines out of `os_fn_value_result` and watch `fn_value_owner_view_returned_refused.zrl` fail; re-run the grid under `--provenance` (0 unsafe accepted, 8034 newly refused twins); probe the sibling routes: a `fn` value returning a `Box`/`HashMap`/`String` built in an argument's arena, a closure returning a view of an owned capture, a method value, a generic `fn` value instance.
    - **Reviewed (2026-11-06, another session; scratch files in `build/z92r/`):** the 11 `fn_value_*` tests and both goldens pass, and `sh scripts/test_origins.sh` passes. With the two `vs` lines taken out of `os_fn_value_result` (`build/z92r/zoril-novs`), `fn_value_owner_view_returned_refused.zrl` loses its `(return rule)` and only the old rule's error is left, so the test sees the defect. The grid under `--provenance` (`ZORIL_CC=build/z92r/zp.sh sh scripts/check_store_grid.sh --dir build/z92r/grid`, with `build/zoril` at HEAD): 0 unsafe cells accepted, 8034 safe twins newly refused, the same set as `build/z92/grid` cell for cell; the modular step stops on the same 125 stale entries. Sibling routes, each run against `build/zoril` and `build/z92/zoril-base`: a `Vec`, `String` or `HashMap` a `fn` value builds in a local arena, pushed into a parameter or returned, is refused by the new rules (as at a direct call); a `Box` built from a frame view through a `fn` value, returned, is refused; a closure returning a view of an owned capture is refused inside its body, called locally or returned; a method value (`S::get`), a generic instance (`id::<i32>`) and a generic higher-order function instantiated at `&i32` (`apply::<&i32>(same, &y)`), each handed a local's address, are refused by the return rule; each of their safe twins with a parameter is accepted where the base compiler refused it. Kept as tests: `fn_value_method_frame_arg_refused.zrl`, `fn_value_generic_frame_arg_refused.zrl`, `fn_value_arena_owner_pushed_refused.zrl` (its refusal rests on the result's `O`, so it holds with `S` reduced to the fresh storage, `build/z92/zoril-owner`: it is not a second test of the `vs` lines) and `fn_value_sibling_accepted.zrl` (a method value and the arena bodies; the base compiler refuses them four times; the generic instance is left out because of `gap_66`). Not kept: a `Box` built through a `fn` item from a parameter's view, which the new rules accept but the old rules refuse (`region escape: ... a call through a fn value`, `build/z92r/s_box.zrl`). No defect found in the change.
    - **Found in the review (not Z-0092's change; the base compiler gives the same results):** a generic instance named with type arguments and used as a value lowers to nothing, so `let f: fn(i32) -> i32 = id::<i32>; f(3)` calls through an uninitialized slot and faults, from safe code (`gap_66`, Z-0100; the first version of `fn_value_sibling_accepted.zrl` crashed with exit 139). The caller-side refusals in the arena twins (`f(&a, mk_vec, &mut v)`) are Z-0097's class, and that class also carries up through a summary (a finding recorded on Z-0097).
    - **Closed by the review (2026-11-06):** the change stands as committed in dca9bdc and e6b14ac. The review added tests and a probe only (0c18a79 `gap_66`, 2e5075b the four tests); no file under `src/`, `stdlib/` or `tools/` changed, so `make preflight-changed` was not re-run (the implementer's run is above). Checks: `make test` 3412 run, 3412 passed, 0 failed, 88 skipped; `sh scripts/run_safety_matrix.sh` 486 probes, 0 mismatches (443 passed, 43 known gaps); `sh scripts/check_safety_model.sh` passes; the grid as above. - [x] +safety +tests +AI-D Z-0095 An `unsafe fn` made into a `fn` value needs `unsafe`
    > **Created:** 2026-11-06 **Found by:** Z-0092 (the `unsafe fn` value probe). **Check-in:** stop for review after it: a fix to a safety rule. Independent of the provenance series; first after Z-0092's review because it is a hole in the default mode, reachable from safe code.

    - **Now:** an `unsafe fn` coerces to a plain `fn` type with no `unsafe` block, at every conversion site probed: a `let` with a `fn` type, an argument (`call(rd)`), a struct field (`H { f: rd }`), a return (`return rd;`). The value is then called from safe code, so the precondition `unsafe_policy.md` §1 makes the caller's is never marked: `bench/safety_matrix/probes/gap_65_unsafe_fn_as_fn_value.zrl` hands `unsafe fn rd(p: *i32) -> i32 { return *p; }` a forged pointer from safe code and faults.
    - **Decide, then do:** refuse the coercion outside `unsafe` (the value then carries the claim, as a raw pointer read does), or give `unsafe fn` its own `fn` type, callable only in `unsafe`. Probe every conversion site, a generic instance and a method; flip `gap_65` to `compile_reject` with its Known gaps entry and `doc/SAFETY.md` §6 class (L) removed. `fn_value_result_accepted.zrl` and `fn_value_frame_arg_returned_refused.zrl` make `pick_unsafe` and `pick` values with no marker and change with it.
    - **Decided (2026-11-06): refuse the coercion outside `unsafe`.** It is the rule a `fn` value read from raw memory already has (the `unsafe` block is the claim about the value, and calls through it are ordinary), it is one check at the one place every conversion site goes through (`ZirLowerer.fn_value` in `src/lower_zir/expr.zrl`: a function, a method and a top-level item named as a value), and it needs no new type. An `unsafe fn` type callable only in `unsafe` is more precise (the claim stays at each call, not at the one place the value is made) but touches the parser, the type checker, monomorphization, `.zri` interfaces and the stage1 closure: a language feature, its own task if it is wanted.
    - **Implemented:** `fn_value` reports "an unsafe function made into a value requires unsafe block" for an `unsafe fn` that is not `extern` outside `unsafe` (an `unsafe fn` body counts as `unsafe`, as for a direct call). `src/`, zilc and zbuild build under the new rule with no change: nothing in the stage1 closure makes an `unsafe fn` into a value. Probed (`build/z95/*.zrl`, each with `build/zoril`): refused unmarked, a `let` with a `fn` type, a `let` with none, an argument, a struct field, a `return`, an assignment, a tuple, an array, both arms of an `if`, a closure returning it, a method (`S::get`); accepted, `unsafe { rd }` in a `let`, `unsafe { call(rd) }`, `return rd;` inside an `unsafe fn`, a safe `fn` item. A generic `unsafe fn` instance cannot be probed yet: as a value it lowers to nothing (`gap_66`, Z-0100), and chosen by the expected type it is refused ("unknown type `T`"). `gap_65` is `compile_reject`, cited with `tests/error_reporting/err_unsafe_fn_value_outside_unsafe.zrl` (six sites) under the row _Safe Function Dereferencing a Raw Pointer Argument_; its Known gaps entry and `doc/SAFETY.md` class (L) are removed (the former (M), `gap_66`, is now (L)); `unsafe_policy.md` §1, `surface_syntax.md`'s `unsafe fn` entry, `safety_model.md`'s boundary paragraph and the design note §3 state the rule. The two provenance tests mark their values `unsafe { .. }`. With the check taken out (`build/z92r/zoril-novs`, the source before it) the error test compiles with no error.
    - **Found, not fixed (the structural twin; Z-0101):** a bare `extern` function made into a plain `fn` value needs no `unsafe`, and safe code calls it through the value: `let f: fn(*mut u8, i32, usize) -> *mut u8 = memset;` passed to `fn call(f: ..) { f(16 as *mut u8, 0, 8); }` faults (`build/z95/x_extern_call.zrl`, exit 139), while the direct call is refused ("call to extern function requires unsafe block"). An `extern "C" fn` with a body does not convert to a plain `fn` type (type mismatch), so it is not this class.
    - **Committed:** bfd38a8 (the check, the tests, the probe flip and the docs); 85e4c8c records `gap_67` for Z-0101.
    - **Checks:** `make test` 3415 run, 3415 passed, 0 failed, 86 skipped at bfd38a8 (the first run failed only on figures the change moves: `md-check` on `matrix.md`, and the §5 line counts in `doc/SAFETY.md` and `unsafe_policy.md`; fixed before the commit); 3415 passed, 0 failed, 88 skipped with `gap_67` added. `make preflight-changed` passed: 14 mutations of the changed files all caught, the clean-tree build and the fixpoint, `[PASS] reject-diff: 0 newly accepted, 2 newly refused, of 69126 programs against c436fcf`, the two being `gap_65` itself, whole-program and under zbuild. `sh scripts/run_safety_matrix.sh` 0 mismatches (444 of 487 passed, 43 known gaps with `gap_66` and `gap_67`); `sh scripts/check_safety_model.sh` passes. The fuzz record (`bench/fuzz/README.md`) is stale: `src/` changed.
    - **Awaiting review:** re-run `./scripts/test.sh tests/error_reporting/err_unsafe_fn_value_outside_unsafe.zrl tests/language/memory/provenance/fn_value_*.zrl` and `sh scripts/run_safety_matrix.sh` (`gap_65` refused); take the check out of `ZirLowerer.fn_value` and watch the error test and `gap_65` fail; probe the sibling routes again with a fresh eye (a value made inside a closure body inside `unsafe`, a nested module's `unsafe fn` named by path, a `pub unsafe fn` read through a `.zri` under zbuild, a trait method declared `unsafe`); and judge the decision above (refuse the coercion, not a separate `unsafe fn` type).
    - **Review 1 (2026-11-06, another session):** the re-runs and the take-the-check-out run pass as recorded; the closure-body, imported and nested module, `.zri` under zbuild, trait method, trait default method and aggregate routes are refused outside `unsafe` and accepted inside. Findings, each re-run here before acting:
        - **Bug (fixed):** the check exempted `is_extern`, so an `unsafe extern fn` (either spelling, `unsafe extern fn` or `extern unsafe fn`) made into a value in safe code was accepted, and the call through it faulted (`build/z95r/b1.zrl`, exit 139). The exemption was copied from the direct-call check, where the `extern`-call check covers it; nothing covers it for a value. Removed; the error test gains the `unsafe extern` line and a closure returning `rd`, and with the exemption put back (`build/z95r/zoril-exempt`) it reports 7 of its 8 errors.
        - **Generic `unsafe fn` with turbofish** (`id::<*i32>` outside `unsafe`, accepted): reproduced, and the ZIL has no function address at all, so it is `gap_66` (Z-0100), not a route around this check. Z-0100 now says its lowering must go through `fn_value`.
        - **The decision** (the coercion launders the precondition): a closure `|p: *i32| -> i32 { return unsafe { rd(p) }; }` and a safe wrapper `fn wrap(p: *i32) -> i32 { return unsafe { rd(p) }; }` compile today and fault the same way (`build/z95r/d_closure.zrl`, `d_wrapper.zrl`), so forbidding the coercion and requiring the closure adds syntax, not soundness: any `unsafe` block's author owes soundness for every input of the safe code around it. Kept. The wording overclaimed ("the claim that every call through the value meets the precondition", which the maker of the value cannot know): `unsafe_policy.md` §1, `safety_model.md`'s boundary paragraph, the design note and the comment in `fn_value` now say the block claims the function is safe to call with every argument the plain `fn` type admits, the obligation of a safe wrapper. A separate `unsafe fn` type, which keeps the claim at each call, is deferred as Z-0102.
        - **Checks after the fix** (652d142): `make test` 3415 run, 3415 passed, 0 failed, 88 skipped (the first run failed only on the §5 compiler line count, which the longer comment moved; fixed in the commit); `make preflight-changed` passed: 14 mutations caught, the clean-tree build and the fixpoint, `[PASS] reject-diff: 0 newly accepted, 2 newly refused, of 69127 programs against c436fcf` (`gap_65`, whole-program and zbuild), the `--checked` suite 1263 passed; `sh scripts/run_safety_matrix.sh` 0 mismatches (444 of 487). `gap_67`, a bare `extern`, stays open for Z-0101.
    - **Awaiting review (round 2):** re-run `./scripts/test.sh tests/error_reporting/err_unsafe_fn_value_outside_unsafe.zrl` and take `is_unsafe` out of `fn_value`'s condition, or put `!p_func.is_extern` back (it then reports 7 of 8 errors); probe the `unsafe extern` declaration in another module and through a `.zri` under zbuild; read the new wording in `unsafe_policy.md` §1 (the safe-wrapper obligation) and judge whether it states the rule the code enforces.

- [x] +testinfra +decision +AI-D Z-0103 T0 Test efficiency: the problem, the options, and a prioritised plan
    > **Created:** 2026-11-06 **Asked by:** the maintainer. **Output:** a ranked list in this task's text, with Z-0104 to Z-0114 reordered, split or dropped to match it. Any new task gets its own ID. **Check-in:** stop and report the ranking before starting any of them.

    - **Problem.** Testing takes most of a task's time. `make test` runs 3347 items in 230 s on 16 cores (`build/make_test.log`, 2026-11-06): 34 gates, then 6 groups (1283 per-file tests under `tests/` and `corpus/`, plus 48 scenario scripts). The 4-vCPU cloud VM takes about five minutes. `make preflight-changed` adds a clean-tree build, the self-hosting fixpoint, mutations of the changed files, a second pass over the whole suite under `--checked` (`scripts/test_checked.sh`), the bench thresholds, and, when the checkers changed, the rejection differential, which builds the base compiler from scratch (about 4 min). Sessions run both several times per task, including after documentation-only and task-file-only edits.
    - **Background: Session analysis figures (`zoril_agentic_sessions_summary_nov2026.md`, 2026-11-04 to 2026-11-06 across 136 commits and 18 tasks):**
        - _Testing overhead:_ `make test` takes 222–233 s (~3.8 min) per full run on 4 vCPUs (aborted/fail-fast: ~112–118 s), averaging ~2.1 full runs per task (~7.5–8.5 min total per task).
        - _Preflight overhead:_ `make preflight-changed` takes 800–900 s (~13–15 min) when the rejection differential runs against 69,120+ programs (rejection diff alone is 620 s, clean-tree build 65 s, checked suite 52 s, mutations ~25 s, benchmarks ~36 s); takes 120–129 s (~2.1 min) when the differential is skipped (e.g. stdlib-only). Averaged ~1.2 runs per compiler/safety task. Combined verification represents 65%–80% of active machine compute time per task.
        - _Documentation and Taskmark share:_ 92 of 136 commits (67.6%) were documentation-only (33 recording findings/verification/awaiting review, 14 `tm sweep` rotations, 12 active backlog adjustments, 22 review feedback, 11 safety model/spec). Active agent wall-clock time spent on docs is ~1.0–1.5 min per sweep and ~3.5–6.0 min per task findings writeup (~18%–25% of active interaction time). Full test/preflight runs triggered after pure documentation or task-file edits were a primary source of idle turnaround time.
    - **Brainstorm** (to rank; the numbers come from Z-0105):
        - _Measure first._ No per-item timing exists: `test.sh` prints counts, `preflight.sh` prints only `reject-diff: N s`. Z-0104 adds timing; Z-0105 measures with it.
        - _Change-scoped runs._ Map each path to the checks it can affect (Z-0106). A change only under `doc/tasks/` needs `tm validate` and `md-check`. Other `.md` changes add the doc-snippet, md-links and comment gates. A `tests/` file needs its own test and the strength gate. `scripts/` needs that script's self-test. `src/`, `stdlib/`, `rt/` and `tools/` need the full run. `preflight-changed` reports `[SKIP]` and exits when nothing under `src/ stdlib/ tools/ rt/` changed from its base.
        - _Stop re-testing an unchanged tree._ `build/full-test.stamp` already records the tree the last whole passing run saw. `make test` on an identical tree could say so and stop, with `FORCE=1` to rerun. Paths that cannot affect a result (`doc/tasks/`) could be left out of the stamp's hash.
        - _A per-item result cache._ Key each per-file test on its file, its imports, the compiler, zilc, the runtime and the toolchain fingerprint. A change to `src/` rebuilds the compiler and invalidates everything, which is correct. A change to tests or docs reruns only what it touched.
        - _Gates that check frozen or rarely changed code._ The seven `stage0c-*` gates (about 400 items) test a seed that changes only by a recorded fix (`AGENTS.md`). `mutation-self-test` builds four mutant compilers (about 90 s, the slowest gate) to test `mutation-check.sh` itself. Most of the 48 scenarios test our own scripts: runner-output, preflight-selftest, comment-noise-selftest and the like. Each could run only when its subject changed, plus always in `make preflight`.
        - _Work done twice._ The safety matrix runs whole-program and under zbuild (443 probes each, required by "two implementations tested against each other"). `test_checked.sh` reruns the whole suite. The reject differential's base compiler is rebuilt on every `preflight-changed` against the same base: cache it in `build/` keyed by the base commit. `selfhost-check` and the reject differential may each build a compiler from a clean tree.
        - _Redundant and obsolete tests._ Find the duplicates: same normalised ZIL, same diagnostic for the same construct, tests of retired features or of bugs whose code is gone (Z-0107).
        - _Fewer processes per test._ Each run test spawns zoril, zilc, qbe, as and ld. Measure fixed cost against compile time. Then weigh merging many run tests into one file, or a compiler mode that compiles several inputs in one process, against losing failure isolation. A `compile fail` or `EXPECT:` test cannot be merged (Z-0108).
        - _Scheduling._ Start the longest items first, using the recorded timings (gates are in a fixed display order today). Find the serial parts: the preflight steps after mutation run one after another, the stage0c `make` targets run without `-j`, and scenarios loop serially inside (Z-0109).
        - _Progress output._ The current output looks hung during a long item: a gate prints only when it ends, and lines print in plan order, so one slow gate holds back every line after it. Show what is running as it starts, a heartbeat at least every 10 s, and fast items collapsed into one count line (Z-0110).
        - _Git in the tests._ About 41 scripts call git. Some test our own git-based tools (build-info, full-test-stamp, the preflight base, stray-files, the reject differential). Some use `git ls-files` only to list files. Some build scratch repositories (`git init` in `test_build_info.sh`, `test_full_test_stamp.sh`, `dist_user_paths.sh`, `check_clean_tree.sh --selftest`, `check_stray_files.sh --selftest`). Inventory them, and keep git only where the thing under test is a git feature (Z-0111).
        - _The workflow rule itself._ `AGENTS.md` asks for `make test` and `make preflight-changed` on every change to `src/`, `stdlib/` or `tools/`, but does not say when within a task. Run them once, after the last code edit. A task-file-only or completion-note commit after a passing run reruns nothing (Z-0112's wording, once the tiers exist).
        - _The author and the reviewer run the same suites on the same tree._ A check-in's reviewer rightly reruns everything rather than take the author's word, so each safety task pays for its full verification twice. Results depend on inputs, not on who ran them: cache each item's verdict keyed by a hash of its inputs, which the reviewer computes from its own build. The reviewer then reruns a random sample, everything the change touched, and every failure, and treats any disagreement as a finding (Z-0113). The differential is the largest single cost and the easiest to key (Z-0114).
        - _Iterating on failures._ `make retest-failed` reruns only the items that failed last time, from `--results`. Fail-fast is the default while iterating.
    - **Do:** from the figures above, rank the ideas by expected time saved per session against cost and risk to what the tests prove, and record the ranking here. Then reorder, split or drop Z-0104 to Z-0114. A test or gate removed by any of them is listed with the reason, never removed silently.
    - **Measured (2026-11-06, 16 cores, `build/zoril` at 160aee0; enough to rank, the full profile stays Z-0105's):**
        - _F1, the 16-core critical path is one gate._ The gate logs of the last `make test` (their mtimes against the run's first log, 20:04:44) show `mutation-self-test` ending at 219 s of a 230 s run; every other gate ends by 121 s (`safety-matrix-zbuild`), then `stage0c-minimal` 87 s, `reject-diff-selftest` 86 s, `safety-model` 84 s, `safety-matrix` 82 s. The pool already starts it first (`scripts/test.sh` `--pool`), so scheduling cannot shorten it; only not running it, or a cheaper one, can. On 4 vCPUs the total CPU time decides instead, and Z-0105 measures it.
        - _F2, per-file tests are cheap._ `scripts/test.sh --verbose tests/functional/structs/stack_bloat_baseline.zrl`: the whole pipeline (zoril, zilc, qbe, as, ld, run) is 34 ms. 1283 such tests are under a minute of CPU, about 4 s of wall time at `-j16`. Merging them (Z-0108) can save seconds, not minutes.
        - _F3, the front end's cost is the stdlib, not the program._ `zoril --emit-zil` on a store-grid cell takes 33 ms; a program that only imports `vec.zrl` and `mem.zrl` and returns 0 takes 31 ms; one with no imports takes 2 ms. The differential runs both sides over 68,640 grid programs (`lua scripts/fuzz/store_grid.lua --generate-all`, 2.3 s) and the probes: about 138,000 front-end runs, about 4,500 CPU-seconds, about 94% of it re-reading the same two stdlib modules. Its shell loop costs little beside that (2,000 cells: 71 s with the `run_front.sh` loop body, about 66 s of it in zoril). The base compiler's build is the rest of its 612 s.
        - _F4, the harness's own dependencies._ `scripts/test.sh` needs `flock`, `mkfifo`, `xargs -P`, `setsid` and `read -t`, and says that "this repo's /bin/sh is bash"; `flock` and `setsid` are util-linux, not on the BSDs. Four scripts are bash (`check_safety_model.sh`, `check_md_links.sh`, `fuzz_safe.sh`, `test_check_no_new_deps.sh`). Lua is on the preflight path (the differential's grid), not only the fuzzer's. The stdlib already has what a runner needs (`proc.zrl` spawn/wait/kill, `fsys.zrl` `list_dir` and `acquire_file_lock`, `time.zrl` `monotonic_ns`, `sha256.zrl`), and `tools/zbuild` already spawns the toolchain from Zoril. The pinned stage1's stdlib has no `proc.zrl` or `fsys.zrl`.
        - _The dependency task:_ it was in the incubator repository this one spun out of (dropping Lua: `sh`/`awk` for glue, Zoril for anything larger). The maintainer supplied it, and it is filed as Z-0118 in `future.tm.md`, updated against this tree. Since the incubator, the store grid (`scripts/fuzz/store_grid.lua`) has put Lua on the differential's path, and `gen.lua` has grown from 980 to 3,666 lines.
    - **Ranking** (time saved per session, against cost and the risk to what the tests prove):
        1. _Change-scoped tiers and the workflow rule_ (Z-0106 decides, Z-0115 implements, Z-0112 words it). About two thirds of the commits change only docs or task files, and each can skip a 4-minute `make test` and a `preflight-changed`. The self-tests of our own scripts move into the tier of their subject; `mutation-self-test` alone takes the 16-core wall time from about 230 s to about 125 s (F1). Small cost. Low risk while an unlisted path selects everything and `make preflight` runs everything.
        2. _The differential's caches_ (Z-0114). The largest single cost: 612 s of an 800 to 900 s preflight, paid by both the author and the reviewer against the same base. Caching the base compiler and the base side's verdicts removes its build and half of its front-end runs. Small cost (keys over files the script already names). Low risk, with the planted-invalidation test.
        3. _The front end's fixed cost_ (Z-0116, new, a measurement and a decision). 94% of a differential front-end run is the stdlib (F3), and the same holds wherever a small program imports it: the safety matrix in both modes, `test-strength`, the fuzzer. Checking many programs in one process, or reusing the stdlib's checked interface, would take the rest of the differential from minutes to seconds. It is a `src/` change, so it costs more and carries the risk that one program's state leaks into the next. Two implementations tested against each other (batch against one process per program, over the probes and the grid) keep that risk visible.
        4. _A test runner in Zoril_ (Z-0117, new, a decision). This answers the maintainer's question whether to rewrite the harness. Recommendation: replace the dispatcher, the pool and the per-file pipeline of `scripts/test.sh` (where `flock`, `mkfifo`, `setsid`, `xargs -P` and `read -t` live, F4) with `tools/ztest`. The gates and scenarios stay POSIX `sh` scripts that it runs, behind the same output contract (`scripts/test_runner_output.sh` as the oracle, both runners compared over one tree while both exist). Timing, progress, the result cache and tier selection then live in the runner, written once. Medium cost, done in pieces. The risk is a runner built by the compiler under test: Z-0117 decides what builds it (see the task).
        5. _Timing_ (Z-0104, then Z-0105's profile). Cheap, and Z-0105, Z-0109 and Z-0110 need it. It stays small in `sh` (one TSV line per item) so that it does not grow the code Z-0117 may replace.
        6. _Git in the tests_ (Z-0111). Mostly reliability, not time. It also feeds the dependency work (Z-0118).
        7. _A result cache for the author and the reviewer_ (Z-0113, a decision). The largest saving after the tiers, and the most risk: an undeclared input passes a stale verdict. It comes after the tiers and the differential cache, which cover most of the saving with fewer inputs to declare, and is implemented in the runner if Z-0117 builds one.
        8. _Scheduling_ (Z-0109). The pool already starts the slowest items first (F1). What is left: the stage0c `make` targets with `-j` and the preflight steps side by side. Moderate on 4 vCPUs, little on 16.
        9. _Progress output_ (Z-0110). Saves agent idle time, not machine time. Waits for Z-0117, so it is written once, in the runner that stays.
        10. _Redundant tests_ (Z-0107). Narrowed to gates, scenarios and the stage0c gates (F2: per-file duplicates cost seconds).
        11. _Fewer, larger test files_ (Z-0108). Dropped from the series: by F2 the most it can save is about 4 s per run on 16 cores, for lost failure isolation. Moved to `future.tm.md` with this reason, in case Z-0105 finds a 4-vCPU figure that says otherwise.
    - **Dependency rule for the series** (asked by the maintainer, 2026-11-06, with Z-0118 in view): until Z-0117 decides, no task adds machinery to `scripts/test.sh` beyond Z-0104's timing line, and no task adds a dependency or a new use of Lua, bash, `flock`, `setsid` or git to the test path. A cache or a tier table is kept as plain files (TSV) that a `sh` script and a Zoril program can both read.
    - **New order:** Z-0104, Z-0105, Z-0106, Z-0115, Z-0112, Z-0114, Z-0116, Z-0117, Z-0111, Z-0113, Z-0109, Z-0110, Z-0107. Z-0108 is deferred; Z-0118 is new in `future.tm.md`. No test or gate is removed by this plan. The tiers move gates out of the default `make test`, and `make preflight` keeps all of them.
    - **Awaiting review:** the maintainer reads the ranking and the new order and decides on Z-0117's direction (a Zoril runner, with gates and scenarios kept in `sh`). To re-check F1 to F3: the gate log mtimes under `build/gates/` after a `make test`; `scripts/test.sh --verbose tests/functional/structs/stack_bloat_baseline.zrl`; `time build/zoril -I stdlib=stdlib --emit-zil -o /dev/null P` for a store-grid cell, for a file holding only `import "vec.zrl"; import "mem.zrl";` and a `main` that returns 0, and for one without the imports.

- [x] +testinfra +tests +AI-G Z-0119 T1c A nested run does not overwrite the outer run's timing files
    > **Created:** 2026-11-06 by the review of Z-0104 and Z-0105. **Before:** Z-0120, Z-0115, Z-0109, Z-0110 (each reads these files).
    > **Completed:** 2026-11-06 git:17baac6

    - **Found (2026-11-06):** `scripts/test_preflight.sh` runs `./scripts/preflight.sh` in this checkout without `PREFLIGHT_TIMINGS` in every case but 9b, so each run truncates `build/preflight-timings.tsv` and writes `0` lines into it. Reproduced: `printf '999\tsentinel\n' > build/preflight-timings.tsv; sh scripts/test_preflight.sh; cat build/preflight-timings.tsv` prints six `0` lines (mutation, selfhost, calibrate, reject-diff, checked, bench) and no sentinel. In a real `preflight-changed`, the `checked` step runs `scripts/test_checked.sh`, which runs the scenarios (`preflight-selftest` is not in its `TR_EXCLUDE`), so the outer file loses its first steps mid-run. `test_checked.sh` also sets no `TR_WORK` of its own, so it overwrites `build/tests/timings.tsv` (and `build/tests/items`) from the last `make test`.
    - **Why Z-0105's fix did not hold (git:2884e76, ad32959):** `unset PREFLIGHT_TIMINGS` / `unset TR_TIMINGS` sends a child to the default path, which is the outer run's file. Case 9b passes an explicit path, so it cannot see this.

Decide whether the `unset` lines stay (a child given no path should not inherit the parent's, but must not default onto it either).
    - **Decision on unset lines:** `unset TR_TIMINGS` in `scripts/test.sh` stays: child callers isolate themselves by setting their own `TR_WORK` (e.g. `build/tests-checked`, `build/tests-runner-output`, `$work/tests`), and defaulting `TIMINGS_FILE` to `${TR_TIMINGS:-$TR_WORK/timings.tsv}` correctly routes timings to the child's own work directory without inheriting an outer explicit `TR_TIMINGS`. `unset PREFLIGHT_TIMINGS PREFLIGHT_LOG` in `scripts/preflight.sh` stays so child processes do not inherit the parent's file targets, with `scripts/test_preflight.sh` exporting scratch paths for all test cases.
